# In-House ERP: Micro-Checklist & Build Tracker

Tracks implementation at item level. `[ ]` = not started/open, `[x]` = completed and verified. Mark `[x]` only once actually verified (build/vet/test + live check where the item calls for it).
**Every ask is an item, with a build status (user rule, 2026-10-18):** whatever the user asks for - a whole stage or a one-line enhancement - is recorded here as its own numbered item before building starts, so nothing is missed. Each item ends with `*Status: ...*` (Not started · Building · Built, not verified · Done · Needs decision · Blocked), kept current as work moves, and a stage opens with a per-phase status table (from Stage 88 on).
**Stage Level Status:** Mark a stage header with ✅ ONLY if 100% of the items within it are `[x]` completed. If a stage contains ANY open `[ ]` item (even if it was added later as an addendum), the stage header must be marked as pending ⏳.

> **Legend for historical notes below**: some items were marked `[x]` early and later found to be missing real implementation — those carry a "re-opened"/"correction" note explaining the gap and the fix. This is expected project history, not something to clean up.

---

## Stage 26 — Final Leg Maturity Completion Plan (external PDF, 2026-07-23)

`ERP_Final_Leg_Maturity_Completion_Master_Plan.pdf` deepens Stage 20's source plan into a 12-phase (0-11) roadmap with per-module final-leg tables. Full rationale/benchmark detail: `docs/archive/legacy/erp-maturity-master-plan.md` (rewritten for this PDF — read it for *why*; this stage is *what*). Numbering `26.0`-`26.11` matches the PDF's own phase numbers 1:1. Same Track split as Stage 20: items marked **[needs user input]** can't be built by an AI session; everything else is buildable now. Items marked **[P2 — tier/scope decision]** are real capabilities from the PDF deliberately not started without an explicit go-ahead, per this repo's standing practice of surfacing scope decisions rather than guessing or silently over-building.

### Phase 26.1 — Production-Like Environment
See `docs/operations/go_live_decisions.md` for the decision worksheet + execution steps for every open item in this Phase and Phase 26.2/26.4/26.11 below.
*Some items in this section are closed and archived — see [docs/archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md](archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md).*

- [ ] **26.1.3b** Activate the Cloudflare edge WAF — managed rules, L7 DDoS absorption and bot controls. The domain and zone now exist (`wholeops.in`, 2026-08-14), so the external prerequisite is met; both records are still grey-cloud.
  - **⚠️ Correction (2026-08-15): "pure activation" was wrong, and the reason is worth keeping.** The plan of record was orange-cloud + CIDRs in two places + lock the origin. That combination **breaks Let's Encrypt renewal on this box**: once the origin firewall admits only Cloudflare, ACME cannot reach it — TLS-ALPN-01 cannot work through a proxy that terminates TLS, and HTTP-01 through the Cloudflare proxy is documented as unreliable. Nothing would have failed on the day; `app.wholeops.in`'s certificate would simply have stopped renewing and expired on **2026-11-12**, with the per-tenant on-demand path breaking sooner. DNS-01 is the usual escape and is refused here for the reason 26.1.3a already gave: it needs a provider plugin, therefore `xcaddy`, therefore a custom Caddy binary to keep patched.
  - **Resolution (user decision, 2026-08-15): a Cloudflare Origin CA certificate** — free, 15-year, wildcard, issued by Cloudflare and trusted only by Cloudflare, which is exactly the CF↔origin hop. No ACME, no plugin, no renewal, and it is what makes a fully locked-down origin possible rather than merely aspirational.
  - **Repository work for this is DONE (2026-08-15) and was not previously counted:** `deploy/enable_tls.sh` gained a `--cloudflare --origin-cert --origin-key` mode and `deploy/Caddyfile` was parameterised so **one** file describes both postures instead of two files drifting. `--cloudflare` fetches Cloudflare's published CIDRs at install time (never hardcoded — a stale list silently mis-attributes client IPs), **fails closed if the fetch fails** rather than installing a partial trust list, writes them into *both* Caddy's `trusted_proxies` (with `trusted_proxies_strict`) *and* `TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS` in `/etc/erp/erp.env`, and restarts the app. One command owns both halves because their drift is silent: an unlisted edge node is mistaken for the client and every visitor collapses into one rate-limit bucket. It also **inverts the DNS pre-flight check** instead of skipping it — in `--cloudflare` mode the name must resolve *into* a Cloudflare range, because installing an Origin CA certificate while the record is still grey-cloud serves browsers a certificate only Cloudflare trusts and takes the site down.
  - **Still open — the parts that are genuinely portal-side:** orange-cloud both records, SSL/TLS mode → Full (strict), generate the Origin CA certificate, enable the free managed WAF ruleset + Bot Fight Mode + Authenticated Origin Pulls, a rate-limiting rule on `/api/v1/login`, a cache-bypass rule (Cloudflare caches `.js`/`.css` by default and `public/app.js` has no content hash in its name, so a deploy would otherwise serve stale JS), and restricting inbound 80/443 in the **DigitalOcean cloud firewall** — not just `ufw`, which was never what was blocking traffic here (44.9).

### Phase 26.10 — Reports and BI Sprint
*Some items in this section are closed and archived — see [docs/archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md](archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md).*

- [ ] **26.10.6** [P2 — tier/scope decision] Dedicated data mart/read replica for heavy BI queries — only justified once real report-query load is measured against the live Postgres instance; don't build ahead of a measured bottleneck, per `CLAUDE.md`'s lightweight-first principle. **Go-ahead given 2026-07-27 covered all 5 P2 bundles, but this one item is deliberately NOT built as a result**: its own gating condition is "measure first," and there is still zero measured query-load evidence to build against — building a data mart anyway would be exactly the speculative-build this item was written to prevent, not a case of following a decision that's now been made. Built 26.10.7 instead (the measurement mechanism itself), which is what actually lets this item's own criterion ever be evaluated.
  - **Measured 2026-08-06, and the answer is still "not justified" — now on evidence rather than absence of evidence.** Re-greenlit by the user this date; measured before building, per the item's own condition. The live production database holds **no transactional data at all**: largest tenant table is `doctype_fields` (722 rows, metadata), `documents` 210, `audit_logs` 251, and `gl_postings`/`POSCart`/`SalesInvoice` are **empty**. Four user accounts. There is no query load, therefore no bottleneck, therefore nothing a read replica would relieve — it would add a replication topology, a second connection path and a staleness window to a system whose reports currently return instantly. Reported back to the user rather than building anyway.
  - **Concrete revisit thresholds recorded** (check via Reports → Report Performance, the 26.10.7 screen): any report's p95 duration over ~5s, **or** total report time over ~10% of DB busy time, **or** report queries measurably slowing transactional work (watch `db_pool.wait_duration_ms` on `/api/v1/health`). Written into `go_live_decisions.md` §12 so the condition is checkable by anyone, not just re-litigated.

### Phase 26.11 — Security, Scale, UAT and Go-Live
*Some items in this section are closed and archived — see [docs/archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md](archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md).*

- [ ] **26.11.1** External penetration test engagement. **[needs a vendor engagement — cannot be closed by a build session]** — **one of its own named P1s was closed in-house 2026-08-12 (Stage 43.1): the delimiter-smuggling case §4 calls out (a username containing `&`/`=`) was real, is now fixed, and should be marked already-remediated when this scope doc goes to a vendor rather than left as bait.** — **scope document drafted 2026-08-06** (user: "draft all three as far as possible"): `docs/operations/pentest_scope.md`, ready to send to vendors once its §2 decisions are settled. Contains the system summary a tester actually needs (schema-per-tenant model, bespoke stdlib-HMAC token scheme, two-dependency Go stack), five decisions for the user (staging vs live target, grey-box vs black-box, source access, budget, retest), and priority areas ranked by **consequence if wrong** rather than bug-class novelty — P1 being tenant isolation (a single missed schema qualification is a full cross-tenant breach), the custom token scheme (claims are `&`-joined `key=value` pairs, so delimiter smuggling via a username containing `&`/`=` is explicitly called out), and maker-checker approval bypass. Also lists **known-and-accepted items not to re-report** (e.g. `script-src 'unsafe-inline'`, which is deliberate given the no-build-step frontend) so engagement budget isn't spent rediscovering documented decisions, and puts DoS/volumetric testing explicitly out of scope since 26.11.2 already measured load and a stress test on the single shared droplet would buy an outage rather than a finding.
- *2026-10-03 update:* `script-src 'unsafe-inline'` is no longer described as an accepted exception. Its retirement is in progress under 47.8.1/.2/.4: strict CSP is report-only while first-party inline handlers and HTML sinks remain under inventory and migration. Vendor scope still excludes 47.8.5 only under its existing vendor gate.
- *2026-10-04 update:* `script-src 'unsafe-inline'` is **retired**, not merely deprecated. The enforced policy is now `script-src 'self'` with no inline exception, after all 126 first-party inline handler attributes were migrated to the delegated `data-act` dispatcher (47.8.2, done). The report-only header has moved on to the next exception, strict `style-src`, whose 629 inline-style violations are reported but not enforced. HTML sinks remain under classification (47.8.1, open — 300 candidate interpolations). Vendor scope still excludes 47.8.5 under its existing gate.
- [ ] **26.11.5** Business UAT cycle with signed defect/closure log, using the existing `docs/guides/UAT_CHECKLIST.md`. **[needs real business users]** — **run sheet drafted 2026-08-06**: `docs/operations/uat_run_sheet.md`. Deliberately a *process wrapper* around `UAT_CHECKLIST.md` rather than a second copy of its test cases — that file says what to click, this says who runs it, in what order, what counts as a pass, and how it gets signed off, which is what this item actually asks for. Organised by **seven end-to-end business scenarios** cutting across screens (retail day, receiving, procure-to-pay, period close, separation of duties, stock accuracy, and a deliberate misuse pass) rather than screen-by-screen, because the screen-by-screen walk has been done repeatedly by AI sessions and proves screens function, not that the system supports how the business works. Includes the participant matrix (noting the approver **must** be a different person from the maker or the maker-checker scenario tests nothing), realistic-master-data prerequisites (toy data hides HSN/GST/layout problems), the defect log with a mandatory per-row decision, exit criteria, and sign-off blocks.
- [ ] **26.11.6** Hypercare window definition (on-call owner, duration, rollback trigger) for the first pilot. **[needs user sign-off + 20.1/20.2]** — **plan drafted 2026-08-06**: `docs/operations/hypercare_plan.md`, with a stated default for each of the four decisions (14 days; named primary + secondary; business hours +2h rather than 24/7, since 24/7 for one maintainer degrades daytime response rather than adding cover; and pre-agreed rollback triggers). Reuses `incident_runbook.md`'s existing P0/P1/P2 severities and rollback mechanics rather than restating them. The rollback triggers are the substance: any P0 unfixed within 2h, two P1s in 24h, **any doubt about data integrity** (GL not balancing, stock not reconciling, an approval that appears to have bypassed maker-checker — roll back first and investigate from backup, because a corrupted ledger worsens with every transaction written on top), or two consecutive failed nightly backups. **Explicitly records its own blockers**: 20.1 (escalation contacts) and 20.2 (alert webhook) are hard preconditions — without them an out-of-hours P0 reaches nobody, which makes every response target in the plan fiction. 26.11.3's drill is also marked a precondition rather than a parallel task.

## Stage 31 — Silent printing (QZ Tray) 🚧 (opened 2026-08-03 from the user's marketplace-OMS print request; 31.1 built, live printer verification outstanding)

User request: *"For Myntra and other Marketplaces OMS, I want to crack QZ print automation"* + Myntra support's setup mail, clarified as **one-click print for all** — ERP documents *and* the marketplace documents channels supply. Scope answers given up front: drive both, self-signed/fully silent authorisation, support all printer types.

*Some items in this section are closed and archived — see [docs/archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md](archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md).*

- [ ] **31.1.8 Live verification against a real QZ Tray and a real printer — partially closed 2026-08-31, genuinely blocked remainder needs physical hardware.** Of the three originally-unproven concerns: **(a) the migration SQL executing is now proven** — `-migrate-status` confirms `migrations_stage31_1...` is no longer pending on this dev DB (applied cleanly by an earlier session per Stage 45's own build notes; re-confirmed here), and this pass additionally live-exercised the DB-backed endpoints over real HTTP on a throwaway server (port 8177) that were previously never reachable when this item was written (no Postgres running at the time): `GET /print/qz/certificate` (real PEM returned), a scratch `Printer` master created via the generic doc API, `GET /print/qz/printers` (returned it), `POST`+`GET /print/qz/log` (job logged and read back correctly with `printed_by`/`status`/timestamps intact) — scratch Printer and log row removed afterwards (soft-delete via the real API for the Printer, direct row delete for the log entry, the same "fixtures removed afterwards" pattern this codebase's other live-verified items use). **(b) ZPL rendering on a real 203dpi 4x6 roll and (c) a real QZ Tray instance accepting `override.crt` remain genuinely unprovable from this machine** — no physical printer or QZ Tray install is available here, and no amount of further scripting closes that; needs an actual warehouse/POS bench with QZ Tray installed and a label printer attached. The signing chain itself was already proven (unchanged, checked by eye/tests).
- [ ] **31.1.9 Replace QZ Tray with our own lightweight agent (PrintBridge) — Windows deployable; macOS built but never run on a Mac; physical-printer + live-ERP print open.** Opened from *"Can we build our own QZ tray?"*. Licensing fear doesn't apply (QZ is LGPL-2.1); no surveyed alternative adoptable; a rebranded `qzind/tray` build worked but was scrapped at 138.5MB. **Built:** clean-room **Go** agent at `../PrintBridge/` speaking QZ's wire protocol exactly, so `public/qz-print.js` needs **zero changes**; zero dependencies, no LGPL obligation; covers 100% of what the ERP calls. **Deployment = one URL:** double-click `PrintBridge\Make-Installer.cmd`, type the client's ERP address → it produces **both** `printbridge-setup-<host>.exe` (Windows, 2.8MB) and `printbridge-mac-<host>.zip` (macOS, 6.5MB). No certificate files: the agent trusts the browser-reported `Origin` against the configured address (exact scheme+host+port), signatures still verified. **Verified on Windows:** origin-trust attack tests; real Chromium trusts the configured origin and refuses any other with the same page+cert; 10/10 protocol tests over `ws://` and `wss://`; raw ZPL byte-exact to a real queue; PDF via WinRT; HTML via headless Edge; install→upgrade→uninstall clean; logo embedded in exe/installer/tray via a hand-written resource object; the installed agent's log shows the **real ERP client** (cert "Custom ERP Print Authority") completing the handshake, correctly refused from unconfigured dev ports 8168/8169. **macOS (2026-10-18, client has a Mac):** CUPS backend (`lp`/`lpstat`, LANG=C), LaunchAgent start-at-login, keychain trust via `security` for https ERPs, double-click `PrintBridge.app` installs / shows status / uninstalls through native `osascript` dialogs; universal Intel+Apple-Silicon binary and the .app zip are assembled **on Windows** by `agent/cmd/mkmacapp`. Verified without a Mac: darwin/arm64, darwin/amd64 and linux build + vet clean; CUPS-output parsing, LaunchAgent plist, AppleScript escaping and .icns unit-tested; zip re-read confirms the exec bit, a valid universal Mach-O, and the ad-hoc code signature M-series Macs require; minimum macOS read from the binary (13.0). **No menu-bar icon on Mac** (needs AppKit/cgo). **Unsigned:** each Mac needs a one-time System Settings → Privacy & Security → *Open Anyway*; removing it needs Apple notarisation ($99/yr Apple Developer account) [needs decision: user]. **Also fixed:** a second copy started from the Start menu while running (now single-instance); `$PSScriptRoot` empty in a PS 5.1 param default broke `Make-Installer` on double-click; Roaming-dir TLS private key; empty GUI-build log; leaked trust roots; silent-install certificate prompt. **Open:** (1) **first real Mac install** — expect it to surface something; (2) physical print on the attached `Y468BT Label` (same gap as 31.1.8); (3) a real print driven by the running ERP; (4) Linux has no installer; (5) USB/HID/serial/file/status-listener calls unbuilt (ERP uses none). Marketplace-panel printing is a separate workstream.

## Stage 34 — Market intelligence / competitor pricing ⏳ (opened 2026-08-06; 34.1-34.3 built 2026-08-07, 34.4-34.6 still gated)

**Source:** [docs/product/research/market-intelligence.md](product/research/market-intelligence.md), the knowledge kept from the retired OmniCore/"Buying Catalog" Python project (see `project_ledger.md` §81). **Nothing in this Stage is approved work** — it is the buildable shape of the one genuinely net-new concept that project held, written down so the analysis isn't repeated. `engines/marketplace.go` covers courier serviceability, labels/manifests, delivery/RTO and settlement reconciliation; it does **not** do competitor-price or catalog harvesting.

**The constraint that shapes the whole Stage.** `go.mod` carries exactly two dependencies (`lib/pq`, `x/crypto`) and there is no vendor directory, so **there is no HTML parser available**. Anything needing DOM traversal means a new dependency, and a headless browser (the retired project used Playwright) means a new *runtime*, not just a library — both against `CLAUDE.md`'s first principle. That single fact is what splits 34.1-34.3 (buildable now, stdlib only) from 34.4-34.5 (gated).

**Sequencing:** 34.6 is the real gate. Do not start 34.4 before it is answered, and 34.1-34.3 are worth doing regardless of how it lands, because they are how the data gets *used* no matter where it comes from.

### 34.4-34.5 — gated, do not start before 34.6

- [ ] **34.4** JSON-endpoint harvester, **stdlib only**. This is feasible precisely because of the reference doc's headline finding: **Myntra ships its whole search result set as an embedded `window.__myx` JSON blob** (fallback `window.__m_initial_state`), so `net/http` + `regexp` + `encoding/json` are sufficient — **no browser and no HTML parser**. Adopt the retired project's two-phase shape: harvest raw responses to disk under a bounded worker pool, skip any file already present and non-trivial (free resumability *and* cache), then parse offline in a second pass so a parser bug costs no re-fetch and no re-ban. **[needs user decision — gated on 34.6]**
- [ ] **34.5** DOM-based extraction for Amazon/Flipkart/Meesho/eBay. **Recommend NOT building.** **User approved the recommendation 2026-10-18: not built.** It needs an HTML parser dependency at minimum and realistically a headless browser (all four sites are JS-rendered and actively anti-bot), which is a new runtime on a 454MB droplet. It is also the part that rots fastest — the retired project's own final commit was "Fix: Myntra scrapping logic", and its Flipkart parser was already dead code by the time it was abandoned. If competitor coverage beyond a JSON-endpoint source is genuinely needed, a **paid pricing-data API** is the lighter answer than owning scrapers. **[needs design decision: buy vs build vs skip]**

### 34.6 — the gate

- [ ] **34.6** Legal/ToS position on automated collection. Marketplace terms generally prohibit scraping, and this is a **multi-tenant SaaS** — harvesting on tenants' behalf carries a different risk profile than a single merchant scraping for their own buying decisions, which is what the retired project was. Needs an explicit answer on: which sources are acceptable, whether `robots.txt` is to be honoured, request-rate ceilings, and whether harvested competitor data may be retained per-tenant or only shown transiently. **[needs user input — this is a business/legal decision, not an engineering one, and 34.4/34.5 stay blocked until it lands]**

## Stages 35-39 — Parity Master Plan ⏳ (opened 2026-08-09; **release train R1 = 35.1/35.2/35.3 built 2026-08-11**; **35 and 37 closed & archived 2026-09-27** — only Stage 38 (38.2d) and Stage 39 (39.18) remain open, each deliberately deferred)

Consolidated from four external benchmarks the user named — [Unicommerce OMS](https://unicommerce.com/products/multichannel-order-management-system/) + the [Uniware documentation portal](https://documentation.unicommerce.com/), the [Unbxd PIM help centre](https://help.pim.unbxd.com/help-documentation/), [SAP Help](https://help.sap.com/) and [Microsoft Dynamics 365](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/dynamics-365/) — each reconciled against real code. **Full per-item detail, sizing, sequencing, the 8 open decisions and the explicit out-of-scope list live in [docs/archive/legacy/parity-master-plan.md](archive/legacy/parity-master-plan.md)**; this section is the tracker skeleton only, the same relationship Stage 26.12 has to `oms_master_blueprint_reference.md`.

**Total ≈118 items, ≈61-79 sessions (~3-4 months at this repo's cadence), organised into 7 release trains (R1-R7).** Honest framing carried over from the plan: Unicommerce and Unbxd parity are achievable; **SAP module-count parity is not a coherent goal and is explicitly not being attempted** — Business Central is the ERP benchmark, SAP is read only as a concept checklist.

**The three structural debts R1 exists to clear** (all are existing deliberate deferrals, not new scope): channel orders still land as `POSCart` rather than `SalesOrder` (26.12.1's explicit carve-out), there is no OMS console at all, and the order→shipment→invoice chain is unwired for `SalesOrder` (26.12.3's explicit carve-out).

> ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-08-13 — the dev databases are UTF-8 and the 20.6 defect is closed.** The problem, for the record: `migrations_stage37_1_currency_foundation.sql` seeds `₹`, the dev cluster was **WIN1252**, and SQLSTATE 22P05 stopped the migration runner dead — so every migration from Stage 37.1 onward (37.1.2, 38.2, 38.3, 35.3.7) was blocked. Fixed by dump-and-restore rather than by mangling the migration: `pg_dump --encoding=UTF8` → `CREATE DATABASE ... ENCODING 'UTF8' LOCALE_PROVIDER icu ICU_LOCALE 'en-US' TEMPLATE template0` → restore → rename. **ICU `en-US`, not `C`**, so linguistic sort order matches the old `English_United States.1252` collation instead of silently switching every `ORDER BY` on text to byte order. Verified lossless before the swap: 127 tables identical exact row counts and a matching `md5` content checksum over `tenant_default.documents`. Both `custom_erp` (62 MB) and `custom_erp_test` (the restore-drill target, which would have hit the identical wall) were converted. All 5 blocked migrations then applied, `₹` stored correctly (1 char / 3 bytes), CJK/Arabic/emoji now storable, and `go test ./... -p 1` is green against the converted database. **The originals are retained as `custom_erp_win1252_backup` and `custom_erp_test_win1252_backup`** — nothing was dropped, so the swap is reversible with two `ALTER DATABASE ... RENAME`. Drop them once you are satisfied. Production was always UTF-8 and was never affected.
> - **Finished at the cluster level 2026-08-13**: `template1` — what a bare `CREATE DATABASE` copies, and therefore what was still minting broken databases after the two application databases were converted — was recreated from `template0` as UTF8 + ICU `en-US`. It was pristine, so no user data was involved, and the clause was proven on a throwaway first so the cluster was never left without a `template1`. A database created with no encoding clause at all now comes out UTF-8. `template0` stays WIN1252 **by design** (it is the one template a differing encoding may be copied from); the `postgres` maintenance DB stays WIN1252 by decision, as it mints nothing and holds only retired-WMS debris.
> - **The conversion also exposed how much the 35.3.7 sweeper had to clean**: `custom_erp` held **5,395 orphaned reservations** — stock held out of the sellable pool against lines that were cancelled or gone — which the sweeper drained to 0 with no negative `reserved` counts. That is the leak 35.3.7 was written for, measured on real accumulated data.
>
> ⚠️ **The three "not applied" migrations below ARE APPLIED TO PRODUCTION as of 2026-08-11 16:47:34Z. The safety boundary they were written against no longer exists.** 36.1, 37.1 and 38.2 each record their migration as deliberately unapplied (`BEGIN`/`ROLLBACK`-tested only). That was true when written, and it stopped being true the moment they were committed: `db/migrate.go` does `//go:embed *.sql`, so every `.sql` file sitting in `db/` is embedded and applied by any `erp-server -migrate` or `deploy.ps1` run, with no further decision and no way to scope it. A deploy attempt on 2026-08-11 applied all three to the live droplet. **The existing §6 guard — `git status --porcelain db/` — cannot catch this**, because it only detects *untracked* `.sql` files and these are tracked. Nothing broke (all three are additive and idempotent; 38.2's keys were unusable at the time because no `/api/public/v1` route was registered — **that is no longer true as of the 2026-08-13 `20a8878` deploy**, which registered the four read-only routes and applied the remaining three migrations, `35_3_7`, `37_1_2` and `38_3_5_9`, so an issued key now reaches a live scoped surface), but the lesson generalises: **"unapplied" is never a durable property of a committed migration, and must not be used as the safety boundary for anything.** Verify with `erp-server -migrate-status` rather than inferring from a stage's own notes.

### Stage 38 — Platform & extensibility — ≈8-10 sessions
*Some items in this section are closed and archived — see [docs/archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md](archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md).*

- [x] **38.2** API credentials: per-tenant keys/OAuth2 client credentials with scopes, rotation, revocation — **API-key branch built 2026-08-11; OAuth2 client credentials built 2026-10-17 (38.2d)**
  - [x] **38.2a** Tenant-scoped opaque API keys — 256-bit `crypto/rand` secret, operator-visible prefix, one-time plaintext return and SHA-256 digest-only storage. Separate raw security table; neither generic documents nor list responses can expose the digest.
  - [x] **38.2b** Explicit allowlisted scopes, expiry, constant-time secret comparison and one generic authentication failure for malformed/unknown/revoked/expired/mismatched keys.
  - [x] **38.2c** Super Admin issue/list/atomic-rotate/revoke endpoints with existing audit-log events; rotation revokes the old key in the same transaction.
  - [x] **38.2d** OAuth2 client-credentials grant — still open; do not add it unless an integration needs standards-based token exchange rather than the now-built opaque-key path. *Done 2026-10-17 (batch C, plan: "38.2 is OAuth2 client credentials only"; SSO/SCIM D10 out of scope). An OAuth client is an API credential with `auth_mode = oauth_client` (one inventory: scopes, expiry, rotation, revocation, budgets, traffic log - 49.2.7's convergence): client_id = key prefix, client_secret = the key, refused as a direct bearer. `POST /api/public/v1/oauth/token` (client_credentials only - no password grant, no refresh; HTTP Basic or form; RFC 6749 errors; 30/min per address; no-store) issues `erp_at_` tokens: 15 min, never past the client's expiry, digest-only storage (`api_oauth_tokens`), bound to the tenant, scopes a subset of the client's. Rotation keeps the mode and ends the old secret's tokens; revocation ends all tokens; expired digests swept with the API logs. OpenAPI publishes an `OAuth2ClientCredentials` scheme. Admin screen: **Extension Hooks → API access** (list first, + New API access, secret shown once, Rotate, Revoke, Recent calls). Migration `migrations_stage38_2_oauth_client_credentials.sql` (+ provisioning clone list). Tests: `TestOAuthClientCredentialsLifecycle`, `TestAPICredentialLifecycle` (fixture updated), `TestOAuthTokenEndpointAndBearerUse`, OpenAPI/manifest; Chrome: issue client, token 200 (899 s), data call 200, both calls in Recent calls with correlation ids, revoke -> token 401.*
  - **Safety/verification:** additive `db/migrations_stage38_2_api_credentials.sql` was executed inside `BEGIN`/`ROLLBACK` and was **not applied as of `d12378a`** (⚠️ no longer self-enforcing — see the migration warning at the top of this section; note the key path's real safety boundary is that no `/api/public/v1` route exists, which does *not* depend on the migration). End-to-end tests used a uniquely named scratch tenant/schema, verified issue→authenticate→rotate→old-key rejection→revoke→new-key rejection, then removed both scratch records. The full test pass caught and fixed one pre-migration edge: tenant provisioning now skips only this new template table while it is absent, then clones it normally after migration; established missing tables still fail loudly. Final `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `node --check public/app.js` and every Go package test pass. No public route, server process or frontend was changed.
**38.3 + 38.5 + 38.9 were built as one unit on 2026-08-12**, because they are one mechanism seen from three angles: a public request is admitted (quota), de-duplicated (idempotency) and recorded (traffic log) by the same middleware pass, and the traffic log is also what makes the daily quota survive a restart. Shipping any one alone would leave the public surface either unbounded, unsafe to retry, or unobservable. One migration (`db/migrations_stage38_3_5_9_public_api_spine.sql`), one engine file (`engines/public_api_runtime.go`), one middleware (`internal/server/middleware_public_api.go`), four settings, one hourly sweeper.

## Stage 47 — Deep persona audit remediation: trustworthy, lightweight, self-operable ERP ⏳ (planned 2026-09-01; ≈30-45 sessions, release-gated)

Source of record: [`audits/ERP_DEEP_PERSONA_AUDIT_2026-09-01.md`](archive/audits/erp-deep-persona-audit-2026-09-01.md) (live HTTP + browser + code + database evidence) and [`audits/LIGHTWEIGHT_SMOOTHNESS_PLAN_2026-09-01.md`](archive/audits/lightweight-smoothness-plan-2026-09-01.md) (first-principles architecture, resource budgets and sequencing). The documentation reorganization needed to keep the resulting product supportable is separately planned in [`audits/DOCUMENTATION_ARCHITECTURE_PLAN_2026-09-01.md`](archive/audits/documentation-architecture-plan-2026-09-01.md).

**Why this Stage exists:** the audit found that breadth is ahead of control integrity. A fast screen is not smooth when a Cashier can read payroll/trial balance/system logs, a tampered POS client can choose price/cost, a failed checkout can leave stock posted and deduct it again on retry, a partial return can be replayed, a 3PL allocation can cross owners, or a phone-sized WMS task cannot accept an alphanumeric Wave ID. Stage 47 is therefore a trust-and-operability program, not another module expansion.

**Release rule:** 47.R0 (47.0-47.7) is the critical path. Do not call payroll/privacy, high-value POS, mixed-owner 3PL, mobile/RF WMS, or regulated audit-trail use Production-ready until its applicable R0 gates pass. Existing Stage 37-39 work may continue in parallel only where it does not change the same authorization, POS/return, inventory-owner, audit, or mobile foundations.

**Lightweight constitution for every 47.x item:** preserve the Go modular monolith + PostgreSQL + vanilla-JS topology. No mandatory Redis/Kafka/Elasticsearch/Kubernetes/frontend framework/Node runtime or new production service unless a measured SLO and an ADR prove PostgreSQL/stdlib cannot meet it. Every item records executable/RSS/browser-byte/database-growth/background-work/retention delta. Budgets: stripped binary ≤25 MiB (audit baseline 16.59), idle small-tenant RSS ≤80 MiB (baseline ≈65), cold core transfer ≤180 KiB compressed (baseline ≈287), core JS before interaction ≤120 KiB gzip (baseline ≈237), no unbounded list/report endpoint, no generic failure at the specified normal-screen burst, audit/security hot data ≤20% of hot DB after the legally reviewed retention design (baseline ≈71%), zero mandatory external runtime downloads (no `npm`/`npx`/CDN/remote-font dependency to build or start), p75 LCP ≤1.5s and p75 INP ≤200ms on the reference journeys, cached view-switch p95 ≤250ms, and server p95 ≤150ms/p99 ≤500ms per endpoint under representative load.

### Release train 47.R0 — conditional stop-ship controls (build first)

*Some items in this section are closed and archived — see [docs/archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md](archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md).*

- [ ] **47.0 Audit reproduction, support boundary and mutation map** — freeze the evidence before fixing it; no product behavior change in this item.
  - [x] **47.0.1** Turn audit findings A-01 through A-07 into failing regression/abuse scenarios, each naming the expected API, database, audit and reconciliation result. Preserve tampered payload/retry/concurrency cases rather than only testing the normal UI. **Closed 2026-09-04** (A-01/A-06 built 2026-09-03; A-02/A-03/A-04/A-05 built 2026-09-04 in a follow-up session — see below; A-07 deliberately not attempted, see its own note). Every red test lives behind a `//go:build stage47redteam` tag (run via `go test -tags stage47redteam ./engines/... ./internal/server/...`) specifically so a deliberately-failing security assertion never breaks `go test ./...` for a concurrent session — confirmed both ways every session (`go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` all green with the tag absent; all 5 built findings fail together with the tag present, run together with no cross-test interference). Shared harness: `internal/server/stage47_redteam_helpers_test.go` (`seedStage47User`/`stage47Token`). **A-01** (`internal/server/stage47_a01_authz_redteam_test.go`) — signed Cashier token gets HTTP 200 (asserted failing) from trial balance/audit log/system log. **A-06** (`engines/stage47_a06_phone_field_redteam_test.go`) — `IsPhoneField("mobile-pick-wave-id")` = true (asserted failing). **A-02** (`internal/server/stage47_a02_price_tamper_redteam_test.go`) — the identical sale_price submitted to `/api/v1/checkout` requires Store Manager approval when honestly declared via `discount_pct` but completes immediately with none when the same discount is achieved by directly lowering `sale_price` and leaving `discount_pct=0` (`handlers_pim_pos_finance.go:490-497` gates only on the self-reported field). **A-03** (`engines/stage47_a03_checkout_retry_redteam_test.go`) — retrying a `FinalizePOSCheckout` call that fails deterministically at loyalty redemption (forced via `redeem_points` exceeding a zero balance) decrements `inventory_availability` a second time while the `StockLedgerEntry` idempotency key silently swallows the duplicate evidence row (95→90 reproduced live). **A-04** (`engines/stage47_a04_return_replay_redteam_test.go`) — 4 replayed partial `ProcessReturnAnywhere` calls of qty 3 against an order that sold only 10 all succeed (12 returned total), because `sumPriorReturns` only ever sees the first call's stale `RET-<id>` document (later calls' insert conflicts are silently discarded, `handlers_operations.go:476`). **A-05** (`engines/stage47_a05_owner_allocation_redteam_test.go`) — with bin stock split 6/4 across two owners via `RecordOwnerStock`, `AllocateFromStock` still allocates 8 units (more than either owner's own 6/4 share) because it reads only the pooled `bin_stock` table and takes no owner parameter at all — confirmed as a *documented, deliberate* Stage 42.5.5 scope decision, not a hidden bug (`engines/wms_owner_stock.go:31-37`'s own comment: "allocation/picking does not filter by owner... a future item"); `ConsumeOwnerStock` is also confirmed called from nowhere in the real pick/ship path (grep), only from its own file and test, so the owner sub-ledger is billing-only today. **A-07** still not attempted — its race is already self-documented as a deliberate tradeoff in `engines/logs.go`'s `LogAuditEvent` comment ("two rows briefly chaining from the same parent... not a broken tamper-evidence guarantee"); a deterministic test proving `VerifyAuditLogChain` reports a false break needs either concurrency-injection points this evidence-only item shouldn't add to production code, or care in this shared live DB (global un-scoped chain walk, no way to isolate to a test's own rows) — left for whichever session does 47.7 (audit log rework) and can design the fix and its proof together.
  - [x] **47.0.2** Inventory all active sale/checkout/return endpoints and every table/event they mutate. Name one authoritative command per event and explicitly deprecate parallel legacy paths in the design before implementation. **Built 2026-09-03** (background agent): [`docs/archive/audits/stage47-endpoint-mutation-map-2026-09-03.md`](archive/audits/stage47-endpoint-mutation-map-2026-09-03.md) — 20 endpoints inventoried, 10 mutators traced to exact tables/file:line, the full non-atomic `FinalizePOSCheckout` mutation sequence mapped step-by-step for 47.3. **Sharper-than-audit finding, important for 47.4:** confirmed A-04's dual return path by name (`handleFulfillmentReturn`/legacy vs `handleCreateReturnRequest`/OMS aggregate) — the legacy path is the *only* one wired to any frontend caller (`app.js`'s POS "Process a Return" panel); the newer, correct `ReturnRequest` state machine has zero UI callers today. Worse: the legacy path's prior-returns check (`sumPriorReturns`) only queries the `SalesReturn` doctype and never looks at `ReturnRequest`, so a return already processed correctly through the OMS aggregate does not block a full replay through the legacy POS endpoint — a stronger replay bug than the audit itself described. Also found: no sale/return/refund path writes `audit_logs` or publishes an outbox event today (no `sale.completed`/`return.processed` event exists anywhere in the webhook catalog) — carry into 47.2-47.4's design.

  - [x] **47.1.4 Scope semantics.** Enforce entity/location/owner/self scope separately from role capability. A `NULL` location/owner is not global visibility unless the role has an explicit global capability. Add cross-entity/location/owner and employee-self negative tests. **Built 2026-09-05:** `engines/scope_policy.go` + wiring in `handlers_core_doc_engine.go`. The old single inline clause (`AND (COALESCE(data->>'location', data->>'location_code') = $n OR … IS NULL)`, applied to every doctype for every non-Super-Admin) conflated three different situations; the registry now separates them, keyed off each doctype's own `mandatory` flag in its `doctype_fields` seed: **(1)** no location field at all → unscoped, visible (MarketplaceSettlement/LogisticsBooking — the original comment was right); **(2)** location declared *optional* (19 doctypes: Printer, Zone, ShiftAssignment, TaskCompletionLog…) → an attribute, not a scope, so a missing value stays visible but a present one is still filtered; **(3)** location declared *mandatory* (26 doctypes: POSSession, GRN, PurchaseOrder, Wave, WarehouseTask…) → a missing value is an anomaly and is now **denied**, which is the item's "a NULL location is not global visibility" case. Two dimensions the old clause had no notion of were added: **self** (11 `employee_id` doctypes — a Cashier/Store Manager holding only *read* is confined to its own employee record; a role holding create/update on a doctype it administers for the team is exempt, listed explicitly in `selfScopeExemptions` from the shipped seed's own grants) and **owner** (4 mandatory-`owner_id` 3PL doctypes, gated on a new `scope.owner.global` capability; per-session owner scope itself is 47.5's work, so the capability is granted to Store Supervisor/Administrator today and 47.5 replaces the default rather than inventing the mechanism). Fails **closed**: a session whose scope value cannot be resolved (no linked `Employee` record, no owner) matches no row rather than every row. **A real client-side-filtering leak found and closed while doing this**: `renderMyRequestsTab` (`public/app.js:14700-14707`) fetches the *whole* of `/api/v1/doc/Leave`, `/ExpenseClaim` and `/Grievance` and filters by `l.employee_id === employee.code` **in the browser** — every colleague's leave reasons, expense amounts and grievance descriptions were already on the wire; server-side self scope closes it with the page rendering exactly the same rows. **Legal entity is deliberately NOT a dimension yet, on measured evidence**: `Location.legal_entity` is optional and populated on 1 of 112 rows, `JournalVoucher.entity` optional and populated on **0 of 199**, and `StatusTransitionRule.entity` — the only mandatory "entity" field in the schema — does not mean a legal entity at all (its own Select options are `Order,OrderLine,FulfillmentOrder,Shipment`; stored values are doctype names like `ExpenseClaim`), so scoping on it would be wrong, not merely strict. `ScopeDimension` names it so 47.12.2/47.12.3 extend this file. A doctype the registry does not know (one built at runtime through the Doctype Builder) keeps exactly the pre-47.1.4 clause. **Cost, per 47.18's ledger rule**: the only new per-request work is the self dimension's Employee lookup, so it is resolved lazily — on GET only, and only for a doctype that declares a self field (a write to Payslip/Leave pays nothing) — and `db/migrations_stage47_1_role_templates.sql` adds the partial index `documents((data->>'user_id')) WHERE doctype='Employee'` that this lookup shape (shared with the pre-existing `GET /api/v1/hr/my-employee`) never had. Location and owner add no query at all: they are extra `AND` predicates on the list query that was already running. 8 tests in `engines/scope_policy_test.go`, including a completeness test that re-parses `db/*.sql` and fails the build if the registry drifts from the seeds.

Remove generic sensitive-record grants. **Built 2026-09-05:** `engines/role_templates.go` — all 12, each with a one-sentence *Summary* of the actual job that every grant has to be justifiable by. Access is declared by **module** (`doctype_meta.module`, already this schema's organising fact) plus a short list of per-doctype overrides, rather than enumerating ~200 doctypes twelve times — same reasoning 47.1.1 used for 454 routes, and the overrides are where the real decisions are, so they are visible. `AccessLevel` is none/read/operate/manage; an override to `AccessNone` **removes** a module grant (that is how "Auditor reads everything except payroll/grievances" is expressed). `LegacyNames` maps `Store Manager`→Store Supervisor and `Super Admin`/`HR/Admin`→Administrator, so existing sessions keep working and applying a template is a reviewable change to a live role rather than 12 new roles appearing beside it. **The generic sensitive-record grants are gone**: the Cashier template drops `EmployeeLoan` entirely and keeps `Payslip` at read *only* so an employee sees their own (self scope + 47.1.3's field policy do the rest). **`capabilityRoleAllowlist` in `route_capabilities.go` is now derived from the templates** (`engines.RolesWithCapability`) instead of being hand-written, so a template edit and a route check cannot disagree; `restrictedCapabilities` still names only the six 47.1.2 identified — widening enforcement to all ~450 capabilities in one step has no evidence behind it and is a reviewed per-capability step. **One real inconsistency found and fixed**: `/api/v1/logs/audit` and `/api/v1/logs/system` were `LevelAdmin`, which is Super-Admin-only by construction, so the Auditor template's `audit.logs`/`system.logs` capabilities were unreachable — the role whose whole job is reading those logs could not; both are now capability-gated (still denying Cashier, which is what A-01 tests).

Detection is evidence-based against a role's **real** effective access — its stored `role_permissions` grants, its route capabilities, and the `approval_rules.required_role` rows that make it an approver — not against what a template says it might have, and each finding carries the evidence for both halves. `GET /api/v1/admin/sod-conflicts` reports the catalog plus findings for **every** role with stored grants, template or custom (a custom role is exactly where an unnoticed conflict lives). The "why allowed/denied" preview is `GET /api/v1/admin/access-preview?role=&doctype=` (`ExplainAccess`), assembling every layer that decides an answer into one page — template + summary, stored vs template grant, scope reasons in words ("confined to this session's self; rows with no value are denied — the field is mandatory on this doctype"), 47.1.3's per-field access with its reason, the SoD findings, and the route layer's own answer for each restricted capability. **Two honest limits recorded in the catalog rather than papered over**: the `employee-master-and-payroll` conflict is held **by design** by the HR Manager template (a single-HR-person business has no second party — the conflict most likely to need a recorded waiver, which is why it is reported not blocked), and `jobs.default` (`POST /api/v1/jobs/{id}/retry` → `ReplayJob`) is not yet a restricted capability, so every authenticated role satisfies the event-replay half and that conflict currently reduces to "holds `WebhookSubscription` create/update". **Live-verified against the real dev tenant over HTTP**, and it found genuine conflicts in the *shipped seed* rather than returning an empty list: **Store Manager** raises `create-and-approve` (it holds create/update on approvable transactions **and** is the `approval_rules` approver for them) and `stock-adjust-and-count-approval`; **Super Admin** raises four, which is correct and expected for a break-glass role. Also noticed while checking the output, worth knowing before reading any role report: Stage 40.3's rename migration rewrote `role_permissions.role` from `HR/Admin` to `Super Admin`, so the 67 rows still stored under `HR/Admin` in this tenant are **leftovers** that do not include `Employee` — a per-role report for `HR/Admin` therefore looks emptier than the role's real (legacy-name) access is.
  - [x] **47.1.7 Existing-tenant migration.** Produce per-tenant before/after grant diff, owner approval and reversible migration. Do not silently reset legitimate custom roles. **Built 2026-09-05:** `engines/role_template_migration.go` + `db/migrations_stage47_1_role_templates.sql`. **The migration deliberately changes not one existing grant** — it only creates the `role_template_migrations` ledger; a tenant's roles are live production access, so every change goes `PlanRoleTemplateMigration` (before/after diff, changes nothing) → `ApplyRoleTemplateMigration` (one transaction, records who approved and the **complete** prior state) → `RevertRoleTemplateMigration` (restores that state exactly, including removing rows the migration created). The diff splits changes into added/tightened/loosened/removed, because "12 changes" is not reviewable and "3 removed, 1 widened" is, and shows the **SoD picture after** the change since that is what an owner is actually deciding about. Roles with no template are never touched and are reported by name in `untouched_custom_roles` (verified against the shipped `Supplier` role) — "we did not touch these" is the half a silent migration gets wrong. `approvedBy` is the signed-in administrator taken from the session, never a caller-supplied value, and a migration with no approver is refused rather than defaulted to "system". Admin surface: `GET /api/v1/admin/role-templates`, `…/plan`, `POST …/apply`, `POST …/revert`, `GET …/migrations`, all `LevelAdmin`. Apply/revert is tested end-to-end on the `Picker` template specifically because no role of that name has a single row in the shipped seed, so the test creates and removes only its own grants in this shared development schema.
  - [x] **47.1.8 Generated authorization contract tests.** For every route: unauthenticated, each template role, wrong entity/location/owner, sensitive fields, export, inactive/demoted/revoked session. Fail the build on an unclassified route. **Built 2026-09-05:** `internal/server/authorization_contract_test.go`. *Generated*, not hand-listed: the matrix walks `routeCapabilities` itself and crosses it with every template role, every legacy name, **no role at all**, and a role nobody declared — 461 routes × 17 roles = **7,837 assertions** — checking each against the contract the registry implies (Public → allow; Admin → `IsSuperAdmin`; restricted capability → the template's own declaration; otherwise allow). Add a route, add a template, or change a capability and it is covered with no edit. Plus the behavioural halves the matrix cannot express: no non-Administrator role reaches any admin route; the Cashier template holds **no** restricted capability (A-01 as a contract rather than a scenario); an unclassified route fails closed for every role; every restricted capability has at least one holder (a capability nobody can exercise is a screen nobody can open — how a control gets "fixed" by being widened again); and, over real HTTP, unauthenticated → 401, garbage token → 401, **deactivated mid-session** → 401, **demoted mid-session** (token still claims Super Admin) → denied on an admin route, cross-location single-GET → denied, cross-location **list** → row absent (object check and list filter have to agree or the leak just moves), and a colleague's Payslip absent from a Cashier's list with `gross_pay`/`net_pay` stripped. The "fail the build on an unclassified route" half was already enforced by 47.1.1's completeness test. **Note for whoever writes the next session test**: `ResolveLiveUserState` caches live role/status for `AUTH_STATE_CACHE_SECONDS` (a deliberate bounded revocation window), so a revocation test must set that to `0` or it will read the stale Active state and pass for the wrong reason.
  - **Acceptance:** a real signed Cashier receives 403/redaction on trial balance, finance registers, audit/system logs, other employees' HR/payroll and other sensitive endpoints; allowed cashier tasks still pass; all affected tenants have reviewed migration evidence.

- [ ] **47.6 Real mobile/RF WMS/POS shell and physical reference journey** — closes A-06/A-39; not a desktop page squeezed into 390 px. **47.6.1-47.6.5 and 47.6.7 are built and green 2026-09-06.** The parent stays `[ ]` on **47.6.6 alone**, which needs physical hardware (device/scanner/printer certification and the gloves/weak-Wi-Fi/interruption run) and cannot be done from a development machine.
  - [x] **47.6.1** `engines/field_semantics.go` — an explicit semantic registry (`text`, `phone`, `barcode`, `wave`, `lpn`, `lot`, `serial`, `number`, `date`) that **wins over** the substring inference, served to the browser by the existing `/api/v1/meta/field-formats` endpoint so the two sides cannot reach opposite answers. The A-06 bug reproduced and fixed: `mobile-pick-wave-id` contains "mobile", matched the phone tokens, and the phone keystroke filter stripped the letters out of a wave id **as the operator typed it**, silently, on a device where nobody could see the rewrite. **The more dangerous half was server-side** and is also fixed: `IsPhoneField` feeds `NormalizeDocumentPhones`, so without the override the server would have rewritten a stored wave id or lot number into E.164 on every save — mangling data at rest, not just while typing. The inference is deliberately **not** removed wholesale: its own comment explains it covers every phone field in the schema today and every one added later for free, and removing it would have silently stopped normalising all of them. `engines/field_semantics_test.go` asserts both directions — scan fields lose their filter, real phone fields keep theirs, `phone_country` stays excluded.
  - [x] **47.6.2** The RF task shell (`renderRFTraceabilityView`, `public/app.js` + `.rf-*` in `styles.css`): no sidebar or top bar while a task is open (`body.rf-shell`, cleared in `renderViewContent` — the one place every view change passes through, so a bad exit cannot leave the app permanently chromeless), one instruction in words at the top, one scan target that keeps focus and re-takes it after every action, Enter advancing to the next field rather than submitting a half-filled form (a hardware scanner sends Enter after every barcode), a human label beside every code, no naked UUID, and 44px+ targets throughout. The supervisor path is a new **`FloorAssistRequest`** doctype the operator raises from their own session and a supervisor answers on their own device — explicitly **not** a Grievance (that is an HR complaint record; filing "I cannot scan this bin" into somebody's HR file is both wrong and quietly harmful) and explicitly **not** a password handoff, which is how shared logins start on a floor.
  - [x] **47.6.3** Built as **one responsive layer**, not six screen patches — every one of the six screens is assembled from the same vocabulary (`.table-panel`, `<table>`, `.form-input`, `.btn`), so fixing the vocabulary fixes all six *and* every screen added later; patching six screens would have fixed six screens. Three changes, all inside `@media (max-width: 820px)` so the desktop rendering is byte-identical: the sidebar goes **off-canvas** as a drawer (it was a hard 270px with no collapsed state anywhere in the stylesheet), `.table-panel` becomes a horizontal scroll container, and filter/action rows wrap. Plus a 400px reflow block for WCAG SC 1.4.10.
    **The measurement that matters, and a correction to how it was first taken:** `documentElement.scrollWidth` reported zero overflow *even before the fix*, because `.app-container` and `.main-content` are both `overflow: hidden` — so this application never scrolls sideways, it **clips**, which is worse and is exactly what "no *hidden* horizontal overflow" means. Re-measured against the right instrument (elements whose right edge passes a clipping ancestor that cannot be scrolled to reveal them), at 390×844 in Chromium:
    | | content width | sidebar cost | worst unreachable clip |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | before | 120px of 390 | 270px | POS 475px, Returns 553px — **7 of 7 screens** |
    | after | 390px | 0px | **0px on all 7** |
    Verified at 390 portrait, 844 landscape and the 320px reflow threshold, across POS, RF receiving, putaway, wave picking, sortation, loading dock, Returns and the RF shell, with zero console errors. The drawer was checked for keyboard operation too (toggle visible, `aria-expanded` tracking, scrim, Escape closes, focus returns).
  - [x] **47.6.4** The six-outcome vocabulary 47.6.4 names is complete and lives in one table (`RF_OUTCOMES`): success, duplicate, **wrong item**, **owner mismatch**, **hold**, offline — six because an operator must tell them apart *without reading*, and "already scanned" versus "the thing in your hand is not the thing on the screen" call for opposite next actions. Each carries a distinct tone, vibration rhythm, colour **and** border weight/style **and** text — four channels, because an aisle is loud (sound alone fails), bright (colour alone fails) and an operator may be colour-blind (colour alone fails again). Outcomes are resolved from the message-catalog **code**, not the HTTP status, since INVENT-0104/0106/0115 are all 422 and mean three different things on the floor — the first caller to actually use the precise coding `engines/traceability.go` has always returned.
    WCAG 2.2 AA work: a **global** `:focus-visible` ring (there were rings on ~8 components out of the whole application, so keyboard/scanner navigation was invisible everywhere else — SC 2.4.7), raised above sticky headers (SC 2.4.11, new in 2.2), and a 24×24 minimum target size on `.action-btn`/`.btn`/`.icon-btn` (SC 2.5.8, new in 2.2 — the AA threshold is 24×24, not 44×44, which is AAA).
    **Four real AA failures were found and fixed, by measurement rather than by eye:** `--warning-strong` on `--warning-soft-bg` was **2.86:1** in the light theme (fixed to 6.37:1); `--on-primary` on `--primary-color` was **3.90:1** in *both* dark blocks (fixed with the standard on-colour flip to dark-on-accent, 4.58:1, rather than darkening the accent — which would have cost it its contrast against `--panel-bg` and made primary buttons recede); six form controls had adjacent `<label>`s with **no `for=`**, so they were visible but silent to a screen reader; and two controls (`global-search`, `industry-selector`) had no accessible name at all.
    All of it is guarded by `internal/server/frontend_accessibility_test.go`, which parses `styles.css`/`index.html` and fails the build on regression — the same self-checking shape `route_capabilities_test.go`, `scope_policy_test.go` and the Stage 49 manifest test already use, because a one-time accessibility sweep lasts until the next screen. It computes real WCAG contrast ratios across **all three** token blocks (light, explicit dark, and the `prefers-color-scheme` default — a token correct in one and wrong in another is wrong for every user who never touched the theme toggle, which is most of them).
    **What this does NOT claim:** full AA conformance across all ~40 dynamically-rendered screens. That needs axe-core driving a browser over each one; what is guarded here is the foundation (tokens, focus, target size, reflow structure, shell labelling) plus the six screens measured above. The test says so in its own header rather than letting a green run imply certification.
  - [ ] **47.6.6** Device/scanner/printer certification and the gloves/weak-Wi-Fi/interruption/app-restart run. **[physical hardware/user input required — cannot be done from here]**
  - [x] **47.6.7** All four orphaned endpoints now have a caller, built into the task shell rather than as a sixth desktop WMS screen exactly as this item asked: `POST /api/v1/wms/batch/putaway` (bin-level lot assignment — what makes a lot visible to FEFO at all), `/batch/consume`, `/batch/expiry-sweep`, `/serial/status`. Live-verified against a scratch server on :8099: all four reachable and validating (422 on an empty body, 200 for the no-argument sweep), and the semantic registry confirmed served with `mobile-pick-wave-id → wave`.

  - **Four unrelated defects were found and fixed while doing this**, all recorded here so nobody re-derives them: (1) the RF shell CSS from 47.6.2 hid `.top-bar`, a class that does not exist in this markup - the header is `.app-header` - so the shell was still rendering the desktop search bar and industry selector above the task; (2) `db/migrations_stage47_2_*.sql` was not self-correcting on `POSPriceOverride.location` - an early draft declared it optional and `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` will not update an existing row, so any database that applied that draft would have read as unscoped for ever and leaked one store's override evidence to every other store; a follow-up `UPDATE` now makes the correction part of the migration. (3) `cmd/surfacescan`/`internal/securityscan` walked into `.claude/worktrees/agent-*` - full, **gitignored** copies of this repository that concurrent agent sessions leave on disk - and counted every `engines/*.go` outbound call site once per worktree, so `TestAttackSurfaceManifestIsCurrent` and `TestNoUnreviewedBypassPattern` failed for everyone the moment any session had a worktree open, for reasons unrelated to the code under review; `.claude` added to both scanners’ skip lists (`skippedDirs` in `bypass.go` already skipped `.git`/`node_modules`/`graphify-out`/`docs`, and `surface.go` repeated the same list inline, so both needed it). (4) `cmd/brainmap` emitted `BRAIN.md`'s lobe diagram in Go's randomised map order (sorted on weight alone, and `sort.Slice` is not stable), so `docs/brain/update-brain.ps1` failed at random on its own `-check` step - the redraw this repo's conventions require after any change that adds or moves a file. Fixed with the same (weight, from, to) tiebreak the region-edge sort in that file already used; verified deterministic over three paired runs.

### Release train 47.R1 — security, performance and operability foundation

- [ ] **47.8 Session containment and XSS-resistant browser boundary** — closes A-08.
  - [ ] **47.8.1** Inventory every inline handler/dynamic HTML insertion and classify safe text, trusted template or user/external data. Add shared escaping/sanitization at the narrowest choke point. — *2026-10-03: added a source-count inventory and escaped high-risk PIM/report/log/import and fixed-asset/printer renderers; all-source classification remains open. See `docs/security/browser-boundary-inventory.md`.* — *2026-10-04: inventory now complete and reproducible; classification deliberately reported as **incomplete**. The inline-handler half is finished (see 47.8.2): all 126 attributes enumerated, classified by shape and migrated. The HTML-sink half: **461** insertion sinks hold **637** interpolated expressions, mechanically classified as 192 escaped at the sink, 115 with no interpolation, 102 trusted/config/assembled markup, 43 markup loops, and **300 candidates flagged for review**. The 300 is a candidate list, not a defect count: a systematic 16-item hand sample (every ~19th finding) found **6 genuine or needs-a-human-call and 10 false positives** (fixed colour values, fixed labels, counts, markup helpers, assembled-markup locals, and nested templates whose inner interpolation *is* escaped), so the real population is on the order of 110. Sound separation of trusted config (`t.label`) from untrusted data (`line.sku`) needs data-flow analysis the classifier does not do, and a wrong classification is worse than an acknowledged unknown — which is why this stays open rather than being declared resolved. Full artifact committed at `docs/security/browser-boundary-sink-classification.json` so the next pass starts from it instead of re-deriving it; per-file distribution in `docs/security/browser-boundary-inventory.md` §3. **Shared choke points improved**: `actionAttrs` now owns escaping for every action argument (previously an unescaped JavaScript source position), and `cfgEsc` — a byte-for-byte duplicate of `escapeHTMLText` with 55 call sites across five modules, where an escape-rule change would have had to be made twice — is now a delegating alias, so there is one escaping implementation. Renaming the 55 callers is a mechanical follow-up with no behavioural content. See `project_ledger.md` §190.*
  - [x] **47.8.2** Replace inline handlers with event delegation/module listeners and adopt nonce/hash CSP without `script-src 'unsafe-inline'`; enforce in report-only mode first, then blocking. — *2026-10-03: strict same-origin candidate now reports violations to a bounded same-origin handler; targeted Log Hub, report, generic-record and asset row actions moved to listeners/data attributes. More than 90 handler locations remain; enforcing CSP stays unsafe.* — *2026-10-04: **DONE.** All **126** inline event-handler attributes removed — 109 across `app.js` and the 18 view modules, 17 in `index.html` — replaced by one document-level delegated dispatcher (`handleDelegatedAction`) driven by `data-act`/`data-act-args` that `actionAttrs()` emits. Converted mechanically after classifying every handler first: 107 of 109 were a single function call, and the two that were not became named functions (`goToDefaultView`, `selectFieldText`) because the dispatcher calls one function per action and has no statement sequencing — sequencing would mean evaluating source, which is the thing being removed. **This was a security fix, not only a CSP enabler**: an inline handler interpolated its arguments as JavaScript source inside an HTML attribute, so a record id, SKU or doctype containing a quote closed the attribute and the string and executed whatever followed — a live injection sink wherever that value came from imported data, a connector payload, a report row or a CSV cell. Arguments now travel JSON-encoded and HTML-escaped, so a hostile value can only arrive as data. **The new harness found a latent primitive in the dispatcher itself**: `window['eval']` is a function, so a hostile `data-act` name with a string argument would have been arbitrary code execution. `resolveActionFunction` now requires a plain identifier that is an **own** property of `window` (excluding `constructor`/`__proto__`/`toString`) and is not a native built-in — an allowlist by construction rather than a denylist chasing the platform. Not reachable today (every name is a string literal in this repository), so defence in depth rather than a patched hole. **CSP flipped report-only -> blocking**, both phases measured in real Chromium across all 29 dispatched views: the strict candidate reported **zero** violations of any directive *before* it was enforced (that is what authorised the flip), and after enforcing `script-src 'self'` — zero enforced violations, zero `script-src` violations, zero page errors, re-confirmed a third time after the dispatcher hardening. **No nonce and no hash list, deliberately**: there are no inline `<script>` blocks for a nonce to permit (all six scripts are external files), a nonce would add a per-request value that defeats static caching of `index.html`, and `script-src` nonces do not whitelist handler *attributes* anyway — that needs `'unsafe-hashes'`, a weaker position than removing them. The report-only header now carries the next candidate, strict `style-src`, whose 629 inline-style violations are visible but not enforced, so the same two-phase mechanism is in place for that step. All **128** `data-act` reference sites were also checked statically against every way this codebase publishes a function to `window`; all resolve except four pre-existing dangling names in dead `index.html` modal markup (reported, not fixed — removing those overlays is a UI decision). `TestSecurityHeadersEnforcesStrictScriptSrc` now guards the inverse of what it used to: that the exception is not quietly reintroduced. See `project_ledger.md` §190.*

Live deactivation/demotion and per-session revocation both remain enforced. **[needs security/architecture design decision: cookie vs. token topology]**

Every shared renderer and session-revocation proof remain open.* — *2026-10-04: payload coverage now spans every surface this item names, in two committed harnesses driving real Chromium; **stays open** on "every shared renderer" and on the session proof. `docs/qa/browser-xss-boundary.cjs` (prior pass, still green) covers integration/audit/system log rows, report columns and rows, PIM reports, the PIM workbench (imported master data), CSV preview errors and the attachment-link URL contract. New `docs/qa/browser-xss-action-boundary.cjs` covers what 47.8.2 changed and what that file cannot reach: **seven real breakout payloads** driven through the delegated action-argument channel (each asserted to reach the handler byte-for-byte while injecting no element, no handler attribute and no competing `data-act`, and executing nothing), **six call-primitive attempts** through the action-name channel, malformed `data-act-args` **failing closed** (only the well-formed array dispatches), and connector failure messages, API error envelopes, attachment filenames and CSV cells in **both** text and quoted-attribute position. See `project_ledger.md` §190.*

- [ ] **47.11 Graceful request control, worker ownership and side-effect safety** — closes A-10/A-11/A-15/A-16/A-17/A-44; extends rather than duplicates Stage 38.6.
  - [x] **47.11.1** Bound/prune rate-limit and tenant-concurrency state; define replica-consistent semantics using PostgreSQL only where shared state is required. Unique invalid keys cannot grow memory indefinitely.
    — *Done 2026-10-11.* **Measured first** (200,000 requests each, old code): rotating IPs left
    200,000 limiter keys (+22.2 MB), and rotating junk bearer tokens from one IP left 200,000
    keys (+23.8 MB). None were ever freed, and the junk-token run was **never refused**: each junk
    token got its own fresh budget, a full per-IP limit bypass. The tenant in-flight map kept
    every tenant id it had seen at 0, and before auth that id can come from `X-Tenant-ID` or
    `?tenant_id=`. **Fix, `internal/server/middleware.go` only:**
    - `sessionFingerprint` gives a per-session bucket only to a token that passes
      `engines.ParseToken` (signature + expiry, in-memory keyring, no DB), so junk shares the
      address's budget.
    - `RateLimiter` sweeps keys idle past the window (at most every 10 s) and caps its table at
      100,000 keys (~11 MB). A new key that finds it full, after a forced sweep at most once a
      second, counts in a shared overflow bucket per limit. Known callers keep their own budget,
      and overflow is logged once per sweep.
    - `tenantConcurrencyLimiter.release` deletes a tenant at 0.

    **Replica semantics** (written in the code and ADMIN_GUIDE §D): both are per process by
    design. Behind N replicas a caller can draw up to N× a budget, and the tenant cap protects
    each process's own pool. State that must be shared is already in PostgreSQL (account lockout
    `users.failed_login_count`/`locked_until`, MFA/reset tokens, `credential_version`), so no new
    shared table was needed. **Tests** (`internal/server/rate_limit_bounds_test.go`): 300,000
    rotating keys → 100,001 keys / +11.1 MB; sweep after the window; a full table keeps an
    existing 5/min budget exact; 5,000 junk tenants leave an empty map with the cap intact;
    through `apiMiddleware`, junk tokens hit the address's budget on request 101 and two signed
    sessions behind one address keep separate budgets (Stage 30.5.9). The junk-token and tenant
    tests are A/B-confirmed to fail on HEAD.
  - [ ] **47.11.2** Coalesce identical browser requests, cancel stale view work, define per-view initial-call budgets and return a specific retryable overload response/`Retry-After` instead of generic `GLOBAL-0010` for real overload.
  - [ ] **47.11.3** Reserve capacity for authentication/health/command completion so heavy reads/jobs cannot starve transaction finalization. Load-test the exact normal-screen burst that produced 8/30 failures.
  - [ ] **47.11.4** Complete **38.6** as one PostgreSQL job/outbox runner (`SKIP LOCKED`, lease/heartbeat, idempotency, progress/cancel, bounded payload/result retention, DLQ/replay). Migrate custom tickers incrementally; do not create a second runner in Stage 47.
  - [ ] **47.11.5** Use advisory locks/leases for singleton schedules and prove two replicas do not duplicate dispatch; prove takeover after kill/lease expiry.
  - [x] **47.11.6** External-side-effect safety: development/test default off, visible environment banner, startup inspection/quarantine of stale work, deliberate replay, tenant/schema existence validation and kill switch. Starting a server must not unexpectedly process old test/demo mail/outbox/reservation rows. **Built 2026-09-03 (pulled forward from R1 to satisfy 47.0.5's Gate-0 ordering).** `engines/environment.go`: `ExternalSideEffectsEnabled()` (default off; `ENV=production` turns it on; `ERP_ENABLE_EXTERNAL_SIDE_EFFECTS=1` opts a non-prod run in; `ERP_DISABLE_EXTERNAL_SIDE_EFFECTS=1` kill-switches even production) gates the three real outbound call sites that exist today — webhook delivery (`deliverWebhook`), password-reset SMTP (`sendPasswordResetEmail`), ops-alert webhook (`SendOpsAlert`) — each simulating/logging instead of calling out when off. `QuarantineStaleExternalDispatchJobs` runs at server boot (`routes.go Run()`, before any worker starts) and moves a Pending `webhook_delivery` job to a new `Quarantined` `async_jobs` status (free-text column, no migration needed); `ReplayJob` was extended to accept `Quarantined` alongside Failed/DeadLettered/Cancelled, so the existing Async Jobs tab's Retry action is the deliberate-replay path (UI: badge + "Replay" button, `public/app.js`). `GET /api/v1/system/environment` + a fixed, non-dismissible page banner (`renderEnvironmentBanner`/`#environment-banner`) show SIMULATED vs LIVE. Tenant/schema existence validation was already satisfied by the existing design (`listTenantSchemas` re-queries `public.tenants` live every tick) — no new code needed there. Scope note: "reservation rows" in this item's own acceptance text is a stock-reservation-replay concern, not an external-side-effect one — that belongs to 47.3 (exactly-once atomic checkout), not here. New test coverage: `engines/environment_test.go` (switch truth table incl. kill-switch precedence; quarantine-sweep + replay round trip) plus `alerting_test.go`'s two real-send tests updated to opt in via `ERP_ENABLE_EXTERNAL_SIDE_EFFECTS=1`. `go build`/`go vet`/targeted `go test ./engines/...` clean.

  - **Acceptance:** specified 30-call screen burst has zero generic failures, limiter memory remains bounded under churn, two-replica soak yields one job outcome, restart/stale-work drill performs no unauthorized external side effect.

**Stage 47 completion definition:** all applicable R0 conditional stop-ships closed or unsupported configurations technically blocked; R1 budgets and replica/restart behavior proven at representative scale; target owner/floor roles complete reference journeys without developer help; R2 documentation and supported claims match runtime; R3 qualified/external evidence is attached where required; no new infrastructure added speculatively. Only then may the product claim the applicable configuration is Production rather than an exceptionally broad prototype.
