## Stage 48 — Documentation architecture, governance, controlled migration and deletion ⏳ (in progress; started 2026-09-06, reviewed/rebuilt 2026-09-10)

Source of record: [`audits/DOCUMENTATION_ARCHITECTURE_PLAN_2026-09-01.md`](archive/audits/documentation-architecture-plan-2026-09-01.md). Evidence inputs: the [deep persona audit](archive/audits/erp-deep-persona-audit-2026-09-01.md), the [lightweight smoothness plan](archive/audits/lightweight-smoothness-plan-2026-09-01.md), the current 98-file/78-Markdown inventory, and the failed `docs/guides/update-guides.ps1 -Check` audit run that proved `cmd/gendocs` still writes to default live KB/OpenAPI paths behind a staged guide check.

**Purpose:** make every reader—CEO, product owner, normal/floor user, implementer, developer, SRE, security reviewer, auditor and legal reviewer—reach one authoritative, owned, current document for their question. This Stage reorganizes knowledge; it does not use folder movement to hide unresolved product defects or rewrite historical evidence.

**Non-negotiable rules:** plain Markdown + existing Go/PowerShell generators; no Docusaurus/MkDocs/Node documentation build/hosted docs database/new runtime service. The full `docs/` tree is not shipped to production; only the intentionally embedded Knowledge Center output/assets enter the binary/static release budget. No bulk delete, no `git add -A`, no path migration while another session is editing the same documents. Audit/UAT/incident/restore/decision/legal evidence is archived, never casually deleted.

**Dependency rule:** 48.0/48.1 may start independently once the shared worktree settles. Support/capability statements in 48.2 depend on 47.0 evidence. Security/POS/returns/WMS/audit user and normative documents must describe the real current limitation or wait for the applicable 47.x/49.x remediation—never document the intended fix as already built. Stage 47.15 closes only after 48.0, 48.2, 48.7 and the applicable 48.11 gates pass.

**Execution status (2026-09-10): implementation expanded and verified; Stage 48 is not fully accepted.** The isolated recovery capture preserves 889 hash-verified files at `fed51b4`; approval of a baseline commit/tag remains pending. The canonical drafts, 14-domain/42-FR traceability, 199-doctype/1,542-field dictionary, canonical OpenAPI projection, 49 KB topics covering all 56 registered screens, two generated manuals, strict offline lint and cached monthly external checks are built. A 24-path/five-batch migration is prepared in an isolated review checkout with retained published-path stubs; main-workspace paths and evidence are preserved. All 14 live development screenshot captures pass the repaired harness. These are technical observations, not customer, legal, product or physical-device acceptance. The [dated verification and findings](archive/audits/stage48-verification-2026-09-10.md) and [concrete migration review](archive/readme.md) record scope and remaining gates. Checked authoring/tooling items below do not imply approval of their draft policies.

### Phase 48.D0 — Freeze, inventory and make documentation tooling safe

- [ ] **48.0 Pure generators, recoverable baseline and complete document register** — must close before any folder move.
  - [ ] **48.0.1 Fresh inventory in an isolated docs branch/worktree.** Record commit, dirty/concurrent files, every document/asset/generator/output, file size, inbound links, first heading, metadata, last commit, audience, confidentiality and current authority. Create an approved pre-migration commit/tag before moves. **[capture complete: 889 files hash-verified in `stage48-baseline-20260909`; approved baseline commit/tag pending]**
  - [x] **48.0.2 Make `cmd/gendocs` output-root complete.** Guide references, KB Markdown projections and OpenAPI must all write under one explicit staging root; no implicit repository default can remain active when `-out`/check is supplied.
  - [x] **48.0.3 Make every `-Check` read-only.** `update-guides`, `update-kb` and `update-brain` generate to temp and compare only; brain check cannot copy `BRAIN.md`/HTML back; failure leaves repository bytes/timestamps unchanged.
  - [x] **48.0.4 One atomic wrapper.** `pwsh docs/update-docs.ps1` generates all declared outputs to temp, validates them, then copies only after every step succeeds; `-Check` compares a manifest and performs zero repo writes.
  - [x] **48.0.5 Generated-output manifest.** Record source → generator/version → output → scope/release/schema/tenant → checksum. Detect missing, stale, orphaned and hand-edited generated files.
  - [x] **48.0.6 Classify every current file** as normative/procedure/reference/record/generated/template; active/draft/deprecated/superseded/archived; canonical/projection; keep/rebuild/move/archive/delete-candidate; assign provisional owner.
  - [x] **48.0.7 Add immediate truth banners** to stale/high-risk living docs before the rebuild: BRD, PRD, ERP Blueprint, old loophole/QC/UX/durability audits, permission matrix, mixed current/proposed architecture and parity/maturity plans.
  - **Implementation evidence:** `docs/test-docs-safety.ps1`, `internal/docgen/*_test.go`, `cmd/gendocs/main_test.go`, `cmd/doclint/*_test.go`; `docs/update-docs.ps1` owns the output manifests. `doclint -write-register <explicit-file>` captures the whole working-tree documentation inventory. Content CI is reproducible without the ignored local graph; graph checks remain a separate source-snapshot dependency. Verified again 2026-09-08: 13 real-command safety cases pass (including manual checks); build, focused Go tests/vet, generated manual links and synthetic Chromium capture regressions pass. Embedded KB and search remain within the 2 MiB / 250 KiB budgets.
  - **Acceptance:** clean-check hash proves zero repository mutation; every file/output has one registered disposition; a reader cannot mistake an old audit/tenant snapshot/proposal for current product assurance.

**Stage 48 completion definition:** authoritative core rebuilt and approved; all living documents classified/owned/reviewable; user help canonical and executable; technical/operational/implementation/assurance/legal material separated by lifecycle; moves completed in recoverable batches; only eligible files deleted through explicit evidence gates; generated checks are pure and CI-enforced; documentation stays outside the production footprint except the bounded KB content intentionally served.

## Stage 49 — Evidence-driven maximum-security program ⏳ (planned 2026-09-03; ≈40-60 sessions, continuous release gate)

**North-star, not a marketing claim:** “most secure in the world” is not an objectively provable permanent state. The product goal is the strongest practical, independently evidenced security posture for each supported deployment and industry configuration. Never claim “unhackable,” universally secure, certified or compliant. Publish the exact scope, release, configuration, controls tested, residual risks, exceptions, assessor and expiry behind every assurance statement.

**Security constitution:** secure by default; deny and fail closed; least privilege; explicit tenant/entity/location/owner scope; server-authoritative business decisions; one durable outcome per command; no shared or hidden support access; no hard-coded/default credential; no secret or sensitive payload in source, URL, client storage, ordinary log or documentation; no control silently disabled to improve speed; no security-through-obscurity; no production claim without prevention, detection, response and recovery evidence. A security control that operators cannot understand, users route around, or the team cannot patch is not complete.

**Dependency and ownership rule:** Stage 49 does not reopen or duplicate work. **47.0-47.7** remain the P0 stop-ship remediation; **47.8** owns browser/session containment; **47.11** owns bounded request/worker/side-effect safety; **47.16** owns India-first control implementation; **47.17** owns reference-journey release evidence; **48.6** owns the normative security/privacy/legal documents; existing **20.5/26.11.1** own the external penetration-test engagement; **38.2d** owns OAuth2 client credentials. Stage 49 turns those controls into one threat-led security system and adds the missing platform, SDLC, detection, recovery and continuous-assurance gates. Security, privacy, infrastructure, product, finance/control, data, SRE and qualified legal/audit owners approve their domains; an AI/developer may draft and test but cannot self-certify them.

**Lightweight security rule:** retain one Go application plus PostgreSQL and the vanilla-JS client. No mandatory WAF appliance, service mesh, SIEM cluster, secrets platform, message broker, policy engine, antivirus daemon, Node toolchain or security sidecar merely to obtain a checkbox. Prefer compile-time route contracts, shared middleware, PostgreSQL constraints/transactions, OS/reverse-proxy controls, bounded structured events and optional export adapters. A new dependency/service requires 47.18.4 evidence and an ADR. Every 49.x item records binary/static-byte, cold-start, request CPU/latency, idle/peak RSS, DB/index/WAL/log/backup growth and operator burden. Security evidence obeys approved retention/legal holds and may not recreate the audit-log space failure Stage 47 found.

**Priority rule:** 49.S0 and the applicable Stage 47 P0 items are release-blocking. 49.S1-S3 must close before an internet-facing multi-tenant Production claim. 49.S4 must close before the corresponding enterprise, regulated or contractual assurance claim. Existing module work may continue only when it does not alter the same security boundary without updating its threat model and tests.

### Phase 49.S1 — Identity, authorization, application and data integrity

- [ ] **49.2 Identity, authentication, recovery and machine identity** — prevent account takeover without making safe recovery impossible. — *2026-09-08: 49.2.2 closed. 2026-09-09: 49.2.4 closed (see its own note). Parent stays open pending 49.2.1, 49.2.3, 49.2.5-49.2.8.*

  - [x] **49.2.2 Password baseline.** Long passphrases, breached/common-password rejection using a privacy-preserving bounded method, modern adaptive password hashing with version/cost migration, constant-time comparisons, no arbitrary periodic reset, no recoverable password and safe rate-limited change flow. — *2026-09-08: `engines/password_policy.go` + embedded `engines/common_passwords.txt` (~300 curated entries incl. India-corporate `Word@123` patterns) is the one choke point `handleChangePassword`, `CompletePasswordReset` and `handleCreateUser` all call now — length (new `security.password_min_length` setting, default 12, Stage 30.7 registry pattern), denylist match, username-equality and a cheap ascending/descending/all-same-character sequence check. **Real pre-existing bug found and fixed**: `handleLogin`'s bcrypt check had a plaintext fallback (`u.PasswordHash != req.Password`) left over from old local-seed configs — every real seed is already a bcrypt hash so it had no legitimate caller, but its failure mode was backwards for a security control (a `password_hash` value that was ever plaintext, from a bad migration or manual SQL fix, would authenticate via a non-constant-time `!=`); removed, bcrypt is now the only accepted form. Added bcrypt cost-migration (`handleLogin` re-hashes at `bcrypt.DefaultCost` on successful login if the stored hash's cost is lower — best-effort, does not bump credential_version since it's not a real credential change). "No arbitrary periodic reset" and "no recoverable password" were already true and are now fully true with the fallback gone. **Also closes the credential_version half of 49.2.4** — see that item.*

It now requires `current_password` (the same bcrypt check `handleChangePassword` already performs) whenever the email actually changes — a same-value resubmission from a form that always sends every field is exempt, so an idle-timeout-only save needs nothing. `engines.SendRecoveryEmailChangedNotice` (new, sibling to `SendPasswordChangedNotice`) alerts the OLD address, never the new one (a risk notice, not a confirmation flow — building email verification is out of scope here). MFA-change reauthentication was already true: the only self-service MFA mutation, `/me/mfa/reenroll`, already requires the account password (Stage 32.5); there is still no self-service "disable MFA" endpoint at all. **Auditable dual-control helpdesk recovery for privileged users** — the actual missing piece: there was no admin-driven "reset another user's password" endpoint at all before this. New `engines/admin_password_reset.go` (`RequestAdminPasswordReset`/`ExecuteApprovedPasswordResetRequest`) + new doctype `PasswordResetRequest` (`db/migrations_stage49_2_4_admin_password_reset.sql`, seeded into the existing generic maker-checker engine exactly like `HoldReleaseRequest`/`LoyaltyRedemptionRequest`) + new `POST /api/v1/admin/users/reset-password` (Users screen → **Reset Password**). An ordinary target is reset immediately, in one call, the same single-admin shape `handleAdminResetUserMFA` already uses for MFA — a one-time 20-character random password (same unambiguous alphabet as MFA recovery codes) is returned once, credential_version bumps (revoking every existing session), and `SendPasswordChangedNotice` fires. **A Super Admin target instead requires a second, different Super Admin to approve** via the existing generic `/api/v1/approval/submit`+`/decide` (maker-checker already refuses the same actor as both requester and approver); the reset does not execute until Approved, and the generated password is surfaced **only in the approver's own decide response** (`handleDecideApproval` gained one doctype branch, since this is the one approval hook in the codebase whose result may not reach the requester — every other doctype's hook lives inside `engines.DecideApproval` itself and returns nothing to the HTTP layer). A reason is mandatory once the target is privileged, optional otherwise. An admin cannot use this endpoint to reset their own password (points to My Profile instead). Every decision — immediate or approved — leaves a `PasswordResetRequest` evidence row alongside the audit log entry. **Real gap found and deliberately deferred, not silently accepted**: `handleAdminResetUserMFA` has the identical single-admin-clears-a-Super-Admin's-second-factor shape and was NOT retrofitted with dual control in this session — its own test (`TestAdminResetUserMFA`) seeds actor and target with the same privileged role by construction, so the fix is a real behavior change deserving its own session, not a drop-in.     *Built 2026-10-09 (five-item pass, item 3), to the scope the user set.*
    - **Where:** new `internal/server/request_envelope.go`, called once from `apiMiddleware`
      and from `publicAPIMiddleware` (the second caller boundary). It replaces the bare 2 MB
      `MaxBytesReader`.
    - **Limits** (in ADMIN_GUIDE §D's table): methods allowlisted (405 `GLOBAL-0303`). JSON or
      multipart only, plus per-route CSV/CSP types, and compressed bodies refused (415
      `GLOBAL-0304`). Bodies 2 MB, or 10/16/100/5 MB on the upload and import routes (413
      `GLOBAL-0305`). Headers 100 fields/32 KB (431 `GLOBAL-0306`). JSON is streamed and checked
      for depth 32, lists of 50,000, strings of 1 MB (2 MB for StickerTemplate), exact duplicate
      keys including escape-spelled ones, and trailing values (400 `GLOBAL-0307`). A 55 s
      deadline flows through `r.Context()`; an expired one answers 504 `GLOBAL-0308` instead of
      `writeEngineError`'s empty 499.
    - **Measured first:** stored documents reach depth 3, arrays of 7 and strings of 654. A
      sticker logo is ≤400 KB, about 546 KB as a data URL, and all of a design's logos share the
      one `design` string. `max_import_rows` is 20,000. Measuring found that the old 2 MB cap
      silently overrode every larger per-handler limit, because a nested `MaxBytesReader` cannot
      raise an outer one: media upload, imports and the hook were all really capped at 2 MB.
    - **Decisions:** case-variant keys are allowed, because `copies_override`, `dispositions` and
      `exchange_for` are SKU/line-keyed maps. A body with no `Content-Type` is read as JSON and
      fully bounded; the allowlist's real job is blocking preflight-free `text/plain`/form
      bodies. Rejections are not logged (log-flood risk).
    - **Tests:** `request_envelope_test.go` has one case per limit through `apiMiddleware`
      (status, code, matching correlation id, no leak). Large legitimate payloads pass: a
      3.0 MB 20,000-row multipart CSV, a 1.6 MB three-logo sticker design and a 2,000-line PO;
      the first two are refused on a default route. Plus deadline-vs-abandoned.
    - **Live on scratch :8147:**
      - a real three-logo StickerTemplate save returned 200;
      - a real 3.4 MB/20,000-row import preview passed the envelope;
      - dup key → 400, TRACE → 405, text/plain → 415, each with its correlation id;
      - a Playwright sweep of all 53 lazy views + 4 setup screens made 341 API calls with zero
        envelope rejections.
    - **Not done (follow-up 49.4.4a):** numeric range bounds and duplicate query-parameter
      handling, which the user's scope did not include.

    - [x] (b) Item import **preview runs ≈5.5 ms/row** (2,000 rows 10.8 s, 8,000 rows 45.7 s), so a

      it). The 20,000-row `max_import_rows` cap is unreachable synchronously. Either batch the
      per-row lookups in the preview or move large previews onto the async job runner.
      — *Done 2026-10-11, synchronously (no job runner needed).* **Profiled first:** on any tenant
      but `tenant_default` it was ~17 ms/row, about 20 round trips per Item row. `GetSettingInt`
      ran once per non-empty field, each re-resolving the tenant schema. On top of that, every row
      re-read the doctype meta, field permissions and the Master flag, did an EXISTS, then an
      upsert. **Fix, in the shared core only** (`runDocDataImportContext`/`importBatch`, so plain
      CSV, PIM preview and templated import all get it): `importLookups` resolves meta, the
      length setting, permissions and the Master flag once per file. Each batch prefetches its Link
      targets with one `id = ANY` query per Link field (self-links stay per row) and writes its
      valid rows in one `unnest` upsert whose `RETURNING (xmax = 0)` gives the create/update
      split. `ValidateDocument` became a wrapper over `validateDocumentWith`, and now reads
      `field_max_length` once per call rather than once per field, which also helps every
      single-record save.
      **Found on the way and fixed in the same statement:** the import upsert had the
      cross-record-type overwrite `7657453` fixed for generic create. An Item row whose code was a
      Brand's id overwrote the Brand's data and reported "created". The import now refuses that
      row. **Measured, same 20,000-row fixture, scratch tenant:** preview 6 m 0.6 s → **5.2 s**;
      import → **4.4 s**. **Live** on scratch :8147 (tenant_default): 20,000-row
      `POST /pim/import/Item/preview` **200 in 6.9 s**, 0 rows written. **Tests:**
      `engines/import_perf_test.go`, both A/B-confirmed to fail on HEAD:
      `TestItemImportPreviewFitsTheDeadlineAtTheRowCap` (must stay under 27.5 s, half the
      deadline) and `TestBatchedImportKeepsPerRowAnswers` (1,203 rows over 3 batches: Link
      found/missing/Cancelled, file-order ids, re-preview = all updates with no write,
      cross-type refusal, restricted fields).
      **Still per row:** a row with no id/code draws its sequence number with one query. Every
      Master import (Item, Vendor, Customer…) carries a code, so it isn't affected. Guides:
      USER_GUIDE §8d.5 large-file note, ADMIN_GUIDE §D.
    - [x] (c) The Extension Hook Log view calls `GET /api/v1/admin/extension/hooks/log`, which is
      not a route (only `/hooks/{id}/log` exists): 404 in the sweep.
      — *Done 2026-10-11, screen fix (no list route).* The screen is one hook's log ("Most recent
      100 calls for this hook"). Its id lived only in memory, set by View Log, so a cold open or
      a reload of `#/view/extension-hook-log` requested `/hooks//log`, which the mux redirects to
      `/hooks/log`, a 404. `public/view-admin.js` now keeps the chosen hook for the tab
      (sessionStorage, try/catch), shows "No hook chosen…" with no request when there is none, and
      escapes the `error` column, which was raw innerHTML. **Chromium on :8147:** cold open showed
      the empty state with no API error; View Log got 200 from the real route; a reload kept the
      hook; a mocked `<img onerror>` error rendered as text and never ran; Back worked; zero page
      errors. *Not fixed, noted:* a non-UUID id makes the handler answer 500 (a uuid cast error)
      rather than 404. The screen only ever passes real ids.
    - [x] (a) *Done 2026-10-11.* **Numbers** (measured: the 29,571 JSON numbers stored in both
      local DBs peak at 5,000,000, with literals of at most 18 characters):
      `validateJSONEnvelope` now refuses |x| > 2^53−1, the largest integer a float64 handler and
      the browser hold exactly (about 700× the largest real class, paise and epoch ms), and
      literals over 64 characters. The only inbound int64 decoder (the BigCommerce webhook's order
      id) carries small sequential ids. **Duplicate query parameters, model approved by the user
      2026-10-11 ("Reject all"):** any repeated name → 400 `GLOBAL-0307`. Names are compared after
      URL-decoding, case-sensitively, and an empty repeat counts. The raw query is split by hand,
      because `url.ParseQuery` drops a pair whose value fails to decode. A route that needs a list
      declares it in `envelopeRoutes.repeatableParams`; none does today. Every one of the 131 server
      reads is first-value `.Get()`, and the frontend never repeats a name. **Tests:**
      `TestValidateJSONEnvelopeLimits` (+10 number cases), `TestRepeatedQueryParam` (11 cases),
      and middleware cases for number, number length and a repeated/encoded-repeat query.
      **Live :8147:** over-limit and 70-char numbers → 400 with a correlation id and no echo; ₹50
      crore in paise plus a 13-digit barcode passed. A Chromium sweep of all 56 lazy screens plus 5
      paginated/searched doctype tables made 538 API calls with **zero envelope rejections** and
      zero page errors. ADMIN_GUIDE §D limits table updated.
  - [ ] **49.4.5 Host/proxy/cache security.** Explicit trusted-proxy list; canonical host/tenant binding; reject spoofed forwarding headers and host ambiguity; HSTS only after correct TLS scope; no-store on session/sensitive responses; safe cache variation; clickjacking, MIME sniffing, referrer and browser-capability policies.
  - [ ] **49.4.6 Canonical input validation.** Normalize once, validate business and storage form, reject control/bidi/confusable/path characters where unsafe, parameterize values, allowlist identifiers/sort/filter operators and test parser differentials across proxy/Go/PostgreSQL/browser.
  - [ ] **49.4.7 API misuse controls.** Per-command idempotency and replay window, resource/tenant-aware pagination and export, optimistic/row locking as appropriate, ETag/version checks for dangerous stale writes, consistent 401/403/404 disclosure policy and bounded rate/concurrency limits.
  - [ ] **49.4.8 Browser/API security regression suite.** Stored/reflected/DOM XSS, CSRF, CORS, clickjacking, request smuggling ambiguity at the deployed proxy/app pair, method override, open redirect, header injection, cache poisoning, path traversal, SQL/filter injection, mass assignment and oversized/decompression payloads.
  - **Acceptance:** stolen script execution cannot read reusable session material; cross-site requests cannot perform an authorized mutation; malformed/oversized/ambiguous inputs stop at one shared boundary without resource exhaustion or information leakage; strict policy works in the declared browsers/devices.

  - [ ] **49.5.1 Command catalog** for sale, quote/discount/override, tender, loyalty, return/refund/exchange, allocation/pick/ship, adjustment/count, vendor/payment, journal/period, payroll, master merge, credential/config change and export. Name actor capability, authoritative inputs, state preconditions, invariants, idempotency and audit result.
  - [ ] **49.5.2 Close Stage 47 atomicity work.** **47.2-47.5** must prove server price/tax/cost, exactly-once sale/return, owner isolation and reconciliation. Apply the same transaction/idempotency/failure-injection analysis to every high-value command.
  - [ ] **49.5.3 Workflow transition enforcement.** State machines reject skipped/backward/duplicate transitions, direct generic-doctype mutations and concurrent stale approvals; cancellation/reversal creates a compensating event and never edits away history.
  - [ ] **49.5.4 Abuse/fraud patterns.** Detect threshold splitting, excessive void/refund/discount/manual price, rapid stock adjustment, negative-stock exception, bank/vendor change before payment, duplicate invoice/tender, period reopening, mass export and unusual privileged configuration changes; tune by role/tenant volume.
  - [ ] **49.5.5 Trusted totals and reconciliation.** Persist authoritative money/quantity/currency/UOM/tax/valuation versions; balance subledger↔GL, order↔shipment↔invoice↔payment, stock availability↔ledger↔ownership and command↔audit/outbox. Exceptions are owned, visible and cannot be dismissed without evidence.
  - [ ] **49.5.6 Safe overrides.** Named capability, bounded value/duration/scope, reason, step-up/approval where risk requires, prominent operator feedback, evidence and after-the-fact exception review. No boolean “force” field creates a general bypass.
  - [ ] **49.5.7 Adversarial invariant tests.** Tampered client, lost response, retry/replay, two clerks/tabs/devices, boundary amount splitting, clock/currency/UOM changes, partial failure/process kill, webhook duplication, malicious import and direct generic-API attempt.
  - **Acceptance:** an authorized insider or tampered client cannot create an unauthorized economic result merely by changing payload/order/timing/path; every high-value command yields zero or one complete reconciled outcome and an explainable evidence trail.

### Phase 49.S2 — Hardened runtime, integrations and software supply chain

*Some items in this section are closed and archived — see [docs/archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md](archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md).*

  - [x] **49.7.1 Least-privilege identities and filesystem.** `deploy/postgres_harden.sql` splits the shared `erp` role into `erp_app`(DML)/`erp_migrate`(schema owner)/`erp_backup`(SELECT); verified end-to-end against an isolated scratch database, fully dropped afterward. `deploy/erp.service` narrows writable paths to three explicit directories (was: all of `/opt`). — *Backup/migrate identity split ready now; the server's own runtime identity is blocked — see 49.7.4 note.*
  - [x] **49.7.2 Service sandbox.** `deploy/erp.service` gains capability/namespace/device/kernel isolation, safe umask, file/process limits — `[needs deployment: verify via \`systemd-analyze security erp.service\` and a full smoke test on a real host]`. `SystemCallFilter`/`MemoryDenyWriteExecute` documented, deliberately left disabled pending that verification. *Done 2026-10-19 (109.11): installed on the droplet by `deploy/install_service_unit.sh` after adding `/opt/erp/audit-archives` (engines/audit_archive.go's default directory) to `ReadWritePaths=` - as written the unit would have broken the nightly audit archive. `systemd-analyze security erp.service` 8.4 EXPOSED → 3.6 OK; checked inside the running sandbox: the four writable folders take writes, `/opt/erp/public` is read-only; both hosts 200. SystemCallFilter / MemoryDenyWriteExecute stay off as documented.*
  - [x] **49.7.3 Network boundary.** `deploy/README.md` Part G has the exact operator commands (default-deny, DB non-public, direct-origin/alt-port, IPv4/IPv6 parity) — `[needs deployment: none of it is checkable without a real host]`. *Verified on the droplet 2026-10-19 (109.11): ufw default deny incoming; only 22 / 80 / 443 open (IPv4 and IPv6 rules alike); PostgreSQL listens on 127.0.0.1 / ::1 only and the ERP on 127.0.0.1:8080 (Caddy fronts it); from outside 5432 and 8080 are closed, 22 and 443 open.*
  - [x] **49.7.4 PostgreSQL baseline.** `deploy/postgres_harden.sql` sets least grants/schema ownership/default privileges, per-role timeouts/connection caps, revokes public's default CREATE; README adds a version/extension/logging checklist. — *Found and fixed a real gap while testing: `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` doesn't cover a tenant schema created after hardening ran — `engines/saas.go`'s `ProvisionTenantSchema` now grants it explicitly (no-op pre-hardening).*

All four fixed to match `engines/webhook.go`'s existing pattern, each proven with a real TCP/HTTP canary test.

  - [ ] **49.8.1 Upload/file policy.** Allowlisted purpose/type/extension/signature, randomized server name, path normalization, per-file/request/tenant quota, streaming size limit, archive depth/ratio/member limit, safe image/document handling, isolated storage/origin and download disposition. Reject traversal, polyglot and decompression/zip bombs.
  - [ ] **49.8.2 Import/export safety.** Stage/validate/preview/approve/reconcile as **47.13**; no formula injection in spreadsheet output, hidden sensitive columns, unauthorized link traversal, parser ambiguity or unlimited memory/temp/disk use; generated downloads are tenant/user-bound, short-lived and revocable.
  - [ ] **49.8.3 Inbound webhook contract.** Per-tenant secret/key, strong signature over exact raw body and timestamp, bounded skew/replay store, event identity/idempotency, content/body limit, schema/state validation, generic response, secret rotation overlap and quarantine—not immediate blind mutation.
  - [ ] **49.8.4 Outbound webhook security.** HTTPS and verified hostname, destination approval, DNS/IP re-resolution and private/link-local/loopback/metadata-network denial unless explicitly governed, redirect restriction, connect/read/total limits, response-size cap, signature, secret rotation, replay-safe delivery and redacted history. Test DNS rebinding/SSRF.
  - [ ] **49.8.5 Connector credential and scope.** Separate per tenant/channel/environment, minimum provider permission, never returned after creation, owner/last-use/expiry visible, rotation/revocation and disconnect cleanup. Provider errors/payloads cannot inject HTML, secrets or cross-tenant IDs into UI/logs.
  - [ ] **49.8.6 Job/outbox boundary.** Complete **38.6/47.11**: allowlisted job type/schema, explicit tenant+actor, bounded payload/result/attempt/runtime/concurrency, no serialized secret, lease/idempotency, poison/DLQ authorization, safe cancellation/replay and two-replica takeover proof.
  - [ ] **49.8.7 Egress and external-side-effect control.** Inventory every DNS/HTTP/SMTP/SMS/payment/courier/channel/printer destination; development/test default off; production allowlist or approval where practical; emergency tenant/global kill switch preserves authoritative pending state and reconciliation.
  - [ ] **49.8.8 Integration abuse lab.** Forged/stale/reordered/duplicated webhook, provider compromise, credential revocation mid-command, slowloris/large response, TLS/DNS failure, redirect to private IP, poison job, printer unavailable and provider says success while local commit fails.
  - **Acceptance:** external input cannot escape its tenant, parser, resource or workflow boundary; outbound calls cannot become a general network oracle; retries/provider ambiguity yield one reconciled business outcome; disabling an integration never requires deleting evidence.

### Phase 49.S3 — Adversarial verification, detection, incident response and resilience

CIS benchmark target versions confirmed live against production (Ubuntu 22.04.5 LTS, PostgreSQL 14.24) via SSH; exact CIS document edition number not yet pinned, recorded honestly rather than guessed. Reuses NIST SP 800-218/SSDF (already adopted, Stage 48/49.9) rather than re-adopting a framework. Explicitly **not** a line-by-line ~350-requirement ASVS audit — that's real, separate follow-up work, named as such rather than faked. `go run ./cmd/doclint`/`go build ./...` clean.

Every fixed security bug receives a durable regression.

  - [ ] **49.12.5 Notification decision workflow.** Versioned jurisdiction/contract/customer/processor matrix, discovery/assessment clock, qualified legal/privacy approval and evidence. CERT-In/DPDP/contractual actions align with **47.16**; the system does not make autonomous legal conclusions.
  - [ ] **49.12.6 Eradication and recovery.** Identify entry/root cause/blast radius/persistence; patch/rebuild from trusted signed artifact, rotate affected credentials, restore/reconcile, verify tenant/control integrity, monitor recurrence and avoid reconnecting a compromised worker/integration blindly.
  - [ ] **49.12.7 Post-incident learning.** Blameless timeline, control/test/detection/runbook/doc/support changes, customer commitments and tracked completion. Preserve immutable incident record; sanitize reusable lessons without erasing evidence.
  - [ ] **49.12.8 Tabletop and live drills.** Quarterly priority tabletop plus scheduled technical credential-revocation, malicious-release rollback, tenant-isolation, backup/restore and provider-outage drills; measure legal decision, detection, containment, RTO/RPO and communication accuracy.
  - **Acceptance:** the on-call operator can contain each priority scenario without developer invention or cross-tenant collateral damage; required decision/evidence clocks are met in drills; recovery uses a verified trusted state and every money/stock/security invariant reconciles afterward.

- [ ] **49.13 Availability, abuse resistance, backup integrity and ransomware resilience** — secure service remains usable for legitimate work.
  - [ ] **49.13.1 Availability threat/SLO model** per login, command finalization, lookup/report/export, worker/integration and restore path; identify resource pools and define which work sheds, queues, degrades or remains reserved under pressure.
  - [ ] **49.13.2 Bounded admission control.** Complete **47.11** for request/body/query/concurrency/rate/job limits with bounded memory and useful `Retry-After`; isolate/reserve capacity for auth, health, command completion, cancellation and incident controls. Limits cannot be bypassed with unique keys or used for cheap victim lockout.
  - [ ] **49.13.3 Query/storage safety.** Timeouts, pagination, result/temporary-file quotas, worker concurrency, connection pool, WAL/disk thresholds and cancellation for every report/search/export/import/audit path; expensive operations become authorized bounded jobs, never an unbounded goroutine.
  - [ ] **49.13.4 Backup threat model.** 3-2-1-style risk separation appropriate to deployment, encrypted off-host/off-account or immutable copy, least-privilege backup vs restore credentials, monitored success/size/age, integrity manifest, retention/legal hold and deletion protection.
  - [ ] **49.13.5 Restore is a security control.** Automated isolated restore verification, malware/compromise point selection, key availability, schema/release compatibility and post-restore tenant/money/stock/audit reconciliation. Backup success without a successful restore drill is failure.
  - [ ] **49.13.6 Ransomware/destructive-admin drill.** Assume application host/database credential or cloud admin is compromised; contain, preserve evidence, rebuild clean infrastructure, restore an uncompromised point, rotate trust and quantify data loss/outage against RPO/RTO.
  - [ ] **49.13.7 Graceful dependency failure.** DNS/TLS/DB/storage/payment/courier/channel/email/SMS/printer/clock/disk-full and backup-target failures produce explicit safe state, bounded retry and reconciliation; never silently skip security evidence or accept client truth because a dependency is down.
  - **Acceptance:** hostile or accidental load cannot starve priority commands or grow RAM/disk without bound; recovery meets approved RTO/RPO and proves integrity; no single online administrator/credential/failure domain can both destroy production and every usable backup.

  - [ ] **49.17.1 Per-change security gate.** Surface/threat/data/capability/dependency/config impact; tests and code review; migration/rollback; docs/runbook; resource delta. High-risk “not applicable” requires security-owner rationale.
  - [ ] **49.17.2 Release-candidate gate.** Exact signed commit/artifact/SBOM/migrations/config; clean build/vet/tests; route/permission/surface diff; secret/dependency/static/dynamic/fuzz/race results appropriate to risk; backup/restore and rollback; zero unresolved critical/high; approved expiring lower-risk exceptions.
  - [ ] **49.17.3 Configuration go-live gate.** Supported capability level, hardened deployment/drift result, tenant role/SoD review, credential/key/cert ownership and rotation, external destinations, backup/RTO/RPO drill, incident contacts, data/retention/legal basis, device/integration proof and customer acceptance.
  - [ ] **49.17.4 Security scorecard with evidence, not one magic number.** Attack-surface classification, privileged/access recertification, tenant negative-test coverage, unresolved risk age, patch/rotation/cert age, control/detection/drill pass rate, restore/RTO/RPO, dependency provenance, data/audit growth and user-reported security friction.
  - [ ] **49.17.5 Fixed operating cadence.** Continuous critical advisory/intake; per-release surface/control evidence; monthly vulnerability/patch/access/detection/data-growth review; quarterly threat model/access/vendor/tabletop/restore sampling; annual independent pentest and broader program review or faster when risk/contract requires.
  - [ ] **49.17.6 Canary and rollback.** Deploy security changes by risk-aware canary/config scope; monitor auth denial, business invariant, latency/error, worker/backlog and support friction; rollback never restores a known vulnerability without explicit emergency containment and executive/security decision.
  - [ ] **49.17.7 Independent challenge.** Rotate internal attacker/persona reviews and periodically use qualified external specialists for application/API/multi-tenancy, cloud/host, finance/fraud, privacy/legal, RF/device and supply-chain domains; evidence expires and must be renewed after material change.
  - [ ] **49.17.8 Honest disclosure and improvement.** Publish supported version/configuration, security contact, material known limits and dated assurance scope; feed incidents, near misses, support workarounds, denied-user friction, performance regressions and assessor findings back into threat model, requirements and regression tests.
  - **Acceptance:** no release/configuration reaches Production without reproducible security evidence and recoverability; overdue high risk, expired assessment/exception, failed restore/verifier/detection or unknown surface automatically lowers/blocks the affected capability status; operators can reproduce the posture from a clean checkout and approved deployment record.

- [ ] **49.18 Security weight, performance and storage budget ledger** — maximum security must remain ultra-light and butter-smooth.
  - [ ] **49.18.1 Per-control before/after measurement:** binary/static bytes, startup, idle/peak RSS, auth and normal-request p50/p95/p99 CPU/latency, DB query/index/WAL, security/audit/log bytes per 1k commands, backup size/time, browser work and operational minutes/alerts.
  - [ ] **49.18.2 Bounded state everywhere.** Login/rate/session/revocation/CSRF/replay/idempotency/signature/job/alert/export/support-access records have key cardinality, TTL/retention, maximum payload, cleanup job, legal-hold behavior, index/query SLO and overload response.
  - [ ] **49.18.3 One-event/one-evidence design.** Reuse correlation and references between business audit, security event, job/outbox and incident evidence rather than copying full payload/diff four times; compress/archive only after integrity and retrieval proof.
  - [ ] **49.18.4 No client bloat.** Security UI uses native browser/platform capabilities and lazy-loaded existing modules; no remote font/script, general analytics SDK, CAPTCHA bundle or heavy crypto in the core path without measured threat benefit and privacy/accessibility/performance review.
  - [ ] **49.18.5 No infrastructure reflex.** First fix root cause in authorization/transactions/query bounds/config. CDN/WAF/managed secrets/SIEM/EDR may be optional deployment defenses when threat/contract warrants, but app security and a supported lightweight baseline cannot depend on buying them.
  - [ ] **49.18.6 Security performance cannot be “optimized away.”** Cache only safe versioned allow decisions/data, never across tenant/user/scope/state; do not skip password cost, authorization, signature, audit or encryption. Optimize shared parsing/query/index/serialization and measure attacker as well as legitimate cost.
  - [ ] **49.18.7 Multi-year forecast and cleanup drill** for sessions/events/audit/replay/jobs/findings/evidence/backups/archives by tenant size; demonstrate expiration/archive/legal hold/restore without table lock, disk exhaustion, lost investigation ability or production service addition.
  - **Acceptance:** Stage 47 latency/RSS/binary/browser/hot-data budgets still pass under security-enabled representative load; every security store/process is bounded and recoverable; the minimum secure production topology remains Go + PostgreSQL + existing proxy/OS facilities unless an approved evidence-based exception says otherwise.

**Stage 49 completion definition:** all supported surfaces, crown jewels and trust boundaries are owned and threat-modeled; Stage 47 stop-ships are closed or unsupported configurations are technically impossible; identity/session/authorization/tenant/business invariants withstand the adversarial matrix; data, keys, host, database, integrations, build and artifacts have verified lifecycle controls; priority attacks are detectable and rehearsed through containment/recovery; independent testing has no unresolved critical/high finding in scope; every customer/legal claim is configuration-specific and approved; continuous gates prevent silent regression; and the fully secured reference deployment remains within the lightweight performance, memory, storage and dependency budgets.

### Stage 50 implementation and acceptance queue

BLD-046 itself still stays open — this only resolved the mechanical manifest-drift blocker, not the item's own real, unaddressed "interrupted cases" gap (abandoned-request cancellation) described above.* — *2026-10-02: BLD-041 (lazy screen code and initial payload budgets) opened — real progress, stays open; first session to touch it. **Baseline measured** (3 real cold-cache HTTP runs against a disposable scratch server, `Accept-Encoding: gzip`, consistent across all 3): cold-core (index.html + styles.css + db.js + components/erp-typeahead.js + app.js + qz-print.js) = 347,586 bytes (~339.4 KiB) vs. the 180 KiB budget; initial JS alone = 307,602 bytes (~300.4 KiB) vs. the 120 KiB budget — app.js alone is ~96.7% of the JS total and the only real lever. **Approach decided**: native lazy loading via a plain dynamically-injected `<script src="...">` per view group, NOT an ES-module/`import()` rewrite — app.js stays one classic script, so every function/let/const a chunk declares at its top level becomes global exactly as it would if it still lived in app.js (classic `<script>` tags on one page share one global function/var and top-level `let`/`const` environment), meaning zero import/export wiring at any of its ~600 existing cross-references. New shared `loadViewChunk(src)` helper (dedup `Set` + one `<script>` element + onload/onerror) lets `renderViewContent`'s existing per-view dispatch branches `await` a chunk before calling its render function — fits the already-existing scratch-buffer-then-atomic-swap rendering pipeline with no changes to it, and a load failure renders the same `renderErrorPanel`-with-retry every other load failure in this app uses (not a crash, not silently skipped). **Mechanism proven end-to-end on 4 real, independently-verified view groups**, each confirmed self-contained first by grepping every identifier it declares across the whole ~24,500-line file (zero inbound references other than the one dispatcher call, zero outbound references to anything but shared/core helpers) before moving it: Manufacturing (`view-manufacturing.js`, ~570 lines, incl. its own `currentMfgTab`/`MFG_TABS` module state), Expenses (`view-expenses.js`), Fixed Assets (`view-assets.js`), and Stock Transfer (`view-transfers.js`, incl. its own `transferLineItems` state and its reads/writes of the shared core `state.docData` scratch array). Live-verified with Playwright for all 4: a cold load that never visits any of the four fetches 0 bytes of any of them; the first visit to each fetches its chunk exactly once; a second visit (even after navigating away) is served from cache with no re-fetch; every screen's tabs/actions/sub-views render and respond correctly (incl. Manufacturing's MRP tab and its Quality tab's delegation into the shared, still-core `renderDocTableView`); a simulated chunk-load failure on a fresh page (`route.abort`) shows the retry panel with zero uncaught page errors, and a subsequent real load succeeds. **Measured result**: cold-core dropped 347,586 → 337,701 bytes (~9.9 KB, ~2.8%) and initial JS 307,602 → 297,717 bytes across these 4 of ~56 total dispatched views — a small but real, fully mechanical, fully verified dent; closing the budget gap needs this same proven pattern applied to most of the remaining ~52 views, which is real, scoped, repeatable follow-up (same shape as the `guardAgainstDoubleSubmit` rollout's own 3-session mechanical scale-out), not a one-session undertaking. `node --check`/`go build`/`go vet` clean; one full `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` run 100% green (after two `attack_surface.json` regenerations for the new static files — `go run ./cmd/surfacescan -out %TEMP%\...` then copied in via PowerShell, no permission block this time); `graphify update .` and `pwsh docs/brain/update-brain.ps1` run after (the latter needed one `brain.map.json` edit: added a `public/view-*.js` glob to the existing "SPA Shell" region so future chunk files auto-claim without a further edit). Files touched: `public/app.js`, four new `public/view-*.js` chunk files, `docs/security/attack_surface.json`, `docs/brain/{brain.map.json,BRAIN.md,brain.html}`, `docs/generated/brain-manifest.json`. Full detail `project_ledger.md` §180, build checklist BLD-041 entry.* — *Correction, later 2026-10-02: the dynamic-`<script>` `loadViewChunk` mechanism above was superseded. A concurrent session's scripted extraction (`scripts/_bld041_*`) replaced it with native ES modules loaded via `import()` through a declarative `LAZY_VIEW_MODULES` map and `loadViewModule()` (publishes each module's exports onto `window` so legacy inline `onclick` calls keep working). 18 `public/view-*.js` modules; `app.js` ~24,500 → ~8,400 lines; the four views above are folded into that map. Reconciled in this thread: `TestModuleManifestCatalog` now also reads `LAZY_VIEW_MODULES` keys; `TestRFOutcomeVocabularyIsComplete` now searches `app.js` plus every `public/view-*.js`; four screens that threw `ReferenceError` after the split (Finance, Fulfillment, OMS, Reports) fixed by moving each lost or misplaced declaration into its only consumer's module (`FINANCE_TABS`/`currentFinanceTab` restored; `FULFILLMENT_STATUS_BADGE`, `REPORT_TABS`/`currentReportTab`, `omsConsoleState`/`omsFilterQuery` moved) — no cross-chunk imports added. Measured after reconciliation, three cold-cache runs over the startup set: cold-core 142,667 bytes (139.3 KiB, budget 180 KiB); initial JS 102,684 bytes (100.3 KiB, budget 120 KiB); no view chunk statically imports another, so no waterfall. Browser smoke across all 57 lazy/inline views: zero page errors. **Still open before BLD-041 can close**: print, help and error-recovery are not yet verified on each lazily loaded screen, and owner review of the duplicate-code/entitlement criteria is pending. One unrelated, pre-existing failure remains in `engines`: `TestAuditVerificationOverdueDetectsSilence` — the known timezone-skew bug class (negative seal age from session-timezone offset), not touched, decision item.*

## Stage 52 — Category-based sticker/label printing module (opened + built 2026-09-27)

User asked for a real label-printing module for a jewellery retailer: different categories need visually different labels (an earring tag looks nothing like a necklace tag), printing should start from an actual transaction (a GRN or Transfer Order) rather than manual SKU entry, whole-document or one-line-at-a-time, and the user wants full self-service control over what a label looks like via a drag-and-drop designer rather than a fixed preset. Entered Plan mode given the scope (new doctype, generic cross-doctype transaction loader, a from-scratch drag/resize canvas UI, generalized ZPL rendering); plan approved 2026-09-27.

*Some items in this section are closed and archived — see [docs/archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md](archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md).*

plus 4 new "How do I…?" rows, a §7A pointer in the §3 module map, a cross-ref from §6 step 9 (GRN), and a **Sticker Template** glossary row. **`docs/guides/ADMIN_GUIDE.md`**: new **§B.3.4 "Category-based sticker templates"** (the no-templates-means-identical-output backward-compat guarantee, the HR-Admin/Store-Manager/Cashier permission matrix, the 3-step resolution order, three pitfalls to warn users about — `Item.category` is unvalidated free text, `Inactive` is the retire switch, the default flag is effectively single-occupancy since the resolver has no `ORDER BY` — mm/DPI sizing incl. the 300dpi-printer trap, the required-Printer-record exception, the `sticker_print_log`/`print_job_log` split, and 52.7 as the documented extension point). **Three doc drifts found and fixed while verifying every claim against source**: (a) ADMIN_GUIDE's printing bullet claimed "Nothing depends on this being set up" for all print screens — untrue for Sticker Printing, which hard-requires a Printer record even on the browser-fallback path (`view-printing.js:401`), now scoped to QZ Tray with the exception called out; (b) both guides initially said the Printer needs **Default For** = `Sticker` — it does not, `resolveQZPrinter` honours an explicit `printer_code` first, so that wording was corrected rather than shipped; (c) `QZ_PRINTING_SETUP.md`'s field-palette list was missing **Mfg Date** (present in `STICKER_DESIGNER_FIELDS`), now added, along with a Status note and cross-links to the two new sections. `go build ./...`, `go vet ./engines/...`, `go run ./cmd/doclint -root .` (320 files, 0 findings), `go test ./cmd/doclint/... ./internal/kb/...` and the Stage 52 engine tests are all clean.

Not built speculatively this pass (no request for it yet).
- [x] **52.8** Found live while walking 52.6's own documented steps (the "fix the app if the doc reveals a real gap" half of this repo's second principle), **not previously reported**: the transaction print's line selection was keyed on **SKU alone**, but `ResolveDocumentStickerLines` deliberately keeps a SKU received on two lots as **two lines** (it merges only within a lot — 52.2). So for a batch-tracked GRN: the two rows shared one checkbox and one copies value (unticking or editing either hit both), and the per-row **Print** button printed *both* lots, directly contradicting "print one line". 52.5's tests covered the resolver's lot-splitting but never selected one of two same-SKU lines, so nothing caught it. **Fixed at the one choke point both print paths already run through** rather than at each call site: new `engines.StickerLineSelection{SKU, BatchNo, Copies}` replaces the old `selectedSKUs []string` + `copyOverrides map[string]int` parameter pair on `PrintStickersForDocument`; `filterStickerLines` matches a line on SKU *and* lot (empty `BatchNo` = every lot of that SKU) and applies each selection's own copy count, so copy resolution now happens in exactly one place instead of two. `engines.SKUSelections` adapts the old shape, and a new shared `stickerSelections` helper in `internal/server` resolves both endpoints' requests identically so the browser-fallback and QZ-silent paths can never disagree. **Backward-compatible**: both endpoints accept a new optional `lines: [{sku, batch_no, copies}]` array *or* the original `skus`/`copies_override` fields, and a SKU-only selection still means "every lot of this SKU" — the manual scan flow and any existing API client are untouched. Frontend (`public/view-printing.js`) now keys row state by line index rather than SKU, sends the per-line shape, and shows a **Batch/Lot** column whenever any line on the document carries a lot (it stays hidden otherwise, so the common non-batch case looks exactly as before); `qzTryPrint` in `public/app.js` forwards the new field. New regression test `TestPrintStickersForDocumentSelectsOneLotOfASKU` pins all four behaviours (both lots by default, one lot addressed alone, a copy override hitting only its own lot, and a SKU-only selection still covering both). `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `gofmt` on the touched files, `node --check` on both JS files, the full Stage 52 engine suite are clean. No new route and no new migration, so `attack_surface.json` needs no regeneration (`TestAttackSurfaceManifestIsCurrent` passed mid-session and later drifted only on a concurrent session's new Stage 53 migration file). **Live-verified over real HTTP** on a scratch instance (port 8097, throwaway two-lot GRN): per-line selection returned exactly the requested lot at the right qty, a per-lot copy override hit only its own lot, and both the legacy `skus` and no-selection forms still returned both lots unchanged; the QZ endpoint emitted 3 ZPL labels for a 3-copy single-lot run and 10 for the whole document. Fixtures and their log rows removed afterwards.

ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS batch_no TEXT` migration, one insert column, one extra column on the Print tab's history table. ## Stage 53 — POS rebuild: standard retail till (opened 2026-10-04 from the user's live POS report)

User, looking at the live POS screen, rejected it outright: *"this is shitty pos. who ask cust code. Make it std pos, research and make it best / it should work with name search / Bill should which loction and store / location of store sellable non saleable / it should freeze untill i reset there should reset button"*.

**What is actually wrong today** (read from `public/view-pos.js`, `engines/qz_payload.go`, `engines/inventory.go` before planning, not assumed):
- The customer field is literally labelled **"Customer Code (optional)"** placeholdered *"For loyalty points"*. A `Customer` typeahead *is* already attached (`attachLinkTypeahead`), and the server's `?q=` search already scans **every** field (so name and phone already match) — but the box shows and stores the raw code, so it reads to a cashier as "type a code you don't know". The two loyalty buttons then re-read that box and say *"Enter a customer code first."* The capability is there; the UX denies it.
- The SKU box is `Scan or Enter SKU` with an `Item` typeahead, but `addSKUToPOSCart` sends **whatever string is in the box** straight to `/api/v1/availability?sku=`. A typed product *name* produces a zero-availability phantom line, not an error — there is no barcode→SKU resolution and no result list.
- `GetAvailableToSell` already returns `qc_hold`, `damaged`, `blocked` and `hold_qty` alongside `ats`; the cart throws all of it away and prints one "Available" number, so nothing on screen distinguishes sellable from non-sellable stock.
- `Location` (`db/migrations_stage17h_location_masters.sql`) has `type` (Store/Warehouse/HO) but **no sellable concept**. Nothing stops a POS session being opened, or a sale rung up, against a warehouse or HO. The screenshot the user sent is exactly this: a till sitting on **HO**.
- Receipts print the location **code** and nothing else — no store name, no legal entity, no GSTIN, no cashier. True on both paths: the browser fallback (`printPOSReceipt`, `${location}`) and the server-built ESC-POS payload (`BuildReceiptPayload`, `b.WriteString(location + "\n")`).
- Nothing freezes. The location picker is editable mid-shift, and after `Complete Sale` the cart is silently emptied behind a confirm dialog — the next customer's scan lands on a live, indistinguishable cart with no record of the bill just closed on screen.

**Two readings were ambiguous and were put to the user before planning (answered 2026-10-04): "freeze" = BOTH (lock the store for the shift AND freeze the till on the completed bill), "sellable/non-sellable" = BOTH (a flag on the `Location` master AND splitting the cart's availability figure).**

Scope discipline per this repo's first principle: no new framework, no new dependency, no new picker pattern. Customer/product search reuses `attachCodeNamePicker`/`attachLinkTypeahead` and the existing `/api/v1/doc/{doctype}?q=` endpoint; the sellable/non-sellable split needs **no backend change at all** (the fields are already in the availability response); the `Location.sellable` flag is one additive migration in the Stage 17.9 style.

- [x] **53.1 `Location.sellable` — the master flag.** `db/migrations_stage53_1_location_sellable.sql`: `Location` gains `sellable` (Select `Yes,No`, display_order 6), additive and `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` in the Stage 17.9 style. **Scope decision — `mandatory = FALSE`, not TRUE as planned**: making it mandatory would reject a save of every pre-existing Location the next time anyone edited one, which is the "destructive rework" the first principle rules out. The gap that leaves (a Location created later with no value) is closed instead by a save-time stamp — see 53.1b. **Backfill rule changed after running it against the real dev DB, and this is the important finding of the whole Stage**: the planned "`Store` → Yes, everything else → No" rule produced **0 sellable locations out of 112**, because `migrations_stage17h_location_masters.sql` seeds every location code already in use with a *hardcoded* `type = 'Warehouse'`. Shipping that would not have been an additive change, it would have been an outage — every till in the tenant refusing every sale. The rule now has a first clause: a location with an existing `POSCart` or `POSSession` against it is marked `Yes` regardless of type, because what the business actually did is better evidence than a type nobody ever set. Re-run against the dev DB: 2 sellable (`HO`, `WH01` — the two with real POS history), 110 not. Verified by direct `psql` query before and after.
- [x] **53.1b Save-time stamp (added, not in the original plan).** `engines/master_data_validation.go` gains a `Location` case stamping `sellable` from `type` when blank (`Store` → Yes, else No), never overwriting an explicit value. Needed because 53.1 keeps the field optional: without it, a Location created after the migration would carry no value, and the 53.3 picker filter (`sellable=Yes`) would then disagree with what the server accepts — a picker hiding a location checkout would allow, or offering one it would refuse. Attached at `ValidateMasterDataRules`, the choke point every Location write already runs through.
- [x] **53.2 Enforce sellable server-side, at the choke point.** New `engines.LocationIsSellable` + `ValidatePOSSellableLocation` in `engines/location_masters.go`, returning a `*ValidationError` coded **`POSOFF-0245`** (registered in `internal/server/error_catalog_extensions.go`, HTTP 422, Blocking). Resolution order: no `Location` record → **permitted** (location is still free text on most doctypes per 17.9's own decision, so unregistered codes must keep working); explicit `Yes`/`No` → honoured; blank → derived from `type`. Called from **two** entry points: `OpenPOSSession` (engine-side, so a future third POS entry point inherits it) and `handleCheckout` (above the quote and every other side effect). Checkout is checked separately from session-open deliberately — an offline-queued sale replays through checkout *without re-opening a session*, and a Location can be flipped to `No` while a session is still open.
- [x] **53.3 POS location picker offers only sellable locations.** New generic `opts.filters` on `attachTypeahead` (`public/app.js`) — plain `field=value` pairs appended to the existing `/api/v1/doc/{doctype}` call, which already treats unknown query params as data filters. Added once at the shared control rather than as a POS-specific picker, so "Active vendors only" etc. is one line away for any screen. **Off by default — all ~45 existing pickers send the identical request they sent before.** `attachCodeNamePicker` makes two lookups of its own (resolve-on-blur, seed-the-name) and both had to be threaded with the same filter, or typing `HO` and tabbing away would have committed the exact location the filter exists to exclude. The generic Location picker on the other ~15 screens is untouched — a warehouse is still a valid transfer destination.
- [x] **53.4 Customer by name, not by code.** `Customer Code (optional)` / *"For loyalty points"* is gone. Replaced with a split display/hidden `attachCodeNamePicker(..., 'Customer')`: the cashier sees and searches the **name** (phone and code match too — the server's `?q=` scans every field via `jsonb_each_text`), `#pos-customer` still carries the code, so `checkout`, `redeem_points` and both loyalty buttons needed no change. Label **Customer (optional)**, placeholder *"Search by name or phone"*, and a live note underneath reading **"Walk-in — no customer attached"** until one is picked. Both loyalty buttons' *"Enter a customer code first."* re-worded.
- [x] **53.5 Quick-add a customer from the till.** `+ New Customer` → name + phone → `POST /api/v1/doc/Customer` → selected straight into the picker. Two fields because that is what a counter can actually get; the rest of the master is fillable later.
- [x] **53.6 Scan-or-search product entry.** `addSKUToPOSCart` no longer sends the raw box contents to `/api/v1/availability`. Resolution order: exact `Item.code` → exact `Item.barcode` → name search, all off **one** `?q=` request (the exact-match test runs on the returned rows, so the scanner path costs no extra round trip and never opens a list). Single match auto-adds; multiple matches render a keyboard-navigable result list (↑/↓/Enter/Esc) showing name, code and barcode; **no match now says so** instead of the old silent zero-availability phantom line. Both paths funnel through one `addResolvedItemToPOSCart`, so a scanned line and a searched line are identical.
- [x] **53.7 Cart shows sellable vs non-sellable availability.** New `posAvailabilityCell`: `ats` as *"N sellable"*, plus *"N not sellable (QC hold / damaged / blocked)"* only when `qc_hold + damaged + blocked + hold_qty > 0`. Frontend-only — `GetAvailableToSell` has always returned all four and the screen discarded them. A line whose qty exceeds sellable availability turns red at entry with *"Only N can be sold here"*, rather than being discovered by the server at checkout.
- [x] **53.8 Cart line controls.** `−` / `+` steppers either side of the qty box (box kept for larger numbers); `−` at qty 1 removes the line. The Item column now leads with the product **name** and shows the code underneath. Keyboard: Enter commits the scan box, arrows walk the result list.
- [x] **53.9 Terminal binding.** `applyPOSTerminalLock` called from `refreshPOSSessionStatus` (the one function that knows whether a session is open) and after the first cart line. Store box goes read-only with *"Bound to … for this session"*. **Reset Terminal** confirms, names how many cart lines will be lost, and clears them — and if a session is still open it clears the cart but **keeps the binding**, telling the cashier to close the session first: a session is a cash-accountability record, so Reset must not be a way to walk away from one.
- [x] **53.10 Post-sale freeze.** New `freezePOSTill` / `startNewPOSSale`. All three terminal outcomes funnel through one function — **paid**, **pending_approval** (orange, no receipt offered, explicit "do not hand over the goods"), **queued offline** (explicit "do not ring it up again") — so none can leave a half-live cart. The 409 `price_changed` path is deliberately *not* terminal; it re-enters checkout and freezes on whatever that returns. `New Sale (Reset)` clears cart/customer/coupon/discount/tender but **keeps the store binding** (re-picking the shop between customers would undo 53.9).
- [x] **53.11 Cash tendered and change due.** Cash-only tendered box computing **Change due** / **Short by** live, carried onto the frozen bill. Display-only and never sent to the server — treating a mistyped tender as anything more would turn it into a pricing bug. The frozen bill's change figure is computed against the server's `amount_due`, not the screen's pre-checkout total, so a server-side reprice cannot skew it.
- [x] **53.12 The bill says which store.** New `engines.ReceiptStoreHeader` / `ReceiptStore.Line()` resolving Location → name, and Location → `legal_entity` → LegalEntity → name/GSTIN/state. Wired into **both** server-side branches of `BuildReceiptPayload` (ESC-POS *and* the HTML driver fallback) and into the browser's `printPOSReceipt`. Cashier added: `storedPayload["cashier"]` is now persisted on the POSCart from the resolved identity, so the server can rebuild the full header from the cart alone. Deliberately forgiving — a missing Location, a Location with no entity, or an entity with no GSTIN each drop that line rather than failing the print, because a receipt must always print. `ReceiptStore.Line()` also de-duplicates the Stage 17.9 seed case where `name == code` (no more "HO (HO)").
- [x] **53.12b Pre-existing receipt bug found and fixed (not reported, not in the plan).** The **browser-fallback receipt printed `0.00` on every line**. Stage 47.2 made the checkout payload carry `sale_price: 0` for every server-priced line (only an unpriced assisted-mode line sends a figure), and `printPOSReceipt` was handed that same array — so `qty * sale_price` was 0 for every ordinary line and the line totals summed to nothing. It survived unnoticed because the QZ/ESC-POS path rebuilds the receipt server-side from the stored cart, so the fallback only prints in shops with no receipt printer configured. Fixed via a new `posReceiptItems()` that pairs each payload line back to its cart line and takes the resolved price (and the product name) from there.
- [x] **53.13 Tests.** New `engines/pos_sellable_location_stage53_test.go` — 20 subtests across four functions: `LocationIsSellable`'s full resolution order (including the two that would be easiest to get backwards: the **unregistered-code-still-sells** case and the **blank-on-a-Warehouse** case), the `POSOFF-0245` coded refusal, `OpenPOSSession` refusing a non-sellable location **and leaving no POSSession row behind**, `ReceiptStoreHeader`'s full/no-entity/name-equals-code/unregistered cases, and the 53.1b stamp. All pass. `node --check` clean on both JS files. `go build ./...`, `go vet ./engines/`, `gofmt` clean. Existing `engines/qz_receipt_invoice_test.go` still green after the header change.
- [x] **53.14 Guide docs, walked not summarised.** `USER_GUIDE.md` §4 rewritten end-to-end against the real new UI: prerequisites box now leads with **Sellable = Yes** (was "Type = Store", which is no longer what gates it), §4.0 covers the store lock and why a shop might be missing from the list, §4.1 walks scan-vs-search, the sellable/non-sellable figure, name/phone customer search, quick-add and cash tendered, **new §4.1a** explains the freeze and all three frozen outcomes, §4.3 rewritten around the Print Receipt button and what the header now carries, **new §4.4** (price-changed confirm) and **new §4.5** (Reset Terminal, including the session-still-open case). §4.2/§4.3 physically reordered into sequence (numbers and all cross-references unchanged). 8 new "How do I…?" rows. §13's worked example Steps 7-8 rewritten (they still described the old flow). `ADMIN_GUIDE.md` gains **§B.3.0.4** (what `sellable` gates in all three places, the full backfill rule *including the 17.9 all-Warehouse trap*, the unregistered-code pass-through, the blank-is-stamped behaviour, and an explicit "check this after the migration, do not assume it" warning) and **§B.3.0.5** (what the receipt header is built from, and that filling in LegalEntity is what makes it tax-compliant). `go run ./cmd/doclint -root .` — 322 files, 4 findings, **none in the files this Stage touched**.
- [x] **53.17 A real Reset button, separate from Refresh (user follow-up, 2026-10-04).** User, on seeing 53.9/53.10: *"There should be button of reset. instead of refresh. Refresh will have cahe and have all info but reset button will reset all."* The header's one button was labelled **Sync**, titled *"Refresh local data store"*, and re-fetched labels + registered doctypes only — every cache survived it, which is correct for "a label changed" and useless for "this screen is wrong". **Built as two buttons, not one stronger one**: `sync-btn` relabelled **Refresh** (behaviour unchanged, title/confirm reworded to say what it keeps and to point at Reset), and a new `reset-btn` → `resetClientState()`. Reset clears, in order: every `erp_swr_*` entry in sessionStorage (the stale-while-revalidate cache behind every list/metadata read — collected into an array *before* deleting, since removing while iterating by index reindexes and silently skips every other key), `erp_nav_state`, the in-memory `state` caches, the POS till via the new silent `clearPOSTillState()` (split out of `resetPOSTerminal` so the two resets cannot drift), then re-fetches labels/doctypes/permissions and re-renders.
- [x] **53.17a Found while building 53.17, and the reason Reset would otherwise have been a lie: the lazy view modules had no cache story at all.** `loadViewModule` did `import('view-pos.js')` with **no version parameter** — an ordinary HTTP GET, so (a) after a deploy a browser keeps running the previous release's screen code while the shell updates, and (b) Reset would have cleared every app-level cache and then re-imported the *same stale module* from the HTTP cache, which is exactly the "refresh keeps the cache" complaint the item exists to fix. Fixed with two additions to the URL, both reusing the existing param-composition rather than a second mechanism: a `VIEW_MODULE_VERSION` constant (**must be kept in step with `index.html`'s `app.js?v=N` on every release** — documented in ADMIN_GUIDE §B.3.0.6's deploy note) and a `reset=<timestamp>` bust set by Reset, which also re-runs each module's top-level state (frozen till, locked store) back to initial values. BLD-041's existing `retry=` behaviour is untouched. All three URL forms verified serving `200 text/javascript` against a scratch server.
- [x] **53.17b Three deliberate non-actions, each a data-safety call rather than an omission.** (1) **Reset refuses to run at all while offline POS sales are queued**, with a message naming the count — `erp_pos_offline_queue` holds *completed sales that have not reached the server*, the customer has walked out with the goods, and that is not cache. Same posture `closePOSSessionFlow` already takes for the same reason. The guard runs *before* the confirm dialog, since there is no point confirming an action that must not proceed. (2) **It does not sign the user out** — resetting a screen must not eject a cashier mid-shift. (3) **It does not reset the theme** — a personal preference is not stale data. Every storage read/write is wrapped in try/catch (private browsing, quota, disabled storage): a cache that cannot be read cannot be stale either, so none of those is a failure worth stopping for.
- [x] **53.17c Docs.** `USER_GUIDE.md` new **§12.3 "When a screen shows something that can't be right — Refresh vs Reset"** — a two-row comparison table, an explicit *"reloading the page in your browser is not the same as Reset"* (F5 re-runs against the same stored copies, so the stale data comes straight back — the trap that makes a stale screen seem incurable), the four things Reset never touches, and a pointer that at the till you usually want **New Sale (Reset)** or **Reset Terminal** instead. Plus a new "How do I…?" row. `ADMIN_GUIDE.md` new **§B.3.0.6** with the exact clear-order including the lazy-module half, the three non-actions and the `VIEW_MODULE_VERSION` deploy note. `app.js?v=` bumped 29 → 30 (and `VIEW_MODULE_VERSION` set to match). Verified live on a scratch server: both buttons render with the right labels, the served `app.js` carries `resetClientState` and the offline guard, `view-pos.js` carries `clearPOSTillState`, and the versioned/busted/retry module URLs all serve. Shell accessibility/layout invariant tests green.
- [x] **53.18 The 53.1 backfill only ever covered `tenant_default` — found while preparing the 53.15 deploy (2026-10-06).** `migrations_stage53_1_location_sellable.sql` declares the field and backfills it in `tenant_default` only, but `ValidatePOSSellableLocation` (53.2) ships in the **binary**, so it guards every provisioned tenant the moment the new build starts. On any other schema that left no `sellable` doctype_field (so an admin could not even see or correct the flag) and no value on any `Location` row, which falls through `LocationIsSellable` to its `type = 'Store'` test — and 17.9 seeded every in-use location with a hardcoded `type = 'Warehouse'`. That is 53.1's own all-Warehouse outage, guarded for one schema out of N: **every till in every non-default tenant refusing every sale**, which for prod means the `minn` tenant. New `db/migrations_stage53_18_location_sellable_all_tenants.sql` runs both halves of 53.1 across every `tenant\_%` schema, looping in the style of `migrations_stage30_2_1_grn_location.sql`; statements are dollar-quoted (`$sql$`) rather than single-quoted because the quadrupled-quote form a single-quoted `format()` needs is unreadable and was in fact written wrong the first time. `tenant_default` is included rather than excluded — both statements are idempotent, so re-covering it changes nothing and keeps the file correct standalone. **Verified on a disposable DB** reproducing the trap across two tenant schemas: a Warehouse-typed location with a `POSCart` → `Yes`, one with a `POSSession` → `Yes`, a never-sold Warehouse → `No`, a typed Store → `Yes`, an already-`Yes` row untouched, a `public_decoy` schema untouched (the `LIKE 'tenant\_%' ESCAPE` holds), and byte-identical state after two further runs. **Then on the dev DB**: `HO`/`WH01` preserved as the only two sellable, and **9 blank Locations created after 53.1 ran** (all `*-TEST` fixtures, all Warehouse, none with POS history) correctly resolved to `No` — a gap 53.1 had left open on `tenant_default` itself, since 53.1b's save-time stamp only fires on write and never touches a row nobody edits. 110 `No` + 2 `Yes` = 112. `go build`/`go vet`/`./db` tests clean. **Does not remove 53.15's review requirement**: this guarantees no location that has ever sold stops selling, but a genuinely trading shop with no POS history in the database still resolves to `No`, which remains a business answer only the user can confirm.
- [ ] **53.15 Deploy.** Holds behind the same batched-deploy decision as Stage 51 — do not ship to the `minn` production tenant until told to. **The 53.1 migration needs a production backfill review first**, and 53.1's dev-DB result is the reason: which locations genuinely sell is a business answer, and the evidence-based rule will still mark a newly-opened shop with no POS history as not-sellable. Walk Setup → Core → Location on prod after migrating and confirm every trading shop reads `Sellable = Yes` (documented for the admin in ADMIN_GUIDE §B.3.0.4).
  - *2026-10-06: the `tenant_default`-only scope of the 53.1 backfill was found and fixed first — see 53.18. Deployed on the user's instruction ("deploy as well once all pushed"), which lifts the "until told to" hold. The Setup → Core → Location walk below is still outstanding and is the user's to do: this session cannot read production (classifier-blocked), so no one has yet confirmed that every trading `minn` shop reads `Sellable = Yes`.*
- [ ] **53.16 Found live, not caused by this Stage, needs a product decision: the initial-JS release budget is now over.** `cmd/releasebudget`'s `initial_js_gzip_bytes` gate (NFR-COST-001, ≤120 KiB gzip) fails at **129,143 bytes — over by 6,263**. Measured contribution: this Stage's two large `view-pos.js` blocks gzip to **8,862 bytes**, so Stage 53 is what crossed the line. But the structural issue is older and not this Stage's to decide: `jsFiles` in `cmd/releasebudget/main.go:36` still counts **`public/view-pos.js`**, which **BLD-041 made lazy-loaded** (`import()` only after the route entitlement check) — it is not startup JavaScript any more, yet it sits in a budget whose own note reads *"startup JavaScript compressed individually"*. That list looks stale against the in-flight app.js→view-*.js split (which is another session's uncommitted work — `public/view-pos.js` is not in HEAD at all, and `public/app.js` is 1.14 MB at HEAD vs 309 KB on disk). The test message says *"shrink the artifact rather than raising the limit"*, so the budget list was **deliberately not edited here**. **[needs design decision: user — either (a) drop the lazy-loaded view modules from `jsFiles` so the gate measures genuine startup JS, which is what it says it measures, or (b) keep them in and shrink `view-pos.js`]**. Two other suite failures confirmed **not** from this Stage: `TestAttackSurfaceManifestIsCurrent` (drifts on `GET /api/v1/ready`, a route from another session, plus the migration count) and `TestDriftGuardsUnmappedScreensSummary` (expects a `lazy-module-view` screen mapping, BLD-041's work). `TestDrillIncidentTwoStaleBackup` fails only in a full-suite run and passes in isolation — the shared-DB ordering flake this repo already knows about.

disclaims, so that round found defects this audit could not. Named defects now owned by the items
below, and each must be closed before its item is: **55.4** RFQ status cannot be changed with no

line loads and renders, the SKU typeahead suggests correctly), so it is a deployed-build symptom
and needs the deploy question settled before any code; **55.6** Transfer Order rejects `from_warehouse` because
