id-vs-code cause as 55.6 — check them together); **55.11** the PIM tab strip scrolls back to the
first icon when Catalog is selected; **55.17** Offer Management has no edit action. The vendor
record not appearing in PO creation is **already fixed in-tree as Stage 51.1 and merely undeployed**
— verify against the tree before writing code for it. Cross-cutting invariants and new scope from
the same round are Stage 57, not this Stage. *2026-10-05/06: **all six named defects above are fixed and verified live** (Stage 57 verification block): 55.4 RFQ - invite vendors, Mark as Sent, Close (Draft -> Sent -> Closed, the transitions the server already enforced), quotes from uninvited vendors join the list; 55.6 Transfer Order - `engines.ResolveLocationReference` accepts id, code or exact name and stores the canonical id (code, name and id all verified, unknown still refused); 55.7 Bin - Zone is a picker with inline Zone create (Zone gained an optional name); 55.10 - DebitNote.reference_po is now a Link to PurchaseOrder in every tenant; 55.11 - the PIM tab strip keeps its position across re-renders (it was being restored onto a still-collapsed bar); 55.17 - row actions are sticky on wide lists (Offer has 20 fields). Found and fixed:
    - **(1) The till did not start on the cashier's own store.** Every cashier had to search for it before Open Session showed. `defaultPOSStore` (view-pos-extras.js) now applies the signed-in user's location when it is a sellable store (the picker's own Stage 53.3 filter, so an admin at HO still picks).
    - **(2) A cart refused for a shortage could not be completed after the cashier fixed it.** The retry under the same cart number got 409 *"idempotency key was already used for a different sale"*. A failed finalise had also left the POSCart row at Processing, so even an identical retry after *"please try again"* got *"already being processed"*. `handleCheckout` now puts the cart row back to Failed on any finalise failure and **releases** the claim on a shortage (the rest still FailCommand). The approval / authorise parking paths are untouched. Test `TestStage555ShortageRefusalThenCorrectedCartCompletes` (a completed sale's key still refuses a different cart).
    - **(3) The checkout error showed only the catalog's generic line.** It now shows message, detail and action (`composeErrorText`), naming the item and quantities.
    - **(4) The Return tab asked for the store again.** Return Location now follows the till's store while blank.
    - **(5) "Return raised" said nothing about money.** It now says what the customer gets back (from the lines' tender shares) and offers to open Returns. Returns has **+ New Return**, which opens the till's Return tab, and its empty state points to it.
    - **(6) Inspect asked for a typed disposition word per line, by SKU code.** It is now one form with a condition dropdown per item, by name, plus an optional exchange item (reuses the exported `posFormDialog`).
    - **(7) Every inspection said "Nothing on this return is refundable".** The client read `total_refund`, but the endpoint sends `total_refund_eligible` (pre-existing bug).
    - **(8) Refund Eligible showed "-" until inspection.** It now shows the expected money, marked *expected*.
    - Cashier menus: the POS group shows only POS / Billing; POS profiles, offline sync / gaps and Offer Management are not shown, so the "separate admin and exception records" bullet holds.

## Stage 56 — Railway Pro migration (opened 2026-10-04 from the user's decision)

The user purchased a **Railway annual plan (₹3,000/year)** and decided to proceed; the DigitalOcean
droplet is ₹500/month. Assessment on opening found the app is already largely PaaS-ready and the
work is smaller than a provider migration usually is: migrations are `go:embed`ed into the binary
(`db/migrate.go:19`) so Railway's pre-deploy command can apply them with no `db/` folder on the
host; an unset `HOST` binds every interface (`internal/server/routes.go:1190`), which is what
Railway needs; every secret already has an env override (`JWT_SECRET`, `CHANNEL_CREDENTIAL_KEY`,
`AUDIT_ARCHIVE_KEY` — the config-dir file fallbacks in `engines/auth.go:61` /
`engines/secret_keyring.go:123` are dev-only paths production already bypasses); job claiming uses
`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (`engines/jobrunner.go:261`), so Railway's overlapping rolling deploys
cannot double-run a job; and the `Dockerfile` — dormant since 2026-07-27, built by CI's
`docker-build-check` on every push so it cannot rot — already produces a distroless static image
stamped with build identity (54.6).

**Architecture decision, recorded because the alternative was the user's first instinct:** this is
**one** Railway app + **one** Railway Postgres serving **many** tenant schemas, via
`TENANT_BASE_DOMAIN` (`internal/server/tenant_host.go`, Stage 44) and `tenantctl provision`.
It is **not** one Railway app per client. One-app-per-client multiplies the metered bill, the
deploys, the migration runs, the backup checks and the version-drift risk by the client count, and
bypasses the multi-tenancy engine already built. Hosting many clients is the goal; separate
instances are not the mechanism. A contractual physical-isolation requirement from a single large
client is the one legitimate exception, and is a priced premium tier, not the default.

**Production stays on the droplet for all of 56.1-56.10.** Nothing in those items touches the live
box, so there is no window where a client is at risk. 56.7/56.8 exist because the two things that
actually decide this migration — metered cost and India latency — are measurements, not
assumptions, and both are now cheap to take since the plan is already paid for.

- [ ] **56.1 Boot on Railway with the security baseline in warn mode.** New Railway project, app
  service from this repo's `Dockerfile`, Railway Postgres service alongside. Leave `ENV` **unset**
  so `engines/security_baseline.go` logs every `[SECURITY] SB-nnn` finding without blocking the
  boot — the point of this step is to read the full finding list off a real deploy rather than
  predict it. Set `JWT_SECRET`, `CHANNEL_CREDENTIAL_KEY`, `AUDIT_ARCHIVE_KEY` from
  `openssl rand`, leave `HOST` unset, let Railway supply `PORT`. Pre-deploy command
  `./erp-server -migrate`; confirm against `-migrate-status` that the count matches the 169
  migrations `custom_erp_t1` carries locally. Done when `/api/v1/version` answers over Railway's
  generated domain and reports this commit.
- [ ] **56.2 SB-015 — encrypted database link.** `engines/security_baseline.go:283` blocks a
  production boot when `DATABASE_URL` names a non-loopback host with an `sslmode` that permits
  cleartext. On the droplet Postgres is on loopback so the rule sleeps; on Railway the database is
  a separate service, so it fires correctly. Add `sslmode=require` and confirm the negotiated
  connection with `SELECT ssl FROM pg_stat_ssl WHERE pid = pg_backend_pid();` — the setting being
  present is not evidence it was honoured. Prefer `verify-full` + `sslrootcert=` if Railway
  publishes a CA for its Postgres; record which mode was actually achieved and why.
- [ ] **56.3 SB-012 — proxy trust against Railway's edge.** `engines/security_baseline.go:251`
  blocks production when `TRUST_PROXY` is on and `TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS` is empty, because
  `clientIP()` (`internal/server/middleware.go:606`) would then accept any caller's own
  `X-Forwarded-For` — forging the identity that login rate limiting, lockout and the audit trail
  all record. Railway does not publish its edge proxy's address range, so this is **determined
  empirically, not from the code**: with 56.1 still in warn mode, log `r.RemoteAddr` on one real
  inbound request, then pin that range (expect a private IPv6 or CGNAT block) into
  `TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS`. Verify by sending a request with a forged `X-Forwarded-For` and
  confirming the resolved IP is the socket peer, not the header. Record the range and the date —
  if Railway renumbers, this silently degrades to trusting a header, so it needs a re-check item
  at cutover and a note in the runbook.
- [ ] **56.4 Volume for `media_store`.** `engines/pim_media.go:39` hardcodes `mediaStoreDir =
  "media_store"` as a relative path and the image's `WORKDIR` is `/app`, so a Railway volume
  mounted at `/app/media_store` lines up with no code change. `engines/pim_dam.go:138` writes its
  render cache to the same directory. Attach the volume, upload a product image **and a
  thumbnail-generating one**, redeploy, and confirm both survive — a container's ephemeral disk is
  wiped per deploy, so an unmounted path loses every uploaded photo on the next release and does so
  silently. Note for 56.12: a service with a volume cannot run multiple replicas on Railway, which
  caps this at vertical scaling — the same ceiling the droplet has, so no regression, but it
  removes horizontal autoscaling as a reason to be here at all.
- [ ] **56.5 Flip `ENV=production` and confirm a clean boot.** With 56.2/56.3 fixed, production
  mode must start with no blocking finding. Also set `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` to the Railway domain
  (SB-008/011 block otherwise) and confirm SB-021 does not fire — `ENV=production` refuses to boot
  while the seed admin credential is still active (`internal/server/routes.go:40`), so a real admin
  with MFA must exist on this instance first, exactly as `deploy/README.md` Part D sequences it.
- [ ] **56.6 Prove multi-tenant hosting end to end — this is the item that makes the money.** Set
  `TENANT_BASE_DOMAIN`, point a wildcard DNS record at the Railway app, then
  `tenantctl provision` two test clients and confirm `client1.<domain>` and `client2.<domain>` each
  resolve to their own schema with no data bleed between them, both served by the single app on the
  single meter. Check `db/db.go`'s shared `dbMaxOpenConns = 50` headroom while both are live —
  go_live_decisions.md §4a.1 already flags the connection pool as a nearer ceiling than tenant
  count, and it is one pool for every tenant.
- [ ] **56.7 Measure the meter for 5 days.** The open economic question. Railway bills metered
  compute/RAM/volume/egress **on top of** the plan fee, and the plan fee is per-seat, not a hosting
  cap — so "₹3,000/year vs ₹500/month" is not yet a like-for-like comparison and must not be
  treated as one. Run 56.1-56.6 live for five days with real clicking, then read Railway's own
  usage dashboard for actual ₹/month at this workload. Costs nothing extra (the year is paid) and
  replaces an estimate with a fact. Record the number here. **Plan confirmed 2026-10-07: Railway Pro** — per-seat fee plus metered usage, so the which-plan question in this item is closed and the metered half is the only number still open.
- [ ] **56.8 Measure India latency.** Railway has no India region; nearest is Singapore, against
  the droplet's Bangalore. go_live_decisions.md:180 weighted India latency deliberately for
  GST/e-invoice/Pine Labs. Measure p50/p95 on a POS add-line and a receipt print from an Indian
  connection, both providers, same build. A cashier scanning items feels this; an admin editing a
  master does not. Verify Railway's current region list first — it changes.
- [x] **56.9 Rework backup monitoring from folder-stat to a database record.** *Built and verified
  2026-10-09 (five-item pass, item 2).*
  - **Table:** new `db/migrations_stage56_9_backup_runs.sql` creates `public.backup_runs` in the
    public schema only, since a backup is a whole-database fact. It is additive
    (`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` + `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`) and applied twice locally to prove
    it is idempotent.
  - **backup.sh:** `deploy/backup.sh`'s `record_run` writes one row per run (scope
    `database`/`tenant`, outcome, file, size, duration, sha256, host). A failed run writes
    `Failed` with its exit detail. A row that cannot be written keeps the backup and posts
    `BACKUP_RECORD_FAILED`.
  - **Monitor:** `CheckBackupFreshness` reads the newest successful `database` row, so tenant
    exports never count (26.1.6). `StartBackupFreshnessMonitor` always runs. Under
    `ENV=production`, no row is an hourly alert plus boot finding **SB-026** (warn, so a fresh
    install can still deploy). Off production it stays quiet until a run is recorded.
  - **Tests:** `TestBackupFreshness` and `TestBackupStaleAlertReachesTheWebhook` cover fresh,
    stale + latest-failed, missing in dev and production, tenant export and failed run. The
    BLD-052 drill now injects a stale row.
  - **Drill:** a real `backup.sh` run on scratch DBs covered whole-DB success, tenant success,
    tenant failure and a DB without the table. Each row's sha256 matched its file.
  - **Shipping:** `deploy.ps1` now re-ships `deploy/backup.sh` (+ `chmod 755`) every deploy,
    as it already did `remote_deploy.sh`.
  - **Docs:** ADMIN_GUIDE §D, `backup_restore.md` and `incident_runbook.md` are updated.

  Original item text: The real code work
  in this Stage, and an improvement independent of hosting. `engines/alerting.go:297` resolves
  `BACKUP_DIR` (default `/opt/erp/backups`) and `CheckBackupFreshness` stats the newest
  `custom_erp_*.dump.enc` in it — and at `engines/alerting.go:310` treats a **missing directory as
  "not a machine that takes backups" and returns silently**. Correct on a dev laptop; on a PaaS it
  means backup monitoring switches itself off and reports nothing, which is the worst available
  failure mode. It also cannot be fixed by pointing `BACKUP_DIR` at a volume, because Railway does
  not share a volume between services: the app checking the folder and the job writing to it are
  different services and cannot see each other's disk. Fix: additive `backup_runs` table (new
  incremental migration), the backup job writes a row on success with size/duration/outcome, and
  `CheckBackupFreshness` reads the latest row instead of stat-ing a directory. Keep the
  `tenant_*`-exports-do-not-count rule (26.1.6). Separately, make "no backup mechanism configured"
  a **loud** finding when `ENV=production` rather than silence, on both providers.
- [ ] **56.10 Stand up the backup job itself on Railway.** A cron-scheduled service running the
  `pg_dump` → encrypt (`BACKUP_ENCRYPTION_KEY`) → retain path that `deploy/backup.sh` does today,
  writing its own volume plus the 56.9 row, with Railway's native Postgres backups enabled as a
  second independent copy rather than the only one. Then a **real restore drill** into a scratch
  database per `docs/operations/backup_restore.md` — a schedule existing is not evidence a backup
  restores, which is the lesson 2026-08-07 already taught on the droplet. Must pass before 56.11.
- [ ] **56.11 Decision gate — migrate production, or keep Railway as staging/demo.**
  `[needs design decision: the user's call, on 56.7 + 56.8 numbers, not on the plan fee]`. Inputs:
  measured ₹/month, measured India latency, and what is being given up —
  `deploy/remote_deploy.sh`'s staged `.prev` snapshots with commit-matched health gating and
  automatic rollback (Stage 50/BLD-005/BLD-006), `deploy/postgres_harden.sql`'s least-privilege
  `erp_app`/`erp_migrate`/`erp_backup` split, and the 49.7.3 `ufw`/port-boundary verification, none
  of which port to a PaaS as-is. If the numbers favour the droplet, Railway still earns the year as
  a **demo/trial instance** — sandbox provisioning already exists (`handleProvisionSandboxTenant`,
  Stage 38.7) and a demo that idles keeps the meter near zero. Either outcome uses what was bought;
  only one moves client data.
- [ ] **56.12 Cutover — only if 56.11 says go.** Real domain + TLS on Railway,
  `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` repointed, database moved (small: §26.10.6 measured production as holding
  no transactional data, so this is minutes — re-measure before trusting it), re-run the 56.3
  proxy-range check, full module smoke pass, then DNS cutover with **the droplet kept warm for a
  week** as the rollback. Decommission only after that week. Per the standing release principle: no
  client data impacted, no already-set password changed.
- [ ] **56.13 Docs.** `docs/operations/go_live_decisions.md` §4 gains the revisit entry and the
  outcome (the §4 "decided 2026-08-06 — the droplet is production" call is what this Stage
  reopens); `deploy/README.md` gains or yields to a Railway runbook; `docs/ai_handover.md` §6
  records the new deploy path and dev-server state; `USER_GUIDE`/`ADMIN_GUIDE` need a pass if and
  when 56.6's per-client subdomains become customer-facing, which they do.

**2026-10-07 — pre-emptive fix landed for 56.1's expected first failure (`Dockerfile`, uncommitted).**
At the user's request, rather than waiting for the deploy to fail: the runtime stage's base image is
now `ARG RUNTIME_BASE=gcr.io/distroless/static-debian12:debug`, used as `FROM ${RUNTIME_BASE}`. The
`:debug` variant is the same distroless image plus a busybox `/bin/sh` and nothing else — same
`nonroot` user, same static-only contents. Reason: a PaaS pre-deploy/release hook runs its command
*through a shell*, so `./erp-server -migrate` on the shell-free image fails with
`exec: "sh": executable file not found`, which reads like a missing binary and is not one. Made an
overridable ARG rather than a hardcoded swap so the shell-free image can be pinned back with
`--build-arg RUNTIME_BASE=gcr.io/distroless/static-debian12` without editing the file.
**The ARG is declared global (before the first `FROM`) deliberately** — declared inside the builder
stage it is out of scope at the runtime `FROM`, expands to empty, and the build dies on a bare
`FROM`; that mistake was made and corrected while writing this.
Two caveats, both load-bearing:
- **Unverified by build.** Docker is not installed on this machine (`docker: command not found`), so
  this has never been built. CI's `docker-build-check` is the first thing that will exercise it, and
  Railway the first thing that will boot it. Do not record it as working until one of those passes.
- **It cannot reach Railway until it is pushed.** Railway builds from GitHub, which is at `c90e5ca`
  (2026-10-06); this change is uncommitted in the working tree. Until a commit of `Dockerfile`
  reaches `origin/main`, 56.1 will still hit the original shell failure. `Dockerfile`'s only
  uncommitted hunk is this change (54.6's build-identity ARGs are already committed in `c90e5ca`),
  so it is cleanly committable on its own — but committing/pushing is the user's call, not a routine
  dev step, per the Git Safety Protocol.
- **Retire this before 56.12** if a container path ever becomes production: a shell in the runtime
  image widens the Stage 49.S2 attack surface and is justified only while the migrate hook needs it.
  Either run migrations as a one-off job that bypasses a shell, or confirm the platform can invoke
  the hook in exec form. Today's droplet production runs the plain binary under systemd and is
  unaffected by this line.

**Carried from the opening assessment, so it is not rediscovered:** the four items that read as
alarming (56.2, 56.3, 56.4, 56.9) are ~1 to 1.5 days of work in total, not the bulk of this Stage.
Three are config-sized once the empirical value in 56.3 is known. The weight is in 56.9/56.10
(backups, done properly) and 56.12 (verification and a safe cutover) — the same shape as any
provider move: the container was never the hard part.

## Stage 57 — User QA round: cross-cutting UI invariants and new scope (opened 2026-10-04 from the user's live report)

Full triage, with verbatim reports, root causes and answers: [user QA round 2026-10-04](product/erp-user-qa-2026-10-04.md).
The user operated the **deployed** build and actually submitted transactions (PO, GRN, TO, Bin,
Vendor) — the real-user acceptance Stage 55.0 explicitly disclaims. Confirmed defects from that
round are filed against the matching open Stage 55 sub-items rather than duplicated here; this
Stage holds only the **cross-cutting invariants** and the **new scope** Stage 55 does not authorize.

**Already fixed this pass:** the bulk-import template download asked for browser credentials
because `GET /api/v1/import/{doctype}/template` is behind `apiMiddleware` while
`downloadImportTemplate` used a plain anchor `href` (no `Authorization` header, so a 401 and the
browser's own prompt). It now fetches via `apiFetch` and saves a blob — `public/app.js`.

### Cross-cutting invariants (one choke point each, not a per-screen sweep)

- [x] **57.1 Show the Name, never the document code, in all UI.** The user sees
  `Department/HQ/2026/000001` where a name belongs, and asks for this "everywhere". One
  display-resolution helper for Link fields at the shared renderer; the id stays the submitted
  value, only the label changes. **Highest-leverage item in the round** — it is also the visible
  half of the id-vs-code validator defects (TO `from_warehouse`, credit/debit note PO picker).
  — *2026-10-05/06: built and verified live. `installNameDisplay` (public/app.js) gives every `attachLinkTypeahead` picker a name-showing box whose `.value` still returns the code - no reader changed; a view-wide sweep (`sweepLinkNames`, MutationObserver) swaps names in for `data-link-ref` cells and for cells whose whole text is a master series code `<RecordType>/...` (document numbers like PO/HO/26-27/000002 are left alone; a master's own list keeps its Code column). Browser check: Transfer list shows "Bandra Store" not `Location/HQ/2026/000009`; picker shows the name with value `LOC9`.*
- [x] **57.2 Universal missing-prerequisite pattern.** Today a missing master leaves a half-broken
  form behind the "No Department has been set up yet" prompt. Wanted: a full-screen takeover
  showing only the setup prompt, return-to-origin on browser Back, and the prompt degrading to
  non-blocking once the master exists. Must be one implementation for every prerequisite —
  Department, Zone (Bin creation hard-blocks the same way), HSN, and the rest. Pairs with

Inline quick-create (public/view-pickers.js, lazy) opens on top of the open form, asks only required fields from /meta with the same server-numbering rules as the full form, seeds the typed text, and fills the field on save. Field setup hints create inline; the screen banner's navigate path now closes the open new-record form, pushes one history entry (browser Back returns), and reopens the form with its values and the new record filled in (`performQuickCreateReturn`). Browser check: PR form -> "Set up Department" -> created "Finance" without the form closing; hint drops to the quiet "Can't find…" line.*

One vanilla-JS picker (public/view-pickers.js, loaded on first open) replaces the browser's picker on every `input[type=date]` app-wide via one observer; the native input, its YYYY-MM-DD value, typing and min/max are unchanged. Keyboard: Alt+Down/F4, arrows, PageUp/Down, Home/End, Enter, Esc. **Also found and fixed a real bug:** Appointment Calendar's day/week arrows and week columns used `toISOString().slice(0,10)` - in IST that is the previous day, so "next day" did nothing; same bug in two report defaults. Shared `localISODate` fixes all five. Start/End are now time inputs. Browser check: next-day arrow advances, picker navigates and selects.*

- [x] **57.13 Two-factor recovery code count.** *Decided by the user 2026-10-07: keep 10. No change.* *[needs design decision: user — confirm intent]*
  Reported as "10 of 10 left — there should be no limit", but "10 of 10 left" is the count of
  *unused* single-use codes, not a cap that can be hit. `engines/mfa_recovery.go:28` sets
  `RecoveryCodeCount = 10` at 50 bits of entropy each; 10 is the industry norm and Stage 49.2.3
  deliberately requires single-use hashed codes, so "unlimited" is not a property recovery codes
  can have. If the real want is *regenerating* a fresh set on demand, that already exists and may
  only need to be more discoverable. **Do not change this without confirmation — it is a security
  control.**

  — *2026-10-08: built and verified (user decision 2026-10-06: build it).* New doctype **PurchaseReturn** (migration `db/migrations_stage57_15_purchase_return.sql`, replayed into every tenant schema: doctype, fields, HR/Admin + Store Manager grants, `PRT` and `DN` number series). `engines/purchase_return.go`: a return is raised against the original GRN, capped per SKU/lot/bucket at what the GRN received (Accepted, or the QC Rejected/Damaged split) less posted returns and other open returns; a GRN that is Cancelled (its stock never posted) is refused. Validated at `ValidateTransactionalRules` (the generic doc API's choke point): PURCHA-0116 reason, 0117 over-return / line not on the GRN, 0118 no/cancelled GRN, 0119 already on a return; a doc-API write can only set Draft/Cancelled. `PostPurchaseReturn` (POST `/api/v1/procurement/purchase-returns/{id}/post`) in one transaction locks the return and the GRN, re-checks, moves accepted stock out of available via `PostInventoryLedgerWithVoucherTx` (floor-checked) and set-aside stock out of qc_hold/damaged, posts Dr 5150 / Cr 1200, inserts the DebitNote, marks the return Posted; then posts the DebitNote through the unchanged `PostDebitNote` (Dr 2100 / Cr 5150; failure leaves it Draft with PURCHA-0120), writes stock-ledger entries and takes the receipt back out of item_cost. Maker-checker: if an approval rule exists for PurchaseReturn, only an Approved return posts. `GET .../purchase-returns/context` gives the returnable lines (scope-checked). **Found while verifying:** after a return, AP three-way match still valued the GRN at its gross accepted qty, so the vendor's correct net bill would hold and a bill for the returned units would match - `MatchVendorInvoice` now nets posted returns (engines/vendor_invoice.go). Screen: **Procurement → Purchase Return** (view-procurement.js). Tests: `TestPurchaseReturn` (all five codes' paths, stock 8/2 -> 5/0, DN posted, 2100/1200 net 500, 5150 nets 0, re-post refused, net bill matches, gross bill holds); server tests incl. module-manifest and route classification. Live on :8147: GRN `GRN/HO/26-27/000024` (8 accepted, 2 rejected at Rs 100) -> return 3 accepted + 2 rejected -> stock 5/5/0, `DN/HO/26-27/000001` Posted for 300, GL 2100 Cr 800 / Dr 300, 1200 Dr 800 / Cr 300, 5150 nets 0; browser: returned 2 of lot LOT-AUG on `GRN/HO/26-27/000022` (`DN/HO/26-27/000002`, the GRN then shows Returned 2 / Can return 3); approval-gated path (Draft post refused, self-approval refused, second user approves, post); Cancel releases the qty. Not done: GST input-credit reversal on returned goods (*done 2026-10-10, see "2026-10-09 five-item pass" item 4*), WMS bin quantities (stays out of erp-d1's wms files). PO/GRN are taken from the invoice itself (the UI no longer re-asks; a disagreeing reference is refused), and a hold returns plain reasons shown in the Match dialog (FA-06's "comparison details"). PO with no rates keeps the old rule (invoice = PO total), so it still fails closed. cold core by ~5.7 KB gzip); fixed by moving the picker and quick-create dialog (and their CSS)
into lazily loaded `public/view-pickers.js`, then condensing this round's own shell comments - now

addition by any session should expect to trip it.
**Also fixed this round:** (1) every page load sends ~28 CSP reports (`style-src-attr`, report-only)
with no Authorization header, which shared the IP-keyed "default" bucket (60/min) - several tills
behind one shop IP could exhaust it and get an ordinary unauthenticated request (the login screen's)
refused with a **Rate Limit** message: the likely source of the user's question. CSP reports now have
their own bucket (`rateLimitCategory`, test `TestCSPReportsHaveTheirOwnRateLimitBucket`); the inline
styles themselves remain a separate clean-up. (2) The user's SOP PO chapter was blocked because a
typeahead row sat under the fixed environment banner: menus were positioned once and never followed
their box. `placeMenu` now opens upward when needed, stays below the banner, follows on scroll and
closes when the box leaves the view; verified with the banner shown (PO item suggestion clicks).


## Stage 58 — Sticker Studio: a full label designer with exact-look printing (opened + built 2026-10-07; released 2026-10-09 in `f6e3961`; only 58.7 open)

Opened from the user's request to build sticker printing "with this sort of flexibility … best module ever and better than this", with a screenshot of another ERP's label designer (92 × 12 jewellery tag: size presets, DPI, stickers/row, gaps, roll/media, font family, zoom, snapping, multi-select, QR, rotated text). User decisions (2026-10-07): **printers = TSC (TSPL), Zebra (ZPL) and A4/laser sheets**; **text = exact look** (rendered at printer DPI, any font) — *"but don't steal the UI. Make it better/best UI possible and not similar UI"*; silent printing goes through the print agent, which may be our own PrintBridge (`../PrintBridge/agent/`, QZ wire protocol) rather than QZ Tray — no QZ-specific dependency added.

- [x] **58.1 Rendering engine** — new `public/sticker-engine.js`, the one renderer for studio canvas, thumbnails, browser print and thermal payloads. Draws every element on a canvas at the printer's DPI, thresholds to 1-bit, and emits a TSPL `BITMAP` (inverted bits, `SIZE`/`GAP`/`DIRECTION`/`DENSITY`/`SPEED`) or ZPL `^GFA` with standard ASCII compression (`^PW`/`^LL`/`^PON|^POI`/`~SD`/`^PR`/`^PQ`). Identical consecutive rows collapse into one image with a copy count; jobs go in 50-row batches (PrintBridge/QZ cap one WebSocket message at 32 MB). Browser path: PNGs at ≥300 dpi laid out as roll pages (CSS named `@page` per template, so one job mixes sizes) or an A4 sheet grid. Hand-written encoders, no library: **Code 128** (auto B/C subset — a 13-digit value is 123 modules vs 178 in pure B), **EAN-13**, **QR** (byte mode, v1-40, L/M/Q/H, mask penalty). Bars/modules are whole printer dots. **Verified**: Code 128 — 20 edge-case values decode exactly with ZXing; EAN-13 — 7 values incl. the ERP's own dated `0200…` codes decode with ZXing; QR — all 160 version × ECC combinations at max capacity + Unicode (₹, Devanagari) decode (159 with jsQR; v23-L, which jsQR alone rejects, decodes with ZXing); ZPL compression — 400 random/structured bitmaps round-trip through an independent spec decompressor.
- [x] **58.2 Sticker Studio** — new `public/sticker-studio.js` + `.stk-*` styles: full-screen workspace (own layout, not a copy): top bar (inline name, size chip, undo/redo, **Preview with** a real Item via typeahead, **Test print**, **Save**), icon **Insert** rail (Text, Data, Barcode, QR, Line, Box, Logo), searchable **Data fields** drawer (every label field + every Item field from `/api/v1/doc/Item/meta`; click or drag onto the label), stage with mm rulers, multi-up ghost labels, 1 mm/5 mm grid, smart guides + 0.5 mm grid snap (Alt bypasses), marquee and Shift/Ctrl multi-select, 8-handle resize, floating quick bar (rotate/duplicate/front/back/lock/delete) parked outside the label, bottom dock (zoom, fit, 1:1, Grid, Snap, **Printer view** = exact dots, align/distribute), inspector tabs **Element** (field binding, prefix/suffix, decimals, Indian digit grouping, custom text with `{field}` tokens + Insert field, font family, size mm↔pt, B/I/AA toggles, align/valign, Shrink/Wrap/Cut off, rotation 0/90/180/270, white-on-black, barcode/QR options with live **scan check**, exact X/Y/W/H, centre/full-width), **Label** (12 presets incl. 4 jewellery tail tags and multi-up, W/H, DPI, across/gaps/roll width, shift/darkness/speed/rotate-180, default font, A4-sheet layout with labels-per-page, categories/default/status/code), **Layers** (order, lock, hide); status line (cursor mm, selection, warnings: off-label, unscannable, layout size). Keyboard: arrows 0.1/1 mm, R, Ctrl+D/C/V/Z/Shift+Z/Y/A/S, Del, Esc, +/−/0, G, P. 100-step undo. New-template starter with preset cards. Templates tab is now a card gallery with live thumbnails, Edit/Duplicate and a filter. Codes made from the name and de-duplicated (verified: a second save of the same name became `-2`, not an overwrite).
- [x] **58.3 Any Item field on a label** — `engines.StickerLabel.Fields` (every scalar Item field, ≤500 chars) set in `resolveAndLogSticker`; `StickerFieldText` falls back to it, and now also understands `custom` text with `{field}` tokens and `prefix`/`suffix` (`StickerElement.Kind/Prefix/Suffix`). **Blank-value rule (both renderers)**: a field-bound element with a blank value is left off entirely, and a custom text whose *every* `{field}` is blank prints nothing (no stray "W: gm"). The server-side ZPL path (API clients of the QZ payload endpoint) skips QR/line/box/image kinds instead of misprinting them. Tests: `TestStickerItemFieldsAndFieldFallback`, `TestZPLStickerElementStage58Kinds`, Fields assertion in `TestPrintStickersBarcodeSVG`.
- [x] **58.4 Print flows rewired** — Print tab (manual SKUs and GRN/Transfer document lines) now: `POST /api/v1/stickers/print` (validation, DEVICE-0298 printer check, `sticker_print_log`) → `printStickerRun` → engine: silent TSPL/ZPL through the print agent when the Printer record has a raw language + OS Printer Name and the agent is reachable (job logged to `print_job_log`), else the browser print dialog. **Bug fixed on the way**: a TSPL (TSC) printer used to receive ZPL from `BuildStickerPayload`; the built-in default label is now drawn by the engine too, so it prints on TSC. Old designer code removed from `view-printing.js`/`app.js`; `VIEW_MODULE_VERSION`/`app.js?v` → 34, `styles.css?v` → 27; studio and engine are imported with the view module's own `?v=` so a deploy refreshes them together. No new route, no migration (settings/logos ride in an extra `design` key on the StickerTemplate document; `elements` keeps the Stage-52 shape so old templates open and print unchanged).
- [x] **58.5 Live verification** (scratch server :8147 against the local DB, Playwright + Chrome). Found and fixed during it: (a) the studio's `data-act` buttons collided with the app-wide `[data-act]` dispatcher ("no such action function: drawer") — renamed `data-sa`; (b) the environment banner covered the studio's top bar — studio now starts below it; (c) the grid auto-placed the stage into the closed drawer's column — explicit grid columns; (d) a drag never moved because selecting re-renders the overlay node holding pointer capture — tracking moved to `window`; (e) shortcuts were swallowed because the name box kept focus and the dialog guard matched the app's always-present closed `.modal-overlay` nodes — canvas clicks release field focus, guard checks `.modal-overlay.open`/`#custom-dialog-container:not(.hidden)`; (f) clicking one element of a multi-selection could not narrow it; (g) rotating a long text sideways on a 12 mm tag pushed it off the label — now kept inside; (h) gallery thumbnails showed "W: gm" — blank-value rule + sample values. Final runs: drag with snap guide, undo/redo exact, Shift+→ = 1 mm, marquee 5/5, align tools, resize, save/reopen, 92×12 at 300 dpi = 1087×142-dot TSPL/ZPL with `PRINT 1,3`/`^PQ3`, browser sheet `@page` sizes per template, Print tab manual run → sheet (2 pages for 2 copies) and → stubbed agent (TSPL base64 to `TSC TE310`, `Submitted/TSPL` logged, history rows written); light + dark themes screenshotted; zero page errors. Fixtures (4 templates, 1 printer, 3 sticker-log + 1 job-log rows, showcase template) deleted afterwards. `go build ./...`, `go vet ./engines/`, `go test ./engines/ -run 'Sticker|ZPL|QZ'` green.
- [x] **58.6 Guides** — USER_GUIDE §7A.2 rewritten as a full studio walkthrough (+3 "How do I…" rows, intro note on TSPL/ZPL Printer fields, glossary); ADMIN_GUIDE §B.3.4 updated (rendering, DPI precedence, Item fields, API-client ZPL path, storage limits, test prints not logged; the "byte-identical with no template" claim corrected); QZ_PRINTING_SETUP.md template section replaced. `go run ./cmd/doclint -root .` — no new findings (KB generated-drift warnings pre-date this stage).
- [ ] **58.7 Physical printer check** — not possible on this machine (no TSC/Zebra attached; same gap as 31.1.8). On the first real run confirm: TSPL bitmap polarity (TSPL prints a 0 bit; the engine inverts) and `DIRECTION 1` orientation — if a tag comes out upside down, tick **Rotate the print 180°** on the template; check a 203-dpi Code 128 scans at the chosen width. **[needs user: one test print on the shop's TSC/Zebra]**

## Stage 66 — Smart HSN and GST rate suggestions; no number spinners anywhere (opened + built 2026-10-17 from the user's New Item screenshot)

User, 2026-10-17, on the empty HSN box of New Item: *"Need to suggest HSN for the Industry. Need to make smart. Once Industry selected load those hsn. If silver not silver from item name, Cloth, fabric.. All aspect it should be smart to suggest. User will select the correct one from those recommendations or if not suggest, Type and Add. But we will have a master hsn list present"*, then *"Same goes for gst rate"*, *"hsn rate will be correlated"*, and *"No number updown in numbers anywhere. It is a thumbrule for all UI. But number section will accept number that is correct."*

- [x] **66.1 HSN master list + ranked suggestions.** `engines/hsn_master.json` (embedded via `go:embed`): 290 HSN/SAC codes across all ten industry profiles, each with a description, its GST rate(s) (GST 2.0, in force from 22 Sep 2025; price-banded codes carry both rates with notes, e.g. garments/made-ups/footwear 5% up to Rs 2,500 a piece, 18% above), the industries it belongs to, **keywords** (decide *which* code: the material or the exact product, score 3), **related** words (what *kind* of thing, score 1) and **exclusions** ("german silver", "gold plated" are not precious metal). Hindi retail words included (haar, chandi, kangan, jhumka, dahi, sabzi…); spellings folded (aluminum, jewelry, t-shirt, tyre/tire). Rates checked against the 56th GST Council summary; codes whose rate could not be confirmed (2201 water, 7103 loose stones, clay bricks, concrete blocks, organic manure, wooden joinery) ship **without** a rate rather than a guessed one. `engines/hsn_suggest.go` `SuggestHSN` ranks: the client's own Items/Item Names sharing words with the name (4 per shared word, "used on your item …"), the master list (+1 in the client's industry), HSN-list description matches; agreement between sources adds one; weak matches under a third of the best are dropped. With no name: the client's HSN list, then **Common in <industry>**. Typing searches everything (digits by code prefix, words by description/keywords). `GET /api/v1/hsn/suggest?name=&q=&hsn=` (`internal/server/handlers_hsn.go`; gated on HSNCode read, item names quoted only for Item / ItemName the role can read; route classified `doc.default`; attack surface 521 routes). `EnsureHSNCode` now stores the master description for a code learned from a saved Item.
- [x] **66.2 Choosing the industry loads its HSN codes.** `SwitchIndustryProfile` seeds the industry's master codes into the client's HSN Code list inside its transaction (`seedIndustryHSNCodes`; `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, so a description/rate the client set is never touched; single-rate codes get it as Default GST Rate, price-banded ones none). Clients whose industry was set before Stage 66 are not back-filled: their HSN box still shows **Common in <industry>** from the master list, only the HSN Code list page lacks those rows until they are used or the industry is overridden.
- [x] **66.3 GST rate follows the HSN.** Picking a code fills the rate when the code has one rate and the box is empty or was filled by the picker; a price-banded code clears a picker-filled rate so the user chooses; a typed/clicked rate is never overwritten. Under the GST box: the code's own rates as buttons (with when each applies), then 0 / 3 / 5 / 18 / 40. A client's HSN-list rate wins and is listed first as *Set on your HSN list* (so a stale pre-GST-2.0 12% is visible, not hidden). Picker lives in `public/view-pickers.js` (`attachSmartHSNPicker`, CSS injected with the module); `app.js` `attachHSNPicker` is a stub that loads it, plus a generic `opts.source` / `opts.titleFn` on `attachTypeahead`. Applies to every form with an HSN box (Item, Item Name, design), full form and quick-create.
- [x] **66.4 0% needs a Tax Treatment, asked in quick-create.** MASTER-0044 rejects a 0% Item without Exempt / Nil-Rated / Zero-Rated, and the New Item quick-create never asked it - a dead end for produce. The Tax Treatment choice now appears there the moment the rate is 0 and is required and sent only then.
- [x] **66.5 No number spinners anywhere (user thumb rule).** `styles.css` hides the spin buttons on every `input[type=number]`; `app.js` `installNumberStepGuards()` (installed once with the date picker enhancer) blurs a focused number box on wheel and cancels ArrowUp/ArrowDown stepping (screens' own arrow-key handlers still run). Boxes stay `type="number"`, so only a valid number is accepted.

Reproduced in Chrome on a scratch server (Edit Item, HSN 6109): clicking the box or selecting its text opened no list, and deleting the code closed the list and left it closed - only typing searched. Cause: the shared picker (`app.js` `attachTypeahead`, `showAllOnFocus`) browses only when an *empty* box gets focus (so `pick()`'s own focus and tabbing do not reopen it), and an emptied box closed the list instead of browsing. User, 2026-10-17, sharing `ERP_Standard_Transaction_Print_Formats.docx` (16 document types, header / line / totals / signature / audit spec, GST-aware): *"refer this and update ... adopt, which field not required discard that. Adopted for every document the ERP already holds; fields it does not hold are left out (listed in 71.6); modules it does not have are listed in 71.7 for the user to choose.

- [x] **71.1 Print data** (`engines/print_documents.go`). Every print: `fields.financial_year` (April-March), `fields.currency`, `audit` (`created_by/at` from the document row, `approved_by/at` from the last `approval_log` Approved row, user names resolved). Lines: `item_code` (SKU / barcode), `description` (name · variant values), `variant`, `design` (`parent_product_code`). GRN: `po_qty` (from the PO lines), `qc_status`, `variance_reason`. Transfers: `requested_qty`, `shipped_qty`, and on the receipt `received_qty` / `short_qty` / `reason`, plus `receipt_status`, `value_basis`, `stock_status_after`. **Bug found and fixed:** Transfer In printed its received qty as "Sent" (it read `received_items` for both), so a short line looked complete - shipped now comes from `dispatched_items`. Notes: `original_taxable`, `original_invoice_no/date`, `place_of_supply`, `document_direction`. Totals sum the new qty columns.

Migration `db/migrations_stage71_1_print_formats.sql` adds the four to `PrintTemplate.transaction` options (only the shipped option list is replaced). Print buttons: Purchase Return list, customer credit notes, Returns workbench (`view-returns.js` now loads `trade-docs.js`); the GRN print window offers **Vendor Copy** under Print as.
- [x] **71.3 Standard layouts** (`public/trade-docs.js` `defaultLayout`): common header (brand, title, no., date, financial year, status), store + party boxes, reference lines per document that drop out when blank, the pack's columns per document (`TXN_COLUMNS`), totals per document (`TXN_TOTALS` - GRN / Vendor Copy / Transfer In quantity-only, no tax summary or words), the pack's three signature lines (`TXN_SIGNATURES`), the vendor-copy disclaimer, audit line (created / approved / printed by + when; printed by + at added in `printContext`). Purpose boxes, logo, copy labels and the explanatory rules of the pack are not printed. Saved templates of a tenant are untouched and still take precedence.
- [x] **71.4 Designer catalogue** (`engines/print_templates.go`): new header fields (financial year, currency, audit, per-document references) and line fields (item / barcode, description, variant, design, PO qty, QC status, reasons, shipped / received / short, return qty, condition, original discount), new qty totals - all draggable in the Print Template Studio.
- [x] **71.5 Guides.** USER_GUIDE §7B.1 (what prints where, the per-document table, vendor copy / GRN / transfer / sales return notes), §7B.2 list; ADMIN_GUIDE §B.3.7 (eleven transactions, standard-layout rules, vendor-copy stripping, transfer-in fix).
- [x] **71.6 Fields in the pack left out (the ERP does not hold them):** PO payment / delivery terms, expected delivery, buyer department, vendor quotation ref, freight, tolerance, partial delivery, validity, special instructions, line discount; GRN vendor challan (separate from invoice), vehicle / LR / AWB, packages, receipt type, stock status, bin per line, QC reference, barcode count; transfer transit location, vehicle, e-way ref, packages, gross weight, package condition, excess / damaged at receipt; cess and IRN / QR on notes; company logo, copy labels, print version and reprint reason. Each becomes printable once its module item in 71.7 exists (the print payload already carries every header field).
- [ ] **71.7 Modules the pack needs that the ERP does not have** - **now tracked as Stage 74** (2026-10-17: the user's *Transaction Modules Master BRD* specifies all eleven below; build order = its waves, open decisions = 74.0.1). Kept here as the original list:
  - (a) **Delivery Challan** - standalone goods movement without an invoice (job work, sample, repair, exhibition), returnable / non-returnable with expected return, consignor / consignee / place of supply, copy labels Original / Duplicate / Triplicate. Today only a same-GSTIN Transfer Out prints as a challan.
  - (b) **Customer Advance Receipt** - own number series, tender + payment ref, balance, later redemption against a sale with the advance referenced. Nothing exists (POS tenders are Cash / Card / UPI).
  - (c) **Internal Material Issue / Receipt** - consumables / packaging to a department or cost centre with a stock movement and cost posting. Only production material issue exists.
  - (d) **Inventory Adjustment** as its own document - one-off gain / loss / damage / write-off with a reason code, maker-checker, value impact, GL posting and reversal. Today adjustments come only from cycle counts.
  - (e) **Job Work Outward / Return** - upgrade `SubcontractOrder` (one SKU, one receipt) to many lines, gross / net weight, partial returns, balance with the job worker, loss / variance approval, challan print.
  - (f) **Store credit / credit voucher** - a sales return refunded as credit the customer redeems later. Today every refund books cash.
  - (g) **E-invoice (IRN, signed QR) and e-way bill** - fields plus the NIC / GSP integration. Deliberately not built so far.
  - (h) **Document print log** - print version, reprint count and reprint reason per document print (stickers already log reprints).
  - (i) **Reason codes everywhere** - the `ReasonCode` master exists, but GRN rejection / shortage, purchase return, transfer discrepancy and reprint take free text.
  - (j) **PO commercial terms** and **GRN logistics fields** (71.6 lists them) on the PO and GRN screens.
  - (k) **Print profiles** - company logo setting, copy labels, template-driven POS receipt profiles (B2C / B2B / exchange / gift); Sales Invoice customer GSTIN / place of supply and a real entry screen.
- [x] **71.8 Multi-page prints: header on the first page only, tax / totals / signatures at the end** (user, 2026-10-17: *"if I have two pages, the header should not come and bottom of text tax part and all should come in the end ... header should not come except 1st page"*). Reproduced first in a renderer harness: the standard layouts printed brand / title / number / divider on **every** page (`pages:'all'`), and a template saved from the studio printed a terms line and rule added under the table on **page 1** (palette items default to "First page"); page 1 also stopped at the footer line (218 mm) leaving ~70 mm blank. Fixed once in the renderer (`public/trade-docs.js`), so it covers the built-in layouts **and every saved template** without touching stored layouts: with an items table, **position decides** - `pagesByPosition` / `printsOn` put an element whose centre is above the table on the first page only and one below it on the last page only; a page number and anything beside the table keep their own **Prints on**. `tableBox` gives later pages the top margin and pages before the last the bottom margin (2 mm clear of a page number); `paginate` fills each page and moves to the last page once the rows left fit above its footer, handing one row on when a page would otherwise take every row and leave the totals alone. Column headings still repeat on every page. The auto-added brand prints on the first page only. `contY` ("Later pages start at Y") is retired - the input is removed from the studio, stored values are ignored. Studio: the canvas paints the one design page with `renderPages(..., { design: true })` (every element, the designed box), element tags show the effective page, and **Prints on** is shown disabled ("First page (above the items table)" / "Last page (below the items table)") where position decides; hint text updated. USER_GUIDE §7B.2 steps 2 and 4, ADMIN_GUIDE print section (brand, new **Multi-page documents** note). **Verified:** `node --check` both files; harness (Chrome / Playwright over `public/`, before vs after): PO standard layout at 5 / 30 / 38 / 80 rows and a studio-style template at 30 rows - after the fix page 2+ carry no brand / title / number / divider, the table starts at 10 mm, pages before the last run to 286 mm (38 rows on page 1 instead of 25), the tax summary, totals, words, signatures, audit line and the studio-added terms / rule print on the last page only, page number on every page, a 5-row PO is unchanged on one page. **Live** (scratch :8177, local DB on 5490, print data stretched to 40 lines by route interception, no data written): QA-PO-002 printed 3 pages through the real print dialog with exactly that split; **Design templates** opened the studio with the full design page (header, table, totals), tags "first page" / "last page" / "every page", **Prints on** disabled for brand and totals, editable for the page number, no "Later pages start at Y"; no page or console errors. **Not committed, not deployed** (frontend only - no migration).

## Stage 72 — Item & Material Master: independent classification, sales-triggered backflush at the POS, GWP, gift cards as stored value (opened + built 2026-10-17 from the user's two PDFs)

**Why it was opened.** The user shared *Backflush vs Non-Sellable Items in Retail ERP/POS* (10 pp.) with *"Build this also for pos"* - Stage 60.4's Backflush item type only consumed components at production completion. Asked whether gift cards were in scope, they answered *"Wait. I will reshare"* and then sent the full 41-page *Enterprise Item & Material Master Module - Build Blueprint* (v1.0, 17 Oct 2026, built from the first PDF): *"Read and update checklist and build"*. Its one rule: *"Do not build Asset, Non-Asset, Gift, Backflush, Voucher and Sellable as one mutually exclusive item-type list"* - every item answers six independent questions (class, stock tracking, valuation, sellability, consumption, accounting); behaviour comes from profiles + rule tables, never from a named item.

**Decisions.** User, 2026-10-17: **short packaging stock never blocks the sale** - the default short-stock action is *Allow and report* (Block / Warn stay selectable per rule, blueprint UAT-10). Taken without asking (reversible, say if wrong): the Stage 60 **Item Type master is the blueprint's reusable profile** (section 5, "many items -> 1 profile") rather than six new profile masters, with item-level overrides - one place to configure, the existing pattern; the seven Stage 60 types keep today's behaviour except **Gift and Free With Item become Promo only** (the till could not bill a zero-priced line before this stage anyway - see 72.2); a gift's cost goes to **5170 Promotional / GWP Expense**, not COGS; gift cards are issued as their **own operation with a separate receipt**, not as a bill line (blueprint 16.1 "Separate issue receipt").

**What already existed (gap analysis, 2026-10-17).** Item Type master with barcode / batch / sticker rules and four behaviour flags (60.3/60.4); Item status = generic `documents.status`, soft delete, no Item approval rule; per-key `audit_logs` trigger (records the creator, no reason); `expected_version` optimistic concurrency (409); moving-average costing only; no negative-stock setting; no service / non-stock items; `ProductBundle` Virtual / Stocked kits exploded on OMS sales orders only; GRN -> Draft Asset per unit for Fixed Asset items (57.8); no stored value (`Voucher` is a coupon), one payment mode per bill; no void of a posted bill (returns only); offers: % / flat / BxGy / bundle price, no GWP; Location.sellable only, no item x location policy.

- [x] **72.1 Eight independent dimensions on the Item Type profile, item overrides, compatibility rules** (blueprint 3, 5, 6, 15.2; FR-001/002/022; ITM-005..009). `engines/item_policy.go`: commercial class, stock tracking, valuation, accounting treatment, sellability, customer bill, POS capture, consumption + expense account; precedence Item > Item Type > what the Stage 60 flags meant > company default (ordinary merchandise), each dimension's source reported. `db/migrations_stage72_1_item_policy.sql`: the nine fields on ItemType and (with "Item type default") on Item; GL 5160 Packaging Consumption, 5170 Promotional / GWP Expense, 5180 Consumables Expense, 2610 Gift Card / Stored Value Liability; dimensions filled on the seven Stage 60 types only where blank; new profiles **Packaging, Consumable - Periodic, Service, Non-Stock Expense, Gift Card, Virtual Kit**. Save-time rules through `ValidateMasterDataRules` (UI, import and API alike): MASTER-0401 valuated needs inventory asset, 0402 tracking vs valuation, 0403 fixed-asset accounting only on Fixed Asset, 0404 stored value <-> issue-only + liability, 0405 auto/periodic consumption needs stock, 0406 expense account must exist. Production backflush now also follows `consumption = Auto backflush`.
- [x] **72.2 Sellability at the till** (14.1, FR-013/021, ITM-003/004, UAT-14/28). In `ResolvePOSQuote` (screen, checkout and price override all price through it): Not sellable -> POSOFF-0411, stored-value item -> POSOFF-0413, Promo only -> billed at 0 and allowed only up to what a Gift With Purchase offer (or its Free-With-Item parent) authorises, else POSOFF-0412. Each refused attempt logged once per cart as an Item Exception (Logged). Not-tracked lines flagged `not_stocked`; hidden-bill zero-value lines kept off both receipts (browser + ESC/POS). **Bug found and fixed:** the POS screen refused every line priced 0 ("price greater than zero"), so Stage 60.4's Gift / Free With Item could never actually be billed.
- [x] **72.3 Backflush rules and sale-triggered consumption** (9.1-9.3, FR-004/008/010, UAT-07/26). `BackflushRule` master (Setup -> Inventory, numbered BF-0001): applies to Item / Attribute (Name:Value) / Category / Item Type / All items, store (blank = all), consumed item, qty per unit, group, priority, cashier may change, reason needed, None allowed, short-stock action, effective dates. `engines/backflush_rules.go`: winner per group by 9.2 specificity (item, attribute, category, item type, all; a store's rule before the all-stores one; priority breaks ties); groups add up; only normally-sold lines trigger. Checkout resolves it from the server's own priced lines, stores it on the POSCart with rule id + version, and `finalizePOSCheckoutTx` posts it in the sale's transaction: stock out (voucher `POSConsumption`, the bill number), valuated components Dr expense / Cr 1200 at moving-average cost, non-valuated quantity only. A replay posts nothing twice. MASTER-0408 on an incomplete rule.
- [x] **72.4 POS Packaging override** (9.4, FR-009, UAT-08/09, ITM-011). The quote returns each line's default and choices; **Packaging** button (shown only when something can change) opens the shared dialog: per line x group a choice (+ None where allowed) and a reason; overrides validated server-side (POSOFF-0414 reason, POSOFF-0415 not an option). Verified live (see Verification).

## Stage 73 — Enterprise suite completion blueprint: the 346-row gap register mapped onto the build (external PDF; opened 2026-10-17 · Waves 1-3 and 73.J built 2026-10-17/18, released 2026-10-18 (73.R) · pending list below)

User, 2026-10-17: *"read and plan and update in checklist"*, sharing `Enterprise_Business_Platform_Suite_Completion_Blueprint.pdf` (v1.0, 41 pages, outside the repo, in the user's own documents (`MyBusiness/IT Solution/ERP/Docs/PDF`)). It benchmarks the suite against SAP S/4HANA and Dynamics 365 process areas: §1 assessment and product boundaries (PIM, OMS, WMS, HRMS, CRM/Loyalty and POS as first-class products, ERP as the financial backbone), §3 fifteen end-to-end processes, §4 ten design principles, §5 the P0/P1 go-live gaps, §6 the loyalty target design, §7 twenty-seven module blueprints each with a completion gate, §8 twelve policy decisions (D01-D12), §9 seven delivery waves, §10 ten acceptance journeys (J1-J10), §11 the Definition of Done, §12 the minimum configuration catalogue, §13 a leadership scorecard, and §14 a 346-row capability register (167 Built-verify, 85 Partial, 77 Missing / benchmark gap, 14 External gate, 3 Optional / scale-gated).

**Pending (2026-10-18) - everything else in this stage is `[x]`. 55 open items, grouped by who acts:**
- **Your decisions:** 73.0.1 confirm D04 / D05 / D06 as built (D10 when SSO comes up); 73.0.2 capability register - all 346 rows or the 179; 73.B.1 fractional BOM quantity at material issue (round up / round / refuse); 73.C.11 risky-rights flags on Stage 79's Roles screen; 73.D.1 depreciation dated at month-end (built that way - confirm); 73.D.2 whether an employee may read back their own grievance; 73.4.1-73.4.10 Wave 4 scope (nothing started).
- **You run:** nothing - 73.D.3 was regenerated at the 2026-10-18 release (73.R).
- **Fixes to build, highest risk first:** stock 73.B.8, 73.B.6; smaller 73.B.7, 73.B.10, 73.C.8, 73.C.12, 73.D.18, 73.D.19, 73.D.23, 73.D.24; tests 73.B.9, 73.D.21, 73.C.9.
- **Release steps:** all done at the 2026-10-18 release (73.R): 73.C.6 → 73.C.2 → 73.B.5 → 73.D.5 → 73.C.3 → 73.C.4 → 73.B.4.
- **On site / sign-offs:** 73.B.11 RF scanner floor run; 73.C.13 Stage 55 Done-bar walks; 55.10 Account Head + CA review and 55.14 HR policy-owner sign-off (73.D's header).
- **Dev data cleanup (ask first):** 73.B.13, 73.C.5.

**How this stage is built (read before adding to it):**
- **An index, not a copy.** The PDF's own backlog rule: *"every implementation ticket should reference one capability row ... do not create another parallel master checklist"*. A row an open item already owns points there (→ 59.3); only rows nobody owns get a 73.x item. Its wave order (§9) is the build order: *"do not start a new breadth module while P0 economic correctness is still open"*.
- **Every Partial / Missing claim was re-checked against source on 2026-10-17**, because the PDF's evidence was older docs (handover, an older copy of this file, the capability catalogue). Two read-only sweeps, ~160 claims, file:line evidence in each item; the two P0 claims that open new items on their own (73.1.1, 73.1.9) were also read directly. Where the PDF is wrong: 73.0.3.
- **The 167 Built-verify rows are not listed one by one.** Their acceptance tests run through the journeys (73.J) and the existing 47.17.1 / 50.17 / 26.11.5 work; they are proven, not rebuilt.
- **Missing / benchmark-gap rows are scope decisions, not a build order** (standing rule: no new breadth without the user's go-ahead). Wave 4 lists them as input to 50.18's domain-expansion decision.
- **Numbered 73, not 72:** a concurrent session's **Stage 72** (the user's Item & Material Master blueprint - item policy dimensions, backflush rules, consumption entry, item exception queue, stored-value gift cards / vouchers; `db/migrations_stage72_*.sql`) had no section here when this stage opened; it has one now and shipped in 0845470. Where that work answers a row (gift cards, item taxonomy, a data-quality queue) this stage points at it rather than duplicating it.
- **Acceptance bar for every 73.x item is the PDF's §11 Definition of Done:** normal, partial, cancel, retry and reversal paths work; stock, tax, money and points post exactly once; rules are enforced server-side; lineage and audit exist; an authorised novice can finish the task; reports reconcile; the guides describe what shipped.

### 73.0 Wave 0 — decisions and baseline

- [ ] **73.0.1 Policy decisions D01-D12** **All decided: the user approved every recommended default on 2026-10-18 ("All approved which are recommended for me") - D04, D05, D06 confirmed as built; D10 = local identity, no SSO / SCIM until a target customer requires it.** PDF §8: building without them hard-codes accidental behaviour. The PDF's recommended default is in brackets, followed by where things stand today:
  - **D01 Loyalty on return** [reverse earned points pro-rata on the returned eligible value] - blocks 73.1.2. **Decided by the user 2026-10-17: the default above.**
  - **D02 Loyalty refund** [the redeemed share goes back as points at the original allocation; money only for the money-funded share; no cash conversion] - blocks 73.1.3. **Decided by the user 2026-10-17: the default above.**
  - **D03 Negative points** when the earned points are already spent [business call: allow a negative / recovery balance, or cap at zero and flag the debt] - blocks 73.1.2. **Decided by the user 2026-10-17: allow a negative balance, paid back by later earnings; the cash refund is never cut (built in 73.1.2).**
  - **D04 Transfer approval** [independent maker-checker, or direct approval for low-risk internal transfers] - blocks 73.1.7. **Built to this default in 73.1.7** (direct approval below `inventory.transfer_direct_approval_limit`, maker-checker above); no user confirmation recorded - confirm.
  - **D05 Rejected GRN quantity** [stays open on the PO for replacement, or counts as fulfilled with a claim / debit note] - blocks 73.2.4. **Built in 73.2.4:** rejected / damaged units stay open on the PO for replacement; short-close needs a reason and raises a claim. No user confirmation recorded - confirm.
  - **D06 Vendor quote basis** [unit / line / total price; UOM, tax, freight, lead time and award fields] - blocks 73.2.2. **Built in 73.2.2:** per-line unit price, quoted UOM converted to the RFQ unit, tax %, freight, terms and validity, compared on landed total. No user confirmation recorded - confirm.
  - **D07 Mixed-owner WMS** - **decided 2026-10-08: full isolation, built under Stage 59** (59.1-59.6 all closed; archiving the stage is 73.B.4).
  - **D08 Gift card / store credit** [separate liabilities, refund rules, expiry, transferability] - gift cards built in 72.8 (deployed, 0845470); store credit 71.7(f); refund-to-card and transferability rules are open as 72.D(g).
  - **D09 GL correction policy** [which posted documents may be reversed, reopened or adjusted; who may override a closed period] - today a closed period has no reopen, by design (`engines/accounting_periods.go`); blocks 73.1.10. **Decided 2026-10-17 (user): the Accountant role approves a reopen; built in 73.1.10.**
  - **D10 SSO / SCIM** [only if target enterprise customers require it, local identity otherwise] - go-to-market call; 73.4.9.
  - **D11 Data mart / read replica** - already gated by 26.10.6's measured thresholds.
  - **D12 Hosting / WAF / DR** - partly decided (Stage 56, Railway Pro, 2026-10-04); WAF 26.1.3b, alerts 20.2 and hosting 20.4 still open.
- [ ] **73.0.2 One capability register, not two.** `docs/product/capability-register.json` (schema v3) is module-level - 14 `CAP-*` entries with maturity and configurations, generated into `docs/generated/capability-catalog.md` - and 47.17.5 asks for the maturity registry. Proposal: carry the PDF's rows as a `checks` array under each `CAP-*` (capability, status, priority, acceptance test, evidence, checklist ref), generated into the catalogue, so a row moves to Verified in one place. **[needs design decision: user - import all 346 rows, or only the 179 that are not Built-verify]**
- **73.0.3 Re-verification against source (2026-10-17): where the PDF's status column is wrong.** Use this, not the PDF's column:
  - **Already built (the PDF says Missing / Partial):** WMS cross-docking (Stage 26.5; `routes.go:470-472`, `wms_putaway_ext.go:38`); POS price authority (47.2; `ResolvePOSQuote` `handlers_pim_pos_finance.go:554`, capability-gated override `:1082` - only the tenant opt-in "assisted" mode is left); setup advisor (`setup_advisor.go:44`, closed 41.x); PIM supplier isolation (`pim_supplier_portal.go:32`, enforced in the generic doc handler, tested); PIM localisation (26.4.1, `pim_translation.go`); BOM effective dates, alternate BOM, by-products and the final QC gate (26.9.x; `manufacturing_mrp.go:110-134, 406-421`); return inspection disposition (`returns.go:538`); asset capitalisation from a GRN (57.8, `asset_items.go:78`); employee loans (`hr_payroll.go:261-336`); audit archive restore + re-verify (47.7.6, `audit_archive.go:807`).
  - **Further along than the PDF says:** exchange (same-value SKU swap in Returns QC, `returns.go:538`); cycle count (blind count, recount, maker-checker, posted once - `wms.go:472-576`); marketplace settlement (commission, fees, TDS, TCS and net payout posted to GL with variance, dispute and write-off - `settlement_reconciliation.go`); manufacturing operation confirmation, scrap and rework (logged, 26.9.3-26.9.6); statutory payroll (PF, ESI, PT, flat TDS - `hr_payroll.go:48-96`); onboarding / offboarding checklists and the user disabled when the employee goes Inactive (26.8.6, `hr.go:15`); customer merge (`crm_analytics.go:58`); appraisal, training and grievance (26.8.7); per-tenant feature flags (`saas.go:30`, no canary).
  - **Worse than the PDF says:** transfers carry no batch or serial at all (`transfer_orders.go:12`, a line is `{sku, qty}`); there is no split tender (one `payment_mode`), and every refund - including one labelled "Store Credit" - posts to cash 1100 (`returns.go:867`); manual point adjustment does not exist (ledger types Earn / Burn only); RFQ has no item or SKU field (description + quantity only); expense advances are explicitly out of scope (`expense.go:101-106`); there are no people analytics beyond an attendance summary; and a return refund ignores bill-level discounts (73.1.1).

### 73.1 Wave 1 — P0 economic correctness (first)

- [x] **73.1.1 A POS return appears to refund the full line price, ignoring the bill-level offer discount and redeemed points: reproduce, then fix.** `resolveOriginalSaleLinePrices` (`engines/returns.go:86-130`) refunds each line at its stored `sale_price`. The offer discount is bill-level (`pos_quote.go:389-396`, `Total = Subtotal - OfferDiscount`) and so is loyalty redemption (`pos_checkout.go:234-256`); neither is stored per line, and `returns.go` reads neither. Returning one line of a discounted or points-funded bill therefore likely over-refunds, in cash. **First** a failing test (offer bill + redeemed points, return one line, assert refund = that line's net share). **Then** persist the per-line allocation on the sale at checkout - offer share, points and their money value, tender share, a deterministic rounding remainder (PDF §6 "Partial return allocation", §6.1 step 2) - and refund from it. Foundation for 73.1.2, 73.1.3 and 73.1.6. [rows: Sale return P0, Partial return allocation P0] **Built 2026-10-17 (Batch A) - and the sale side was worse than this item says:** the offer discount never reached the GL or the tax. Every offer sale credited 4100 and debited cash at the list total and backed GST out of the list price (reproduced: 3,000 revenue / 2,730 cash / 457.62 GST on a bill that collected 2,430). Now `EvaluatePOSOffers` splits each offer across its lines (`LineDiscounts`, largest remainder by what is left of each line); the quote carries `offer_share` / `net_total` per line and backs GST out of the net; finalize posts the net (`PostSalesFinanceBookingTx` accepts a wholly free bill), caps points at the net in points not rupees, earns on the money paid, and stores the per-line `allocation` (offer, points, gift card, each money tender) on the cart (`engines/sale_allocation.go`). A return prices its units from that allocation under the sale's lock, cumulatively (`unitShare`; the net is the sum of the tender parts so a refund journal always balances), so a line returned in pieces refunds exactly what it collected; a bill sold before this rebuilds its allocation from what it recorded. **Also fixed:** `RedeemLoyaltyPointsTx` ran `FOR UPDATE` on an aggregate, so every till sale that redeemed points failed (Postgres 0A000) - now a per-customer transaction advisory lock (`lockedLoyaltyBalanceTx`). Receipt prints the posted points; POS Sale Reconciliation holds sales to the net, with tenders measured net. **Historic sales are not re-posted** (release data-safety rule): past offer sales will now read BROKEN in POS Sale Reconciliation, which is accurate - finance decides whether to journal the difference. Tests `TestStage73POSReturnAllocation`, `TestStage73AllocationArithmetic`, `TestStage73RedemptionCapReturnsPoints`; live Chrome on scratch :8201 (throwaway client): 2,700 net, GST on 2,700.
- [x] **73.1.2 Reverse earned points on a return.** `ApplyReturnQC` / `ProcessRefundRequest` never touch `loyalty_point_ledger`. Reverse pro-rata on the returned line's original eligible value (from 73.1.1's snapshot) as an append-only Reversal entry - never an edit - applying D03's policy when the points are already spent. Needs D01, D03. [row: Return earn reversal P0] **Built 2026-10-17 (D01 + D03 decided by the user 2026-10-17: pro rata on the money returned; a balance may go negative and is paid back by later earnings, the cash refund is never cut).** `reverseEarnedPointsTx` (`engines/loyalty_returns.go`), in the refund's transaction under the customer's loyalty lock: cumulative target (the sale's earn x money refunded so far / the sale's money) less what earlier refunds reversed, as an append-only `Reversal` ledger row naming the refund. Tested: 8 then 16 of 24 points over two returns; spent-then-returned -> balance -9 with the full cash refund.
- [x] **73.1.3 Restore redeemed points on a return: points, not cash.** `ReverseLoyaltyRedemption` (`loyalty.go:174`) exists, but only tests call it. Refund the loyalty-funded share as points at the original allocation; restored points keep their original expiry or a configured minimum grace, so a same-day return never loses them (PDF §6 "Expiry after restoration"). Needs D02. [row: Return redeem restore P0] **Built 2026-10-17 (D02 decided by the user 2026-10-17).** `restoreRedeemedPointsTx`: the returned units' points share (from the allocation) goes back as an `Earn` row on the refund, expiring at the latest expiry the customer's points had at the sale, never sooner than new setting `loyalty.restored_points_grace_days` (30) from the return; GL credits 5250 for their value. Tested: 90 restored valid 30 days; a wholly returned bill leaves the balance where it started. Live: 90 restored to 2026-11-16.
- [x] **73.1.4 Redemption controls on the till path.** The till calls `RedeemLoyaltyPointsTx` (`pos_checkout.go:236`), which checks only the balance (`loyalty.go:67`); the OTP and velocity checks live only on `/crm/loyalty-redemption`. Move them into the shared redemption function - one choke point, so till, API and offline replay all enforce them - plus manager approval above a configured value, and reason codes. [row: POS redemption fraud P0] **Built 2026-10-17.** One choke point: `RedeemLoyaltyPointsTx` -> `authorizeLoyaltyRedemptionTx`, under the customer's lock: the daily limit, the one-time code (new setting `loyalty.redemption_requires_otp`, off by default) and the LoyaltyRedemptionRequest approval slab (500+ Store Manager as shipped), each spent by its one redemption (`consumed_at` on the challenge, `db/migrations_stage73_1_4_redemption_controls.sql`; the request -> Redeemed). The `/loyalty/redeem` API, the OTP flow and an approved API request all pass through it. Till: `POST /api/v1/pos/loyalty/redemption/start` + `/verify`, `GET .../status`; a till request is approved, not burned, by the approval hook - the sale burns it. Reason codes: new category *Loyalty Redemption*. Codes CUSTOM-0601/0602/0603. Tested `TestStage73RedemptionControls` (approval spent once, code bound to its cart and spent once, limit, API refused without authorisation); live: the till raised a request with a reason and named it.
- [x] **73.1.5 Loyalty in the server quote, at the configured point value.** The POS screen assumes 1 point = ₹1 (`view-pos.js:996, 1009`) and the quote leaves loyalty out (`pos_quote.go:191`). Add redeemable points, value per point, the cap and the resulting total / change due to `ResolvePOSQuote`, render the screen from it, and refuse a checkout whose points value differs from the quote. [row: Redemption value UI P0] **Built 2026-10-17.** `QuoteRequest.redeem_points`; `QuoteResult.loyalty` (balance, value per point, max points = min(balance, net / value), the capped points, their value, what the controls will ask for) and `amount_due`. The points are in the signed quote version, so a changed point value is a price change at checkout; checkout re-quotes with the points and stores the capped figure. The till renders from it - no more 1 point = Rs 1. Live: *1 point = 1 off ... up to 1000*, Total 2,700 -> 2,430.

**POS / sales (journey J1)**
- [x] **73.2.5 Hold and resume a bill.** Nothing exists. Park a cart server-side with its idempotency key, so resuming can never post twice; list parked bills per till. [row: Hold/resume bill P1] **Built 2026-10-17 (Batch A).** `engines/pos_held_bills.go`: `POSHeldBill` (id `HOLD-<cart number>`, engine-written only - `db/migrations_stage73_2_5_held_bills.sql`) keeps the cart number, store, cashier, customer, coupons, lines and a note. The cart number is the idempotency identity: the checkout claims it before anything posts, so a resumed bill posts under it once; a bill already in checkout or paid cannot be held. Resume is one conditional update Held -> Resumed (a second till is refused, POSOFF-0610); a resumed bill can be held again; discard needs a reason. `POST/GET /api/v1/pos/held-bills`, `POST .../{id}/resume|discard`; held bills listed per store. Till: More... -> Hold this bill / Resume a held bill (view-pos-extras.js; prices re-quoted, availability re-read; points, split and gift cards re-applied by the cashier). No stock reservation while held (stated in the guide). Tested `TestStage73HoldAndResumeBill`; live: 2 lines + customer held, till cleared, resumed under the same number, completed, nothing left held.
- [x] **73.2.6 Exchange at the till with net settlement.** Today only a same-value SKU swap through Returns QC. Return lines and new lines in one till transaction, net pay or refund through 73.1.6, tax and loyalty reversed and re-earned correctly. [row: Exchange P1] **Built 2026-10-17 (Batch A).** `engines/pos_exchange.go`: `StartTillExchange` runs the ordinary return (raise, approve, receive, QC at the counter; 73.1.1-73.1.3 tax reversal and points restore/reversal unchanged) and processes its refund with method *Exchange Credit*, which credits the money part to new **2620 Exchange Credit Clearing** and records an `ExchangeCredit` (Open). The new sale takes it as a tender after points and gift cards (`useExchangeCreditTx` in the sale's transaction: Dr 2620 / Cr primary clearing up to what is owed; any rest paid out from the till, Dr 2620 / Cr clearing, keyed to the credit; the credit -> Used, once, replay-safe). The allocation carries it as a tender, so a later return of the new items refunds it as money (cash). The new bill earns on everything it was paid with, so a like-for-like swap leaves the points as they were. Session expected cash counts a payout. `POST /api/v1/pos/exchange/start`; checkout `exchange_credit_id` (online only); `db/migrations_stage73_2_6_exchange_credit.sql`; codes POSOFF-0611/0612. Till: More... -> Exchange (bill number -> lines to take back with condition -> credit in the bar; *(N back to the customer)*; the finished bill says what to give back). Tested `TestStage73ExchangeAtTill` (like for like, cheaper, dearer, reuse refused, 2620 nets to zero); live: shirt 900 -> socks 450, 450 paid back, 2620 zero, drawer 1,000 + 900 - 450 = 1,450. **Open:** OMS (online-order) exchanges keep the existing Returns QC same-value swap.

Expected cash = float + cash tendered in sales (from the 73.1.1 allocation) - cash refunds processed while a session was open at the store the goods came back to (stamped `pos_session` on the refund) + paid in - paid out - safe drops (`SessionCashPosition`, `GET /api/v1/pos/session/summary`). Close above `pos.drawer_variance_tolerance`: reason required as before, then the session goes **Pending Approval** through the new POSSession approval rule (0+ Store Manager) - approved -> Closed, rejected -> Open for a recount (`finishPOSSessionApproval` hook). Shift handover: `POST /api/v1/pos/session/takeover` closes the open session against the next cashier's count and opens theirs with it as float, each naming the other. **Also fixed:** the till never asked for the variance reason, so a cashier whose count was over the tolerance could not close the session at all - the close flow (now in view-pos-extras.js) asks for it. `db/migrations_stage73_2_7_till_cash_movements.sql`; codes POSOFF-0607/0608. **OMS (journey J4)**
- [x] **73.2.13 Notifications with a real delivery state.** `NotificationLog` writes "Sent" fire-and-forget (`notifications.go:136`). Send through the outbox with attempts, retry and a final state from the provider - never "Sent" without evidence. [row: Notifications P1] *Done 2026-10-17 (batch C): the log row is written **Queued** in the same transaction as a `notification_delivery` job (async_jobs is the outbox's delivery stage; `integration_event_outbox` was not used because its payload is shown on Integration Logs and fanned out to webhook subscribers, and a body can hold an OTP). The job POSTs with `delivery_id`/`X-Notification-Delivery`, SSRF-checks the URL, retries network/408/429/5xx (**Retrying**), stops at **Failed** on the last attempt (Replay resends), never re-sends one the provider has (**Accepted**/**Delivered**), and takes the provider's own delivered/failed answer; a later receipt arrives on `POST /api/public/v1/notifications/{id}/delivery-status` (new scope `notifications:write`; NOTIFI-0461..0463).
